[[sep-dv-verification-plan]]
== SEP OCAH Open-Source Verification Plan

=== Overview

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Field |Value
|Design Under Test |`sep_wrapper` (`hw/top/sep_wrapper.sv` + `sep_ip_integration`)
|Bare core |`sep` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep.sv`)
|Repository |`tt-oca-harness`
|Project |OCAH
|Framework |PyUVM on cocotb (Phase 1 / Phase 2); SystemVerilog UVM on the same top (Phase 3, `--framework uvm`)
|Primary simulator |Verilator
|Architecture reference |`hw/sys/sep/doc/`
|Register export |`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/` (via `env/sep_reg_meta.py`)
|How to run |`README.md` in this DV tree (`tools/dv/run_dv.py --dut sep`)
|Cocotb tests |Every member of the `all` group in `testlists/all.toml`; its `expected_count` is the authoritative number
|SV-UVM realization |`sep_axi_smoke_test` (`--framework uvm`)
|===

This VPLAN is the technical contract for the open-source SEP DV
suite: what each phase covers, how tests are named, which VIPs
drive the DUT, and what each registered test must prove. It is
written for adopters and maintainers of the public tree. Testbench
structure (env hierarchy, HDL top, bring-up helpers) is in
link:SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc[SEP_TB_ARCH].

PASS/FAIL needs positive evidence in the simulation log (named
`CHK-*` lines or equivalent). A clean simulator exit alone is
not enough.

=== Verification Strategy

One DUT. Two environments across three phases.

[cols="1,2,3",options="header"]
|===
|Phase |Environment |Job

|Phase 1
|cocotb / PyUVM + Verilator
|Smoke plus subsystem representatives. Fewest tests that carry the most real datapath behaviour.

|Phase 2
|Same cocotb environment
|Iconic feature coverage by IP, plus cross-IP edges a per-IP bench cannot see. Includes block activation for instantiated IPs that otherwise had no vehicle. Randomize when there is a legal matrix (`RAND-REP` / `RANDCFG`); keep directed (`RAND-NONE`) when there is not.

|Phase 3
|SystemVerilog UVM realization of the same testbench top
|The same scenario names on the same testbench top; `sep_axi_smoke_test` is bound in both realizations (see that procedure). The UVM class library and run recipes are in link:SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc#_systemverilog_uvm_realization_framework_uvm[SEP_TB_ARCH]. Contracts this plan does not claim are listed in <<phase-3>>.
|===

[arabic]
. *Stimulus*: no-CPU tests drive the CPU-LSU AXI splice through
  `ocah_axi_vip` / `SepAxiAgent`. An optional second master drives the
  real SMN-inbound port (traverses the inbound filter). CPU tests boot
  VeeR EL2 firmware from ICCM. SPI flash uses `ocah_spi_vip`.
. *Checking*: frontdoor reads/writes, independent Python goldens where
  needed (AES/HMAC/KMAC/OTBN/entropy/LCC), and named checkers that can
  fail on a broken DUT. Prefer source-derived expected values from the
  register export and architecture docs.
. *Bring-up*: drive `ext_boot_seq_done_i`; use `+skip_fuse_sense` only
  when the test does not need real OTP sense; hold the CPU off for
  `no_cpu` runs.
. *Coverage*: iconic feature scorecard (<<iconic-scorecard>>) for the
  cocotb phases. VCS Phase 1 functional-coverage bins live in
  link:SEP_FCOV.adoc[SEP_FCOV] (`u_sep_fcov`). SystemVerilog covergroups
  are not compiled on the Verilator acceptance path. RTL code coverage
  is a separate, unthresholded number (`README.md`, Code coverage).
Randomization contract: the *same* config object programs the DUT and the
golden. Pin the datapath directed first, then upgrade that test to randomized —
do not ship a one-point stand-in for a matrix. Skip randomization only when the
feature has no legal knob space (a strap, a single named handshake).

[[audit]]
Checker contracts in this plan are written against these known ways a check can
pass while the DUT is unable to fail it.

*An expectation that shares a source with the thing it measures.* A golden must
not be selected by asking the engine which candidate it agrees with. The HMAC
software-key word order is pinned from the IP register specification:
`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/data/hmac.hjson:446-447` states
`key[1023:0] = {KEY0, KEY1, ... , KEY31}`, so `KEY_0` is the most-significant
word and the software path needs no reversal. The RTL agrees
(`hmac.sv:219-221` assigns the key CSRs into the secret-key vector in reverse
index order), and that agreement is corroboration rather than the source: a
golden transcribed from the RTL cannot disagree with the RTL. A two-candidate
scan is legitimate only as a failure diagnostic that separates a word-order
regression from a general digest fault.

*Golden compared against golden.* An assert between a result already pinned
bit-exact to its golden and another output of the same model holds with the
simulator switched off. The degenerate case compares list lengths rather than
domains — a four-word wrong-mode golden against an eight-word digest.

*A guard whose own constructor makes it unfalsifiable.* Non-vacuity must not
rest on a relation the configuration builder already guarantees: an unwritten
(zero-initialised) address required to differ from a pattern built as
`getrandbits(64) | 1`, or `start_lo != 0` where the config builds that value as
`(random & ~0xFFF) or 0x1000`.

*A stimulus with no fan-out.* Driving a pin that is unread in the configuration
under test and asserting that two observables did not move is a DV-invented
scenario, not a specification claim. `dbg_rstb_i` is routed only into
`sep_cpu`, so it has no observable in a CPU-stub run, and
`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` does not require isolation from system reset.

*A tolerance wider than the signal.* A post-event bound above the count the
window actually reaches passes when the event is ignored. The window must run
long enough that the pre-event count clears the tolerance by a wide margin.

*A check that reads back nothing.* Walking a register through several encodings
without reading it back cannot distinguish a register stuck at its reset value
when those encodings produce byte-identical output for the chosen stimulus.

*Entailed guards.* The absence of a complementary-arm marker, or a deferred
line, is not a checker; the owning test already fails if a forbidden marker
appears.

=== Naming Rules

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Rule |Requirement
|Test case name |`sep_<feature>_<scenario>_test` in lower snake case
|Single-scenario module |Module name matches the test case
|Plusarg-selected variant |Several testlist entries may share one module when each selects its scenario with a plusarg. The entry name then does NOT end the module name, so the entry must carry the plusarg in `args` and a comment naming the scenario. The seven in the tree are `sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test` (`efuse.sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`, `+secure_tm_lock`) and the six `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_*_test` (`lcc.sep_lcc_lc_state_transition_matrix_test`, `+lc_start=N`). Use it to avoid copying one implementation per scenario, not to hide a missing module
|Multi-scenario / RAND-REP |One leaf test walks every required discrete cell in one invocation; do not silently ship a one-point stand-in for a matrix
|Open-source files |PyUVM test under `cocotb/tests/`, stimulus in `cocotb/seq_lib/`, shared helpers on `sep_base_test` / `cocotb/env/`
|Testlist enrollment |Leaf entry in the owning `testlists/*.toml` and in `testlists/all.toml`
|Evidence |Every checker needs a positive log line naming the contract, as `CHK-<ID> PASS`
|Evidence gate |A clean exit is not a pass: every graded contract logs `CHK-<ID> PASS`, and `sep_base_test` fails a leaf that graded nothing of its own. The mechanism, its `EVIDENCE_SUMMARY` line, the `min_evidence` / `required_evidence` knobs and the exemption list are described in the evidence gate section of `README.md`, which owns them
|===

=== Open-Source BFM Policy

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Interface / Protocol |Open-Source Replacement
|CPU-LSU AXI (no_cpu) |`ocah_axi_vip` via `SepAxiAgent` on the LSU splice
|SMN-inbound AXI |Second `SepAxiAgent` on the real inbound port
|SPI flash |`ocah_spi_vip` `OcahSpiFlash`
|JTAG (eFuse mux path) |`ocah_axi_vip` AXI-Lite master on the OTP JTAG2AXIL port (`j_axi_*`)
|Entropy noise / goldens |TB noise port + pure-Python goldens in `cocotb/env/`
|eFuse / OTP |Generic OSS eFuse model (no foundry OTP)
|Memories |Wrapper macros + sim-time image load
|===

=== Scenario-Expanded Test Summary

==== Phase 1 — Smoke

[cols="2,2,2,4",options="header"]
|===
|Test Case |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|`sep_axi_smoke_test`
|no_cpu
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves the SEP register fabric decodes and responds on the CPU LSU bus with no CPU booted.
|`sep_hello_world_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|The minimum proof that the CPU boot path works end to end: firmware is compiled for the VeeR EL2 core, loaded into tightly-coupled memory, and the released core fetches, executes and prints over the firmware console.
|`sep_cpu_trace_diag_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Boots a known noinline call chain and one breakpoint trap, then cross-checks the processor-state monitor's reconstruction against the firmware-reported `mcause` / `mepc`.
|`sep_boot_rom_smoke_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Proves the CPU instruction fetch unit can fetch and retire instructions out of the boot ROM.
|`sep_sram_smoke_test`
|no_cpu
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves the SEP local fabric routes to the SRAM and that the SRAM stores.
|`sep_km_mem_smoke_test`
|no_cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|Proves the Key Manager's private instruction ROM and data SRAM are wired and alive.
|`sep_otbn_mem_smoke_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves the OTBN instruction and data memories are writable and readable over the CPU LSU frontdoor at the 32-bit access width OTBN's own program-load driver uses.
|`sep_efuse_sense_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves the eFuse sense sequence runs to completion at reset and that what landed in the shadow registers matches the programmed image word for word.
|`sep_spi_flash_jedec_smoke_test`
|no_cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip
|Proves the OpenTitan SPI host controller is configured, clocks a real command out, and receives a real reply.
|`sep_rom_sanity_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Proves the CPU fetch unit executes real code out of the boot ROM across the main RISC-V instruction formats.
|`sep_hmac_kmac_cpu_crypto_smoke_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Brings the HMAC, KMAC and AES engines up over the real CPU-to-fabric path, as a complement to the sideload tests which drive them from the Key Manager. The AES leg round-trips a block through SRAM in both directions, against the published FIPS-197 vector on the encrypt side.
|===

==== Phase 1 — Representatives

[cols="2,2,2,4",options="header"]
|===
|Test Case |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|`sep_address_map_test`
|no_cpu
|ocah_axi_vip
|Prove the CPU-LSU decodes `sep_cpu_ctrl` and one safe CSR in every LSU-reachable CSR block (including Adams Bridge and the entropy pool).
|`sep_efuse_image_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves the write-one-to-set programming path into one-time-programmable memory: bits that are burned read back as one directly from the array before any re-sense, and after a cold re-sense the shadow equals the original image plus exactly the bits that were burned.
|`sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test`
|cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|The widest single test in the suite: two independent processors contend for the eFuse block at the same time.
|`sep_dma_hash_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Drives the secure DMA engine from real firmware to copy a block from SRAM into the CPU's data tightly-coupled memory with the inline hash engine enabled, then proves two things independently: the copied data is identical to the source, and the hardware digest is correct. Walks both SHA-256, against a software digest over build-varying data, and SHA-384, against the published FIPS 180-4 vector for a fixed message. Also carries the same message as two chunks and re-runs it with the digest byte-swap off.
|`sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, CPU FW
|Proves the hardware handshake between the SPI host and the secure DMA engine.
|`sep_km_otbn_sideload_kat_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|Proves the full key-distribution chain from the real entropy datapath to a real cryptographic consumer: the OpenTitan entropy stack seeds the Key Manager, the Key Manager's firmware accepts a known 384-bit key over its mailbox and sideloads it to OTBN over the dedicated key bus, and an OTBN program running on the real core reconstructs the key from its two shares and writes it to data memory, where the host reads it back and compares against the known value.
|`sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Walks the device through four lifecycle states — one preloaded, three programmed for real into one-time-programmable memory — and proves each reaches the lifecycle controller and produces the expected feature-control decode.
|`sep_dma_cpu_contention_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|The only test where the CPU and the DMA engine are genuinely competing masters on the SRAM at the same instant.
|`sep_clock_uvm_wdt_rst_input_reset_path_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Drives the watchdog reset input pin and proves it takes the SEP CPU-reset net low and releases it again, without disturbing the main SEP reset.
|`sep_mailbox_plic_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Completes the interrupt path all the way to the CPU.
|`sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|The AES member of the sideload family.
|`sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|The HMAC member of the sideload family.
|`sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|The KMAC member of the sideload family.
|`sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`
|no_cpu
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves that interrupts owned by the entropy blocks, HMAC error, and DMA done/chunk/error reach the SEP interrupt aggregator on the correct bit, using each block's real interrupt-test register rather than a forced wire. Also latches the DMA register-path bus-error bit from both a read and a write, and the HMAC, KMAC and OTBN peripheral-bridge bits with a through-adapter SLVERR, watches aggregator bits 40 and 42, and clears each.
|`sep_periph_bus_err_misaligned_reveal_test`
|no_cpu
|ocah_axi_vip
|EXPECTED FAIL. Asserts the misaligned-offset latch condition `sep_cpu_ctrl.rdl` and `doc/interrupts.adoc` state for the bus-error status registers. The fabric refuses a misaligned in-extent beat with DECERR, so no TL-UL error reaches the block and no bit latches.
|`sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|One entropy source feeding two real consumers at once.
|`sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Proves the CPU-side address alias window remaps both data and instruction accesses to physical SRAM.
|`sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test`
|cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|The debug-port counterpart to the two-processor coexistence test. After sense-done both OTP JTAG2AXIL `dbg_disable` bits read 0.
|`sep_nmi_sanity_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Covers the non-maskable interrupt mechanism end to end: the vector register has the right default, accepts a new value, and refuses further writes once locked — and then a real watchdog bark delivers an actual non-maskable interrupt to that handler, which clears the condition and returns.
|`sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Two related mechanisms in one boot.
|`sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves that the lifecycle security state actually gates the external inbound port — the security-boundary property, not just the register that describes it.
|`sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Proves the entropy stack is alive end to end on the open-source build and that its output reaches a real consumer.
|===

==== Phase 2 — by area

===== Crypto and entropy

[cols="2,2,2,4",options="header"]
|===
|Test Case |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|`sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +esrc_noise_force
|ocah_axi_vip
|Walks the nine-cell product of {ECB, CBC, CTR} x {128, 192, 256} in one invocation, with key, IV and plaintext randomised per cell.
|`sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Walks 17 cells: keyed HMAC over every legal key length for each of SHA-256/384/512, plus plain SHA for each variant.
|`sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +esrc_noise_force
|ocah_axi_vip
|Thirteen cells across SHA3-224/256/384/512, SHAKE-128/256, cSHAKE-128/256 and KMAC-128/256 spanning all five key lengths.
|`sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Two crypto sinks draw EDN entropy concurrently and both complete correctly: each AES fork block matches its golden bit-exact and the KMAC digest matches its own, while both are shown to have taken real post-adapter EDN beats inside the contention window. An AES-alone baseline in which KMAC takes zero beats is the control that keeps the both-beats check from passing vacuously.
|`sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Holds AES and OTBN URND `edn_req` together, then grades that both clients are granted and that a prefix of post-adapter grants alternates. Same-cycle dual-request lockstep is not claimed. Then drives OTBN RND with a program blocked on the RND CSR and KMAC with a keyed digest, so all four adapter clients are live, value-checked consumers in one run, with the entropy pool scored alongside them as a fifth endpoint.
|`sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Phase 2 DRBG iconic feature: a CSRNG Generate command completes at the commanded length, `gen_last` asserts, and the trailing CTR_DRBG Update runs.
|`sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Proves the per-IP software reset domains are isolated, using live crypto results rather than a poked status bit: each engine computes a golden-checked value, then one engine is reset and the other's held result must survive bit-exact.
|`sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, +esrc_noise_force
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|The counterpart for the one reset shared by ESRC, CSRNG and EDN: the pool is filled from the internal chain, every consumer is held, and the reset must wait for all three converted AXI-Lite paths to isolate before it asserts. Proves the pool scrubs its stale and half-packed data, that the isolated paths answer SLVERR while the external source-select register stays outside the domain, and that recovery is fresh-only and ordered — consumers are released only after fresh pool progress.
|`sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ML-DSA-87 key generation through the ABR register aperture: identity and reset status, an ACVP keyGen known-answer public key, completion status, the PIC error and notification legs, `ZEROIZE`, and single-bit seed sensitivity.
|`sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ML-DSA-87 signing and verification through the same aperture: a deterministic external-mu signature compared word-for-word against the ACVP sigGen vector, and the sigVer pair that must verify and must not, whose results must differ.
|`sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|ML-KEM-1024 key generation, encapsulation and decapsulation through the ML-KEM register block: the two keys, the ciphertext and the shared key each compared against the published ACVP vector, with decapsulation walked as both the valid case and the modified-ciphertext case that FIPS-203 answers by implicit rejection. Also the Adams Bridge `SW_RESET_N` domain: a live encaps result survives a sibling engine's reset bit-exact, the host path returns SLVERR while the ABR bit is held, and the result does not survive ABR's own reset.
|`sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Every seed walks the high-bit mirrors of the three live registers (the offsets that catch a truncated decode), and refuses a write under all three AW/W arrival orders.
|`sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Loads a tiny RV32I program into OTBN IMEM, stages two seeded DMEM operands, issues EXECUTE, and checks `ERR_BITS==0` plus the DMEM result against an independent Python golden.
|`sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|Health-test watermark arming: each supported selector arms to `0x0000` high or `0xFFFF` low on `MODULE_ENABLE` 0->1, a selector write leaves `HT_WATERMARK` at the entry value, and an illegal selector resolves to `REPCNT_HI`. Scope: the ESRC → DRBG → EDN datapath row owns CHK1–CHK4 and stays where it is.
|`sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|The FIPS lock makes ESRC configuration write-once: a pre-lock write moves the field off reset, the same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`, write-0 cannot clear the lock, and `rst_ni` is the only way back, with the BIW observe enable still writable under the lock. The advisory RCT/APT cutoffs derived from `MIN_ENTROPY_H` are graded against an SP 800-90B oracle. Scope: health-test *quality* is not claimed.
|`sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden
|A short window plus stuck forced noise trips `ALERT_THRESHOLD`.
|===

===== Fabric and remap

[cols="2,2,2,4",options="header"]
|===
|Test Case |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|`sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`
|no_cpu, real PROD fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Brings the lifecycle controller up in PROD so the inbound filter is genuinely active (`sep_debug=0`, value-checked against the lifecycle golden), then walks entries 0 and 7, two address windows, and `{rw, read-only, write-only}` at `src_id=0` (match-all), plus entry 0 / window 0 × `{rw, r, w}` at `src_id=0x5` with a matching AXI `user[3:0]` and one src-mismatch cell — 16 cells in one invocation — then the same-page `allow_burst=1` granule, page-bound, and `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` on entry 15.
|`sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|R/W, 64-bit, RO, valid, and write-once-set across the alias/AP/STEE remap and inbound/outbound filter CSR banks, plus per-entry storage independence across all 64 filter and alias entries.
|`sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Live remap plus outbound-filter drop.
|`sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|`env/sep_reg_meta.py` exports `addr`, `reset`, `mask` (software-usable field bits) and `mask_all` (storage, reserved included) from the generated SystemRDL.
|`sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Negative control on intra-block dead space. Each block owns an address window and populates only part of it; an access past the allocated size owns no register and must be refused. Window ends come from `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`, which states the rule: the fabric refuses an address past the extent a unit allocates, and such an access never reaches a unit. Landing on a live register instead is a hard failure. A later beat of a burst that ends past the window's extent must be refused on the same terms as a single beat.
|`sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Scope is the sixteen programmable local-master regions ahead of the system-peripherals routing demux (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, `axi_alias_remap`).
|`sep_axi_order_sweep_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|AW and W are independent channels with no ordering requirement between them (IHI 0022 A3.3), so AW-first, W-first and same-cycle are all legal and each must deliver the write without displacing its neighbour. Each block terminates AXI at its own register adapter, so the contract has to hold at each of them: 318 cells over 27 blocks, crossed with 1/2/4-byte sizes on the scratch words. The ordering each cell presented is read off the AW/W valid assertions and asserted against what was requested.
|`sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|The same ordering walk driven from the SMN inbound master. m_axi reaches the same eleven IP blocks through the inbound filter, which denies by default, so the walk opens allow windows from the CPU-LSU side first. The sixteen `INBOUND_FILTER_CTRL` blocks are excluded and counted: those CSRs gate the access in flight, so writing them mid-walk would measure filter reprogramming rather than adapter ordering. Unlike the CPU-LSU splice, this path grants a W-first handshake.
|`sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|TB-instantiated `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter`
|All three legal AW/W/AR orderings at a `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` port. Write address, write data, and read address are independent channels (IHI 0022 A3.3). The adapter accepts each write half independently until that half is pending, and accepts a read only when neither write half is pending; both accesses must retire under every ordering. The DUT's own adapters cannot be presented every ordering from the fabric -- the crossbar delivers W one cycle after AW and re-serializes to that order whatever the master does -- so this leaf drives a TB-owned second instance of the same module at its port, with its own reset so cells are independent. A lone write and a lone read are required to retire first. Grades the MODULE; the two leaves below grade the SEP integration.

|`sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|The same arbitration contract on the DUT's own CSRNG lane adapter, reached as ordinary CSR traffic over the SEP fabric. AR is presented while a write is half committed. Calibration measures where each channel arrives at the adapter port, so the overlap is placed from measured latency rather than guessed. Overlap is confirmed at the adapter's own port, not at the TB master.

|`sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|The same contract on the second DUT lane adapter, the EDN control path. `drbg.sv` instantiates `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` twice, so a defect in one instance is not evidence about the other.

|`sep_axi_id_routing_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|AXI transaction IDs on the response path. The crossbars prepend the master index to the incoming `AxID` and the demux keeps one outstanding counter per ID, so the ID is address-like state and a master matches its responses by it. Eight scratch words hold eight distinct values and eight reads are held outstanding together, one per ID; each must return its own word, and the top ID must not alias a lower slot. The rest of the suite issues ID 0, so this is the leaf that presents the dimension.

|`sep_axi_strobe_window_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Narrow-write lane masking against the AXI strobe rule (IHI 0022 A3.4.3), plus write/readback of the OTBN IMEM and DMEM memory windows. The KMAC state window is not probed: it is gated by the engine's own configuration, so a probe there would test KMAC bring-up rather than the read mask. A window behind an SRAM-style adapter applies the mask on reads, unlike a register behind a register adapter, so a bridge driving that mask from the write strobe returns zeros with a correct response — only a data compare sees it.
|`sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Read-side counterpart of the strobe test: a read narrower than the 64-bit beat only obligates the responder to drive the addressed byte lanes (a 32-bit CSR leaves RDATA[63:32] undriven; a narrow-ARSIZE beat leaves every non-addressed lane undriven), so the value handed to the test must be a pure function of the addressed lanes. Every read is compared against an independent golden and repeated around a checked disturbing access, so a result that leaks undriven lanes or leftover channel state fails as a mismatch.
|`sep_axi_map_refuse_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Inter-block reserved space: reserved rows in the DV-owned table in `env/sep_axi_decode_map.py` allocate nothing, so an access there must not answer OKAY. The refusal expectation comes from that table. Named reserved spans whose refuse flavour is unnamed are excluded from the probe set. Walks the gaps BETWEEN block windows; `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` walks the dead tail INSIDE one.
|===

===== SPI and DMA

[cols="2,2,2,4",options="header"]
|===
|Test Case |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|`sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, CPU FW
|Drives SRAM through the secure DMA into the OpenTitan SPI host transmit FIFO and on to the flash BFM, then reads the flash back and compares against the cocotb-seeded random source data.

|`sep_dma_basic_test`
|cpu
|ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW
|Secure-DMA control and copy breadth over the CPU LSU: documented reset values, the configuration lock reading locked while the engine is observed busy, the range-valid gate and its rw0c lock each with a positive control, FIXED/INCR/WRAP against a per-mode golden, 1/2/4-byte widths, `STATUS.done` RW1C, one invalid-opcode error with recovery, the address, address-space-id and transfer-width refusals, the inverted-range refusal, a copy that leaves SRAM for the instruction memory and back, a DMA-issued host-path command-integrity inject (`host_path_err` / aggregator bit 41), and a fabric DECERR dest that raises the same bit with the pin low. The mode and width walk is directed.

===== Key Manager vault

[cols="2,2,2,4",options="header"]
|===
|Test Case |Run mode |VIP / Model |Description
|`sep_km_isolate_termination_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image
|What a Key Manager MASTER transaction receives when its own AXI-Lite path is isolated. Every other isolation entry drives the SEP host port and so reaches the `host_*` paths only; the KM masters the `km_*` paths, so a KM-CPU image is the vehicle. The same store to the HMAC key aperture is clean, then raises an AXI error while `km_hmac` is isolated, then is clean again.

|`sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|The key/policy vault refuses what it should: a store to a locked SRAM region is dropped with the readback unchanged, the matching `SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` bit sets, `IRQ_STATUS` raises AXI_SLVERR or AXI_DECERR, and the status bit clears W1C. The seal walk is here too: the same erase must retire a sealed slot and free an unsealed one.
|`sep_km_command_set_rand_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|Produce, move and destroy a key over the KM mailbox; reject illegal, over-long and mis-framed commands. AES is the consume witness. Version queries, OTP cold lock, recoverable-fault ACK, and a directed ABR ML-DSA seed transfer every seed. RANDCFG extra transfer dest (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN) writes one classic `cp_dest` cell per seed; daily reseed accumulates. The four sideload KATs remain the MUST-fill owners for HMAC/KMAC/AES/OTBN consume.
|`sep_km_wipe_reset_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|KM `SW_RESET_N` park/release returns `RESP_KM_READY` and a later `CMD_KEY_LOAD` succeeds. Then `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` posts `RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT`.
|`sep_km_handover_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|Handover mailbox contract: empty/reserved `CMD_SRAM_*` refusals, `CMD_EXEC_ROM` inhibit, then a 14-word `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` stream and a warm-reset `CMD_SRAM_EXEC`.
|`sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|KM `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` dest ABR ML-DSA seed, KV pull, KEYGEN public key equals a direct-seed KEYGEN of the same palindromic seed.
|`sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`
|no_cpu, real fuse sense
|ocah_axi_vip
|The three ML-KEM lanes of the KV facade: the 16-dword seed lane carrying D then Z, the message lane, and the shared-key writeback the KM consumes back out with `CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER`. Each lane is compared against a direct-register run of the same words, preceded by a run on different words. Also the confidentiality rule: the shared key stops reading back once an input came from the vault.
|`sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense, KM held
|ocah_axi_vip
|The SEP-side mailbox as a register surface: STATUS depth/full/empty, the two SEP-reachable stickies, both `SEP_CTRL` response modes, `IRQ_STATUS` / `IRQ_ENABLE` gating onto aggregator `[14]`, and flush. Not a command stream.
|`sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|CRC co-processor KAT, all three modes, operands from the run seed.
|`sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`
|no_cpu, +skip_fuse_sense
|ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden
|KPV scrambler: stored word and address mapping both transform while enabled, and the vault passes through while disabled.
|===

[[iconic-scorecard]]
==== Iconic Feature Scorecard

The architecture docs under `hw/sys/sep/doc/` define the feature set. An
*iconic* row is the mode, command, or contract that makes that IP itself.
Excluded from the count: IP that is not part of the open-source build,
peripherals outside bare SEP, and features with no vehicle in this suite (AGP,
local UART/GPIO, ROM patch, DMA-AES, DCLS-in-SEP, PCR vault, ROM-FW contracts).

Feature rows are independent of per-test checkers. A row names the tests whose
checkers prove that feature and states, in the row, any part of the feature
those tests leave unproven. A known RTL defect that the suite exercises is
called out in <<known-limitations>>, not as a feature credit.
The architecture spec under `hw/sys/sep/doc/` is the scorecard denominator: a
feature that spec does not define is not a row.
A feature an earlier phase already proves stays that phase's credit even if a
later test could hit it too — credit once, at the earliest phase that proved it.

[cols="3,6",options="header"]
|===
|Iconic feature |Owning test(s) / note

|Fabric reachability / address map
|`sep_axi_smoke_test`, `sep_address_map_test` (`sep_cpu_ctrl` + one CSR per LSU-reachable block, including ABR and entropy pool)

|Inbound filter (block-by-default, allow, `SEP_DBG`)
|`sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test`, `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`. Firmware must keep CFG outside allow windows; the hardware applies the rules it is given and is correct as built.

|Outbound filter / live remap
|`sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test` (live); CSR bank on `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`

|CPU alias window (LSU + IFU)
|`sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`

|Mailbox → PIC
|`sep_mailbox_plic_test`

|EL2 boot + ROM fetch / LSU
|`sep_hello_world_test`, `sep_cpu_trace_diag_test`, `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test`, `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test`, `sep_rom_sanity_test`

|PIC source map
|`sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`

|DMA copy + inline SHA-256 + LSIO
|`sep_dma_hash_test`, `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test`, `sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test`, `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test`

|DMA address modes × width
|`sep_dma_basic_test`

|DMA host-path command integrity
|`sep_dma_basic_test`. A DMA-issued command under `dma_host_intg_inject_i` raises exclusive `host_path_err` and aggregator bit 41; a DMA transfer whose destination sits past the DMA CSR window raises the same bit with the pin low (fabric DECERR). `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` returns both status bits and the aggregator bit to 0; a legal copy then matches the source.

|SRAM datapath
|`sep_sram_smoke_test`, `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test`

|AES mode × key-size
|`sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` (9-cell). CTR x DECRYPT is not claimed, and is not deferred either: CTR is a stream mode and the engine runs the forward cipher whichever way `OPERATION` is programmed, so the cell has no DUT-observable cipher contract and a test there would pass with the field disconnected. `OPERATION`'s shadowed-register behaviour is mode-independent and belongs on a CSR vehicle.

|HMAC SHA variants
|`sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`

|KMAC / SHA3 / SHAKE
|`sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`

|OTBN as compute
|`sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`

|KM sideload consume (AES/HMAC/KMAC/OTBN)
|`sep_km_*_sideload_kat_test`, `sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`. Fail-closed START without a valid key is proven on `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` CHK-GONE: after the engine's key is shredded it produces no output in a bounded window. Post-error recovery is not claimed.

|KM mailbox command set
|`sep_km_command_set_rand_test`. Generate, transfer, revoke and engine-shred on the real KM firmware, with AES as the consume witness; one seed-selected extra `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` dest (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN); a directed ABR ML-DSA seed transfer every seed; version queries; OTP cold lock; recoverable-fault ACK after a SEP flush; the three framing rejections with the values they carry; illegal-command and bad-length refusals; and a shredded engine refusing to start. One seed does not fill all four classic dest cells — daily reseed accumulates, and the four sideload KATs remain the MUST-fill owners. ABR consume is `sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`. The DRBG fault SOURCE is not claimed -- its organic conditions cannot be provoked from the SEP fabric and the KM posts an unsolicited fault rather than a command return code -- but the interrupt plumbing for that bit is graded on the vault vehicle through the architected `IRQ_SET` inject. The mailbox's own register surface is a separate entry, `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`.

|KM wipe and warm-reset
|`sep_km_wipe_reset_test`. `SW_RESET_N` park/release re-announces `RESP_KM_READY` and a key load succeeds. `KM_WIPE_CTRL` then posts `RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT`. KPV-zero is not claimed.

|KM handover
|`sep_km_handover_test`. Inhibit after `CMD_EXEC_ROM`, then a 14-word `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` and a warm-reset `CMD_SRAM_EXEC`.

|KM to ABR seed
|`sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`. Mailbox dest ABR ML-DSA seed plus KEYGEN public-key equality against a direct-seed KEYGEN. The command-set leaf hits the dest cell every seed; this leaf is the consume proof.

|KM mailbox protocol (FIFO status, over/underflow, flush)
|`sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`. Empty-FIFO read and inbound overrun, both `SEP_CTRL` response modes, STATUS depth/full/empty against a golden, `IRQ_STATUS` / `IRQ_ENABLE` gating onto aggregator `[14]`, and flush that empties both FIFOs without clearing the stickies. KM is held so firmware cannot drain or fault the FIFOs. The command-set vehicle stays the transport and is not re-credited here. KM-peer events (`flushed_by_km`, inbound underflow, outbound overflow, separators) stay on the KM IP mailbox vehicles.

|KM key/policy vault (extent + write-lock + seal)
|`sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`. The seal walk lives here rather than on the command set: no mailbox command seals a slot, so over the command set an erase always frees and the retire path has no vehicle there.

|ESRC → DRBG → EDN consume
|`sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`, pool sink coverage

|ESRC lock and alert delivery
|`sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`, `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`. HT quality is not claimed, nor is the health-test counter-redundancy countermeasure (`es_cntr_err` -> `MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR`), which no stimulus can reach and which needs fault injection.

|ESRC health-test watermark arming
|`sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`

|Crypto EDN arbitration (two live clients)
|`sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`, `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`

|eFuse sense + shadow + persist
|`sep_efuse_sense_test`, `sep_efuse_image_test`

|eFuse program × lock
|`sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`

|Lifecycle `feat_ctrl` vs `LC_STATE`
|`sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test`

|Lifecycle demote
|`sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test`

|RMA token match → LC update
|`sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test`

|LC_STATE next state (shadow write-1-to-set)
|`sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_demote_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_rma_sip_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_rma_chiplet_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_end_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_transient_test` — six leaves on one module, one sensed starting state each. The other writer of `LC_STATE`: the frontdoor shadow write, which the stitch walk (OTP program + re-sense) never exercises.

|SPI-OT flash command set
|`sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test` — JEDEC, WREN/RDSR, WRDI, page program, FAST_READ and sector erase against the flash BFM, with a neighbour-sector intact check, write-protect and status-register breadth, and the three host `ERROR_STATUS` classes with recovery. The BFM golden requires the exact observed opcode sequence, not a floor. `sep_spi_flash_jedec_smoke_test` is the reachability vehicle and keeps its own phase's credit. The DMA-to-SPI path is graded on the `DMA copy + inline SHA-256 + LSIO` row and is not re-credited here.

|WDT bark → NMI, pet, lock
|`sep_nmi_sanity_test`, `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test`, `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test`

|Per-IP `SW_RESET_N` isolation
|`sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`. Each engine holds a golden-checked result; a neighbour reset leaves it bit-exact. While HMAC is held, the same `DIGEST_0` read and a `CFG` write complete SLVERR, the read does not return the live digest, and AES stays OKAY. Drain-before-reset is claimed for the HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge domains — see the detail entry; it is not claimed for AES or OTBN. The ABR domain also carries the JTAG `IC_RESET` override check, and its live-result behaviour is graded on `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`. Drain-before-reset for the shared TRNG domain is on the `Coordinated TRNG reset and ordered recovery` row.

|Scratch bank reset domains (warm vs cold)
|`sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`. The graded contract is the reset-domain split across every implemented register: a warm reset clears all eight warm registers while all eight cold registers retain, and only a cold reset clears both banks. Distinct per-index patterns prove the registers do not alias. Read over the CPU-LSU AXI splice with a `scratch_cold_probe_o` cross-check; the cross-check catches a read-mux fault and is not an independent confirmation, since both sides come from the same flops. The bank *size* the specification asks for is not graded here — see <<known-limitations>>.

|Coordinated TRNG reset and ordered recovery
|`sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`. One `SW_RESET_N` bit resets ESRC, CSRNG and EDN together, and the reset waits for all three converted AXI-Lite paths to report isolated, so drain-before-reset is covered here rather than deferred. Also covers the pool scrub, SLVERR while isolated, the external source-select register staying outside the domain, and the JTAG hold/release pair. Intra-window decode of the adopter TRNG passthrough is not graded.

|Clock-gate CSR
|`sep_clock_gate_control_test` (storage / reachability). Live per-IP gate is not in this RTL.

|JTAG vs CPU vs KM eFuse mux
|`sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test`, `sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test`

|SEC_DIS override
|`sep_sec_dis_override_test`. `FEAT_CTRL` match / mismatch / restore after sense. `CHK-SENSE-GATED-RESET` grades that a match before sense-done leaves the reset held (release is a DTP TDR step).

|eFuse set-only (anti-rollback)
|`sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test`. Set-only OR-merge is walked on the six non-`LOCK` set-only rows and on both `LOCK` RDL windows (`LOCKS` word 0, `LOCKS` word 1, `LOCKS_SPARE`) after those other rows. SW-writable rows, `REQUIRED_SIGNERS` included, overwrite and then a write of 0 clears.

|eFuse per-spare write-lock slot numbering
|`sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`. Every run walks `SPARE0..SPARE7`: each spare is programmed while unlocked, then its own `LOCKS_SPARE` bit is set and a second bit in the same spare is rejected. The spares are walked in order after each preceding lock, so a lock bit wired past its own slot fails the next spare's unlocked-program cell. Slot coverage does not depend on the seed; only the bit offsets within a spare do.

|Token comparator fail-closed at the lifecycle gate
|`sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_test` (`CHK-GATE-SIP-FAULT`). A redundancy fault forces the reserved error code onto the token status, and the `LC_STATE` bit[1] advance must not land — the gate compares for equality with the match code, so a gate written as "not a mismatch" would let the fault code through. The cell that follows presents the same token with the fault removed and does advance, so the pair is a contrast rather than a lone negative.

|Token comparator fault latch cleared by reset
|`sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test` (`CHK-FAULT-RESET`). The set-only phases prove the fault bits survive a valid retry; only this one separates that from a latch that never clears. After the two zeros it re-arms once, so the phase ends on a zero-to-one transition rather than on a wedged detector that stays at 0.

|Debug-disable gating ladder (`dbg_disable`)
|`sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test` (`CHK-DBG-DISABLE`, `CHK-FUSE-DFT-DIS`). All eleven `dbg_disable` bits match the DTP gating ladder for the same `FEAT_CTRL` the cell just verified, across the whole demote matrix. `dft_secure` is Case 3 and `dfd` is Case 1. The DFT-inserted fuse-path ports are separate DUT outputs, not members of `dbg_disable`: `sep_fuse_dft_disable` is Case 3 AND `sep_fuse_dbg`, inverted; `smc_fuse_dft_disable` is Case 2 AND `smc_fuse_dbg`, inverted. The compose cell closes those two bits while Case 2 and Case 3 stay open.

|Local-master alias remap datapath
|`sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`

|CSR reset / RW / RO / reserved
|`sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`

|AXI AW/W channel ordering, write landing and neighbour displacement
|`sep_axi_order_sweep_test` (s_axi, 318 cells over 27 blocks, 106 per ordering) and `sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test` (m_axi, 222 over 11, 74 per ordering), all three orderings presented and asserted by `CHK-ORDER-STIM`. `m_axi` grants a true W-first handshake; `s_axi` holds `wready` until AW, so its W-first presentation completes same-cycle at the slave, which is the slave's contract and not a stimulus failure.

|AXI narrow-write strobe and memory windows
|`sep_axi_strobe_window_test`.

|AXI narrow-read lane extraction and repeatability
|`sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`: all 36 byte-run slices, all 14 aligned narrow-ARSIZE cells and the CSR extremes exact and repeatable around a checked disturb. `CHK-RANDCFG` holds the walked counts against those floors.

|Reserved inter-block address space
|`sep_axi_map_refuse_test`.

|Dead-space decode
|`sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` — single-beat refuse, no-alias, and per-beat refusal inside a burst. `CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT` reads each beat's `RRESP` off the bus and fails any beat past an extent that answers OKAY. It also requires at least one window that serves its in-extent beats and refuses only the tail, which is what shows the refusal is per beat rather than per burst. The counts a given run reaches are reported, not graded. Read bursts only — one `BRESP` covers a write burst, so per-beat write refusal is not observable at the protocol level; see <<known-limitations>>.

|Adams Bridge ML-DSA-87 keyGen
|`sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`. The key-vault seed path is not claimed. ML-KEM is a separate vehicle, `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`.

|Adams Bridge ML-DSA-87 sigGen / sigVer
|`sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`. Deterministic external-mu signing against the ACVP vector, and verification walked as the accepting and rejecting pair. `KEYGEN_SIGN` and the key-vault seed path are not claimed.

|Adams Bridge ML-KEM-1024
|`sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`. keyGen, encaps and decaps against the ACVP vectors, with the implicit-rejection case graded on its published shared key. The same leaf grades the Adams Bridge `SW_RESET_N` domain on a live result — see `Per-IP SW_RESET_N isolation`. `KEYGEN+DECAPS` and the key-vault seed path are not claimed.

|Entropy pool aperture + PIC sources
|`sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`. Drain-under-fill is not claimed; stall duration is graded by `CHK-STALL-DURATION`.

|KM CRC engine (PCPI)
|`sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`. All three modes against an independent golden, plus a hardware-only word-versus-byte chaining cross-check that holds if the golden is wrong.

|KM KPV scrambler
|`sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`. Data transform, address tweak and keying, all frontdoor: the vault passes through while disabled, so the raw stored words are readable without a hierarchical peek.

|Mailbox outbox FIFO, threshold interrupt and error flags
|`sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test`. The SEP-host side of the outbound aperture: native 64-bit pushes at one FIFO entry per beat, STATUS compared field-by-field against a golden occupancy model after every push, the write-threshold interrupt gated by its enable and level-held across write-one-to-clear, read-from-empty and write-to-full each answered with their documented response and error flag, control flush, and the write-only decode of the push register. `Mailbox -> PIC` grades delivery on `sep_mailbox_plic_test` and is not re-credited here. The spec's two-word read of an incoming 64-bit message, and the read half of the threshold pair, need the peer side and are graded by <<sep_mailbox_peer_rx_rirqt_test>>.

|Interrupt aggregator packing under simultaneous fan-in
|`sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`. Several cross-IP sources asserted at once must produce exactly their own bits in `sep_internal_interrupts[8:33]`, with every bit of that region resolved rather than floating. `PIC source map` grades the source-to-PIC-id map and CPU delivery on `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`; this row is the aggregation itself — the place a one-hot map can smear onto a neighbour — and is a distinct contract from delivery.

|SPI host control plane (CSR, interrupt, error status, watermark, enable)
|`sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`. Documented resets, per-register write/readback with unimplemented bits proven to hold no storage, the interrupt-test path setting and releasing interrupt status with no write-one-to-clear (whether the enable masks the status register itself is logged, not graded, because the register description does not state it), five of the six `ERROR_STATUS` conditions each induced by its own trigger and exclusive of the others (ACCESSINVAL is not claimed: its documented trigger is a write with no bytes enabled, which this AXI master cannot drive), the transmit watermark polarity at the exact threshold, and the enable gate against a calibrated negative window. `SPI-OT flash command set` grades the flash datapath and `CSR reset / RW / RO / reserved` is the generic bit-bash vehicle; neither covers this control plane.

|SECURE_TM shadow-write lock
|`sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test`. Each of `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE`, `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` takes the same seeded payload twice: with the strap low the write lands and the readback is the OR-merge, and after the strap is raised and the image re-sensed the stored value does not move, `sep_internal_interrupts[33]` pulses and the response stays OKAY. A `SECURE_TM_UNLOCK` spare still takes a write with the strap up, so the refusal is the lock rather than a dead write path. Real fuse sense at `LC_PROD`.

|eFuse locked-field access violation interrupt
|`sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`. A write to a write-locked spare and a read of a read-locked spare each raise `sep_internal_interrupts[33]` (PIC source 34) for the duration of the access, answer OKAY rather than an error response, leave the stored value unchanged, and drop the line after the access retires, with an unlocked spare in the same OTP image as the negative control. Real fuse sense at `LC_PROD`.

|Token comparator redundancy fault delivery to the CPU
|`sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test`. EL2 claims PIC source 40, reads the sticky `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` bit, and masks `meie[40]`; because the fault bits are software-read-only and clear only on reset, the check requires the source to be still requesting across the quiet window, which is what makes the mask the reason for the quiet. `RMA token match -> LC update` grades the comparator matrix on `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test` and is not re-credited here.
|===

=== Test Procedures

Unless a procedure says otherwise, each test starts from the common
SEP bring-up for its run mode (`bring_up_no_cpu`,
`bring_up_and_wait_fuse_sense`, or `boot_firmware`), drives stimulus
through the agents above, and ends with zero UVM/scoreboard errors
plus the checkers listed here.

Each procedure card carries the seven labeled sections: *Source Module*,
*BFM / Model*, *Objective*, *Test Coverage*, *Test Procedure*, *Checkers*,
and *Pass Criteria*. *Test Coverage* is one line — the cells or path walked.
*Pass Criteria* is that every named checker PASSes and `UVM_ERROR` is 0.
The checker table is the fail-able contract.

==== Phase 1 procedures

[[sep_axi_smoke_test]]
===== `sep_axi_smoke_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_smoke_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the SEP register fabric decodes and responds on the CPU LSU bus with no CPU booted.

*Test Coverage*: LSU-splice reset-value decode of `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` plus masked write/readback of four CSRs.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode; the cocotb AXI driver owns the CPU LSU splice.
. Read `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` and require its reset value.
. For each of four read/write registers, write a distinct pattern and read it back, masking the comparison to the implemented field bits.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DECODE
|The reset-value read returns the specified non-zero reset value, proving real decode rather than an unmapped-address zero.
|CHK-WR-RB
|Each read/write register returns exactly the pattern written, masked to its implemented bits. Note the `SEP_NMI_VEC` pattern already has bit 0 clear, so the reserved-bit mask is a no-op here and reserved-bit behaviour is not exercised by this or any other test.
|CHK-RESP
|Every read beat returns `OKAY` and no transaction decode-errors. The cited tally covers read beats; write responses are enforced by the scoreboard line under CHK-NONVAC, which fails any non-OKAY item.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The scoreboard performed a non-zero number of value comparisons.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

The same scenario is bound in the SV-UVM realization (`--framework uvm`). That
pass keeps these checkers on the CPU-LSU splice over 16 seeded runs, requires
the always-on scoreboard `cpu_ctrl_csr` feature, and
`+SEP_CSR_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE` must FAIL the scoreboard. Structure and run
recipes: link:SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc#_systemverilog_uvm_realization_framework_uvm[SEP_TB_ARCH].

'''''

[[sep_address_map_test]]
===== `sep_address_map_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_address_map_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Prove the CPU-LSU decodes `sep_cpu_ctrl` and one safe CSR in every LSU-reachable CSR block (including Adams Bridge and the entropy pool), and that the unpopulated hole inside `sep_cpu_ctrl` reads OKAY with zero and is not a live alias.

*Test Coverage*: One safe CSR in every LSU-reachable CSR block, plus a `sep_cpu_ctrl` sweep and the interior hole.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with the LSU splice owning the bus.
. Read every `sep_cpu_ctrl` reset-check register and require the exported reset value.
. Response-check hardware-driven registers; read the low half of `REFERENCE_COUNTER` twice and require it to advance (the reference clock is driven, so the counter is live and no reset value applies).
. Write/readback/restore the SEP base/size CSRs and the pure-RW storage registers (masked).
. Write the implemented `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` bits, read them back, then restore reset after the fabric walk.
. Walk one readable CSR per LSU-reachable block (DMA, WDT, scratch, reset, OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, CSRNG, EDN, ESRC, ABR NAME, entropy-pool STATUS, lifecycle, KM mailbox, eFuse shadow, AXI mailbox, inbound filter CFG, alias remap, outbound remap, SPI) and require OKAY (value-check where reset is known).
. Read three words in the hole after `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` and before `SEP_SW_DEBUG` (including `0x10A3_0170`). Require OKAY with zero data, which `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` states as the contract for an offset inside a unit's extent that owns no register. Prove `SEP_SW_DEBUG` writable, then require a hole write to leave it unchanged.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-REFCNT-READ
|The reference counter is LIVE and readable: the low half is read twice and must advance. It counts on `clk_ref_i`, which the testbench drives, so no pinned value can be its expectation -- and the advance is a stronger statement than a reset compare, because a neighbouring register's storage, a stuck all-ones and a zero return all hold still between the two reads. The high half is read for the pair but is not value-checked.
|CHK-RW-READBACK
|Every pure read/write register returns exactly what was written, masked to its implemented bits, and is restored afterwards.
|CHK-BASEADDR-RW
|The base and size registers accept a write, read back, and restore cleanly.
|CHK-FABRIC-WALK
|Every LSU-reachable CSR block in the walk returns OKAY on its chosen register; value-checked entries match the exported or seq-derived expectation. The logged count must be non-zero.
|CHK-EXT-DEMUX-BOUND
|The first word of the eFuse-shim window answers something other than DECERR, so the demux selected the shim port, and the word past the window answers DECERR from the extension error slave. Both addresses come from the generated header by symbol. The value behind the shim is adopter-owned and is not graded — only which port the decode selected.
|CHK-CPU-CTRL-HOLE
|Each `sep_cpu_ctrl` word in the hole after `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` and before `SEP_SW_DEBUG` reads OKAY with zero data, and a write to `0x10A3_0170` does not change `SEP_SW_DEBUG` -- whose writability a control write proves first, since `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` is `sw = r` and no AXI write can move it. `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` states both halves: a unit accepts such an offset with zero/OKAY, and non-aliasing follows from exact-match register decode rather than from refusal.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_hello_world_test]]
===== `sep_hello_world_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_hello_world_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: The minimum proof that the CPU boot path works end to end: firmware is compiled for the VeeR EL2 core, loaded into tightly-coupled memory, and the released core fetches, executes and prints over the firmware console.

*Test Coverage*: TCM load, CPU release, console banner and PASS magic.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build the firmware image and stage it into instruction and data tightly-coupled memory.
. Release the CPU in `cpu` mode.
. Wait for the console banner.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-BOOT
|The core boots from tightly-coupled memory and reaches the firmware entry point, proven by console output that only executed code can produce.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_cpu_trace_diag_test]]
===== `sep_cpu_trace_diag_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_trace_diag_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Prove the processor-state monitor reconstructs a known call chain and one breakpoint trap against architectural ground truth. `sep_hello_world_test` only needs the monitor for "PC advanced"; this leaf is the audit that the trap record, symbolization, and shadow stack are the events the core actually took.

*Test Coverage*: Known noinline call chain plus one `ebreak`; monitor trap, symbol, and stack depth vs CSR `mepc`/`mcause`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build `fw/tests/cpu_trace_diag_test` and boot it from ICCM (`rst_vec` = ICCM base).
. Firmware prints the banner, installs `mtvec`, and runs the noinline chain `main` → `diag_leaf1` → `diag_leaf2` → `diag_leaf3`.
. The innermost frame takes one uncompressed `ebreak`. The handler records `mcause` / `mepc`, advances `mepc` by 4, and `mret`s. Firmware prints those CSRs and returns PASS only when the chain result is 7 and exactly one breakpoint trap was taken.
. Cross-check the monitor against that console ground truth.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TRAP-EVENT
|The monitor captured exactly one exception retirement, cause = breakpoint (3), and it is not an interrupt.
|CHK-TRAP-SYM
|The firmware-printed `mepc` symbolizes into `diag_leaf3`. Ground truth is the CSR the handler read, not the trace port's retirement PC.
|CHK-CHAIN-SYM
|Retired PCs cover `main`, `diag_leaf1`, `diag_leaf2`, and `diag_leaf3`.
|CHK-DEPTH
|The shadow call stack reached depth ≥ 4 while the trap frame was live (the three linking calls plus the trap frame).
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_boot_rom_smoke_test]]
===== `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Proves the CPU instruction fetch unit can fetch and retire instructions out of the boot ROM.

*Test Coverage*: IFU fetch and retirement from the boot ROM.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a four-instruction program into the boot ROM at a known address.
. Release the CPU and direct fetch at that address.
. Confirm each instruction address retired.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ROM-EXEC
|All four boot-ROM fetch addresses are reached and retired on the core's retired-instruction trace. Instruction *content* is not compared here — a ROM returning different but sequentially-executable words at the same four addresses would pass; content is covered by `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_sram_smoke_test]]
===== `sep_sram_smoke_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_sram_smoke_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the SEP local fabric routes to the SRAM and that the SRAM stores.

*Test Coverage*: Fabric route to SRAM; store and readback.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode.
. Read the target location and require zero.
. Write a 64-bit pattern; read it back.
. Write a 32-bit pattern to the upper half; read the full 64 bits back.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RESET-READ
|A read of the target location before any write returns zero with an `OKAY` response — the decode and liveness anchor for the write/readback that follows. This is not an SRAM reset property: the macro has no array reset, and the zero comes from simulator memory initialisation, so this leg would not reproduce on silicon.
|CHK-STORE
|A 64-bit write stores and reads back exactly.
|CHK-LANE
|A genuine 4-byte beat (`AxSIZE=2`) to the upper half changes only those lanes; the lower four survive. This is the only narrow-`AxSIZE` access in the suite — every other test on this bus drives full-width beats.
|CHK-RESP
|Every read beat returns `OKAY` and no access decodes to `DECERR`. The cited tally covers read beats only — write responses are counted but their code is inspected for `DECERR` alone, so an `SLVERR` on a write would leave that line unchanged. Write responses are enforced separately, by the scoreboard line quoted under CHK-NONVAC.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_km_mem_smoke_test]]
===== `sep_km_mem_smoke_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_mem_smoke_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: Proves the Key Manager's private instruction ROM and data SRAM are wired and alive.

*Test Coverage*: Key Manager instruction ROM and data SRAM reachability.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode and stage the small Key Manager ROM image.
. Release the Key Manager from software reset.
. Poll until both a ROM fetch and an SRAM write have been observed, or time out.
. Read the traffic counters and the first SRAM word.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ROM-FETCH
|The Key Manager processor fetches from its ROM — a non-zero request count, so a processor held in reset fails.
|CHK-SRAM-WRITE
|The processor writes to its SRAM, and the stored first word is the value the ROM program was built to write.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_otbn_mem_smoke_test]]
===== `sep_otbn_mem_smoke_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`target = "lsu_stub_all_live"`, `+skip_fuse_sense`)

*Source Module*: `sep_otbn_mem_smoke_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the OTBN instruction and data memories are writable and readable over the CPU LSU frontdoor at the 32-bit access width OTBN's own program-load driver uses.

*Test Coverage*: OTBN IMEM/DMEM 32-bit LSU write and read.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode.
. Read OTBN `STATUS` and require it to be neither `LOCKED` nor a memory-wipe state.
. Write a known word to the OTBN instruction memory base; read it back.
. Write a known word to the OTBN data memory base; read it back.
. Read the instruction and data memory request and write counters.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-IMEM
|The instruction-memory word reads back exactly as written.
|CHK-DMEM
|The data-memory word reads back exactly as written.
|CHK-ARRIVED
|Both memories saw exactly the issued accesses — 2 SRAM requests each, of which 1 is a write — proven by the counters rather than by the readback alone.
|CHK-WRESP
|Both write responses are `OKAY`.
|CHK-PRECOND
|OTBN is neither `LOCKED` nor executing when the memories are accessed, so a bus access to them is legal. This is an exclusion, not an exact-state check: any other `STATUS` value is acceptable. Bus access is diverted and flagged illegal only while OTBN executes.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: OTBN sits in `StatusBusySecWipeInt` (`0x04`) for the whole of this
test — leaving that state needs EDN entropy to reseed URND, and the `no_cpu`
smoke configuration never brings EDN up. The internal wipe does not touch
IMEM/DMEM, which is why every access completes OKAY. `CHK-PRECOND` excludes only
the states that would invalidate the access (`LOCKED`, and `BusyExecute`, in which
the core owns these memories and a bus request is diverted to a dummy response
and flagged illegal); it does not pin `STATUS`, and it does not detect a change
from `BusySecWipeInt` to `IDLE` or `BusyExecute`.

*Design context, not a claim*: accesses drive `AxSIZE=2` (4-byte beats). These
memories are 32-bit SECDED words and reject the 64-bit default beat with SLVERR;
the 32-bit width is what OTBN's own program-load driver issues. No enrolled test
covers that rejection.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_sense_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_sense_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_sense_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the eFuse sense sequence runs to completion at reset and that what landed in the shadow registers matches the programmed image word for word.

*Test Coverage*: Reset sense-done; shadow words match the programmed image.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage an eFuse image and bring up with real fuse sense — no skip plusarg.
. Wait for sense-done.
. Compare all shadow words against the staged image.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SENSE-DONE
|The sense sequence completes rather than hanging.
|CHK-IMAGE-MATCH
|Every sensed shadow word equals the staged image, and the comparison is required to span the whole image — the word count is asserted, so an empty field table could not report "0 words, 0 error(s)" and pass. The compare is a passive hierarchical read of the shadow-register output — read-only observation, no write, force or deposit — rather than the software-visible register path. That path is proven separately by `sep_efuse_image_test`, which reads the same registers over the bus.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_image_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_image_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_image_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the write-one-to-set programming path into one-time-programmable memory: bits that are burned read back as one directly from the array before any re-sense, and after a cold re-sense the shadow equals the original image plus exactly the bits that were burned.

*Test Coverage*: OTP write-one-to-set program, array readback, and post-resense shadow.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage an image, release reset, and AXI-read `FEAT_CTRL` while `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` is still low.
. Wait for sense-done; AXI-read `FEAT_CTRL` equals the image golden and is not 0.
. Burn a set of bits through the write-one-to-set path.
. Read the burned bits directly out of the array before re-sensing.
. Program a second bit into an already-programmed word.
. Cold reset, re-sense, and compare the shadow against image-plus-burned-bits.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PRE-SENSE-FAIL-CLOSED
|AXI `FEAT_CTRL` is 0 while `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` is still low. The read must complete before sense-done, or the closed side was not seen.
|CHK-PRE-SENSE-OPEN
|After sense-done the same `FEAT_CTRL` equals the image golden and is not 0, so the guard actually opened.
|CHK-OTP-DIRECT
|The first and last burned bits read back as one directly from the array, before any re-sense, and their whole words match the golden. The remaining burned bits are covered only after the re-sense, by CHK-W1S-PERSIST.
|CHK-W1S-PERSIST
|After cold re-sense the shadow equals the original image plus exactly the burned bits — all twelve, the ten random ones and the two from step 5.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The comparison covered the whole image, not a sample.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Model honesty*: the OTP array behind the fuse-command interface is a
simulation-only behavioural bank, not an OTP macro. Two properties this entry
reads as proven are properties of that model rather than of hardware:
write-one-to-set additivity is OR-only by construction in the generated model, so
the "bank that overwrites instead of ORs" failure mode that `CHK-W1S-NOCLOBBER`
names cannot be represented; and persistence across cold reset comes from the
model having no reset on its storage. What the checkers do prove against real
logic is that each program reached its addressed bit through the real programming
path, and that the sense FSM re-reads the array after reset and the shadow tracks
it.

*Not claimed*: the `final_sec_disable` half of
`fuse_sense_done_i || final_sec_disable` — exposing the shadow before
sense-done — is not exercised here. Only the `fuse_sense_done_i` half of that
guard is proven.

'''''

[[sep_spi_flash_jedec_smoke_test]]
===== `sep_spi_flash_jedec_smoke_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_flash_jedec_smoke_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the OpenTitan SPI host controller is configured, clocks a real command out, and receives a real reply.

*Test Coverage*: SPI host clocks a JEDEC READ ID against the flash BFM.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode.
. Configure the SPI host: clock divider, chip-select timing, enable.
. Issue the JEDEC identify command through the command and transmit registers.
. Read the received identifier out of the receive register.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-JEDEC-ID
|The received identifier equals the flash model's device identifier.
|CHK-RESP
|Every read beat returns `OKAY` and no access decodes to `DECERR`. The cited tally covers read beats only — write responses are counted but their code is inspected for `DECERR` alone, so an `SLVERR` on a write would leave that line unchanged. Write responses are enforced separately, by the scoreboard line quoted under CHK-NONVAC.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The scoreboard performed a non-zero number of checks.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_km_axil_cpu_mux_coexist_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: The widest single test in the suite: two independent processors contend for the eFuse block at the same time.

*Test Coverage*: CPU and Key Manager contend on the eFuse AXI-Lite mux.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a fuse image and wait for sense-done.
. Release the Key Manager from software reset.
. Handshake over the mailbox — receive the ready token, send the go token.
. Loop exactly 512 times: read the chiplet identifier, sample the Key Manager's register payload and ownership tags, publish a counter to cold scratch.
. Stop on the done token.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HANDSHAKE
|Both processors are alive and synchronised over the mailbox.
|CHK-UID-STABLE
|The chiplet identifier reads the same known value on every iteration; no bad reads.
|CHK-TAG-INTACT
|The Key Manager's ownership tags keep their expected values throughout.
|CHK-PROGRESS
|The Key Manager's register payload advances and never moves backwards, so the contention window was real rather than an idle loop.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: this entry is double-gated and the two gates are independent. The
Key Manager firmware counts its own errors and converts them into the pass/fail
magic the boot scoreboard asserts on; separately the host reads the six values
the firmware published to cold scratch and asserts each one in its own statement,
so a fake pass would have to survive both verdicts. Progress is held to a floor
rather than merely non-zero: the host requires the Key Manager's payload to have
advanced at least 256 times across the 512 host iterations, so a multiplexer that
starved it to a handful of writes fails.

'''''

[[sep_dma_hash_test]]
===== `sep_dma_hash_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_dma_hash_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Drives the secure DMA engine from real firmware to copy a block from SRAM into the CPU's data tightly-coupled memory with the inline hash engine enabled, then proves two things independently: the copied data is identical to the source, and the hardware digest is correct. Completion is delivered as an interrupt to a registered service routine, so the interrupt path is proven as well as the datapath. The digest comparison is the strong part: copy correctness alone would not show the inline hash engine consumed the same bytes that were copied. Two digest lengths are walked. SHA-256 runs over build-varying data (the firmware never seeds `rand()`, so the byte vector is fixed within one build and changes when the firmware is rebuilt) against a software SHA-256 computed in firmware over the same bytes. SHA-384 has no 64-bit SHA-2 core in this firmware, so it runs over a fixed message against the published FIPS 180-4 digest for that message; `OpcSha384` is a supported DMA opcode alongside `OpcSha256`.

*Test Coverage*: Firmware DMA SRAM to DCCM with inline SHA-256 and SHA-384, digest compare, copy, and IRQ.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Initialise the fabric filter; register the DMA done and error service routines.
. Program the enabled memory range and confirm the configuration lock is open.
. Fill the SRAM source region with random data.
. Program source, destination, size, transfer width and increment modes.
. Start the transfer with the SHA-256 opcode and digest-swap set.
. Wait on interrupt; the service routine clears status.
. Read the hardware digest; compute the software digest; compare. Compare source against destination word for word.
. Then stage the fixed FIPS 180-4 56-byte test message in the same SRAM region, reprogram only the length and the opcode, and start a second transfer with the SHA-384 opcode.
. Wait on the interrupt again; read the twelve digest words; render them as hex and compare against the published vector. Compare the copied message against the source bytes.
. Disable the DMA interrupt enables, then carry the same 56-byte message as two 28-byte chunks, pacing the chunks on `STATUS.chunk_done` and counting them. Compare the digest against the published SHA-256 vector for the whole message.
. Finally run the same single-chunk SHA-256 twice, once with `DIGEST_SWAP` set and once clear, and compare the two digest word arrays against each other.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CFG
|The DMA configuration lock is open before programming. A wrong value is counted as an error: the firmware emits `ERROR: CFG_REGWEN not unlocked`, increments its error count, and that count gates the mailbox PASS magic.
|CHK-COMPLETE
|The transfer completes and the interrupt reaches the service routine.
|CHK-DIGEST
|The hardware digest equals an independent software SHA-256 over the same data. The eight hardware digest words are read through the array-indexed accessor `SHA2_DIGEST_0_BASE_ADDR(i)`, so a wrong array stride or a silently renamed register shifts the assembled digest and the `memcmp` below fails.
|CHK-COPY
|Destination matches source word for word.
|CHK-NONVAC
|For the SHA-256 pass: the software digest is recomputed from the actual SRAM contents on every run and no SHA-256 digest literal exists anywhere in the firmware or the testbench, so the comparison cannot be satisfied by a constant. `rand()` is never seeded, so the byte vector is the same for every seed within one firmware build -- the seed randomises only the clock period -- but it is NOT stable across builds, which is why no SHA-256 digest literal is pinned anywhere in this plan.
|CHK-SHA384
|The twelve hardware digest words, rendered big-endian as hex, equal the FIPS 180-4 digest for the 56-byte message the firmware staged. Unlike the SHA-256 pass this compares against a literal, so its non-vacuity argument is different and is stated rather than inherited: the message is the published input and the vector is the published output, neither is read back from the engine, and a DUT that returned a constant, a zero digest, or the SHA-256 of the same message fails. The 56-byte message is used in place of the one-block "abc" vector because the DMA transfers whole 4-byte words. `OPCODE_ERROR` on this transfer is reported as a failure, not skipped, so a build that does not implement `OpcSha384` fails here rather than passing quietly.
|CHK-SHA384-COPY
|The same SHA-384 transfer also copied the message to the destination, checked byte for byte. A digest engine fed correctly while the copy path is broken fails here.
|CHK-MULTICHUNK
