*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force +km_rom_hex=km_rom_entropy.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Phase 2 DRBG iconic feature: a CSRNG Generate command completes at the commanded length, `gen_last` asserts, and the trailing CTR_DRBG Update runs.

*Test Coverage*: CSRNG Generate at the commanded length, `gen_last`, and the trailing Update.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped.
. Release the Key Manager with the small entropy-consuming ROM image.
. Program the boot Generate and the auto Generate to `glen=4` from the same config object that feeds the golden.
. Bring up the entropy stack, drain the source queue through the front door, and wait for generated bits and a Key Manager consume.
. Value-compare the word the KM firmware stored to KM SRAM against the word the DUT delivered on the AXIS endpoint.
. Require at least one completed Generate of exactly four blocks, then report the strict CHK1..CHK5 scoreboard.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SEGMENTATION
|At least one Generate command completed, and every completed command carried exactly the commanded `glen=4` blocks — so `gen_last` was observed and a segment of any other length would fail. *The trailing CTR_DRBG Update is not observed*: it fires after the last block of a command, and this vehicle completes exactly one command, so no golden-compared block lands on its far side. Measured at 80,000 and at 240,000 poll cycles with an identical result, so the limit is EDN issuing no second Generate, not the window.
|CHK1..CHK4
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at each stage, in strict mode, with `report()` called. Bit-exactness *across* a trailing Update boundary is not claimed — see CHK-SEGMENTATION for why this vehicle cannot reach one.
|CHK5_km
|The Key Manager consumes an entropy word on the post-mux AXIS endpoint and it matches golden bit-exactly — the same consumer close as the end-to-end smoke. It says nothing about Key Manager *memory*; `CHK5_km_sram` is the row that covers the store.
|CHK5_km_sram
|The word the Key Manager firmware stored to KM SRAM equals the word the DUT delivered on the AXIS endpoint. `km_rom_entropy.S` reads one DRBG-sampler DATA word and stores exactly that word to KM SRAM base + 0, so a wrong-word store, a dropped store, or a store to the wrong offset fails here — where a non-zero-only liveness poll would pass. The stored value is a genbits word and so differs per run; only the invariant is claimed.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: `km_rom_entropy.S` reads one DRBG-sampler DATA word and stores
exactly that word to KM SRAM base + 0, so `CHK5_km_sram` compares that one
delivered word against KM SRAM word 0; it does not claim a sequence of stores. A
second AUTO Generate after the Update is not claimed — the Key Manager ROM reads
one word and parks, which is enough for the legality check to execute.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test]]
===== `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the per-IP software reset domains are isolated, using live crypto results rather than a poked status bit: each engine computes a golden-checked value, then one engine is reset and the other's held result must survive bit-exact. The HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge domains also grade drain-before-reset. ABR grades the JTAG `IC_RESET` override on this leaf; its live-result isolation is on <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>.

*Test Coverage*: Per-IP software reset; neighbour crypto result survives bit-exact; HMAC, KMAC and ABR drain-before-reset; ABR JTAG override.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Compute a golden-checked result on two engines.
. Reset one engine; the other's held result must survive bit-exact. Both directions.
. Hold HMAC in software reset and prove the same `DIGEST_0` read and a `CFG` write complete SLVERR, while the sibling AES port stays OKAY.
. Hold the KMAC reset and prove the gated reset waits for both its host and Key Manager paths to isolate, that in-flight host reads resolve, and that a read arriving inside the window resolves.
. Hold the Adams Bridge reset and prove the gated reset waits for both the full-AXI host path and the shared Key Manager path to isolate, that in-flight host reads resolve, and that the JTAG override holds the domain while `SW_RESET_N` still shows it released.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|Both held results are golden-matched and survive a *second* independent read, so the survives/cleared checks below are observations rather than restatements of one sample.
|CHK-SELF-RESET
|The AES reset pulse perturbs its own output. This is an inequality, not a pinned value — the masking-presented `DATA_OUT` after reset is a secure-wipe PRNG pattern rather than a fixed constant. The compensating evidence is that the entry re-derives the exact golden ciphertext afterwards, so the domain provably reset *and* recovered.
|CHK-NEIGHBOR-SURVIVES
|The HMAC digest is bit-exact intact across the AES reset. The single most valuable check in the entry.
|CHK-REVERSE
|The reverse direction: the HMAC reset clears its own digest to the exact reset value, and the AES output is bit-exact intact.
|CHK-KMAC-SELF
|KMAC `SW_RESET_N` restores `STATUS` to its register-map reset and STATE (share0^share1) reads 0, while HMAC DIGEST stays bit-exact.
|CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ORDER
|The Adams Bridge reset does not assert until both paths its domain depends on
report isolated: the full-AXI SEP host path and the shared Key Manager path. The
two are sampled every cycle from the reset request, so the order is observed at
the assert edge rather than read off a settled state. It also grades the Key
Manager path's ABR term -- a design that raised that bit only for a Key
Manager reset never releases the sequencer and fails here.
|CHK-ABR-HOST-DRAIN
|Host reads accepted before the ABR reset request resolve OKAY or SLVERR, never
DECERR and never a hang, and at least one drains OKAY. The number still
unretired at the cycle the isolate closes is asserted non-zero, so the leg
grades traffic the isolate had to drain rather than beats that had already
completed.
|CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ARRIVAL
|A read issued WHILE the ABR isolate is draining resolves rather than hanging.
It may drain or terminate; the graded property is that the path is not left
wedged.
|CHK-ABR-JTAG-OVERRIDE
|The JTAG `IC_RESET` override holds the ABR domain while `SW_RESET_N`, read back
inside the window, still reports it released -- so the hold is attributable to
JTAG rather than to software -- and the domain returns when the override
releases. Both mux directions are graded.
|CHK-KMAC-NEIGHBOR
|HMAC `SW_RESET_N` leaves KMAC STATE bit-exact intact.
|CHK-OTBN
|OTBN `LOAD_CHECKSUM` (rst_ni CSR) survives an AES reset and clears to its register-map reset on OTBN's own pulse. DMEM holds the staged mark across the AES neighbour pulse (cross-domain leak). OTBN's own reset runs a secure wipe, so DMEM retention across that pulse is not claimed.
|CHK-ISOLATE-PRE
|HMAC `DIGEST_0` answers OKAY with the live golden digest before the isolate request, so the later SLVERR is at a live mapped address.
|CHK-ISOLATE-LANDED
|`SW_RESET_N` readback shows the HMAC bit held, so a refusal cannot be a missed write.
|CHK-ISOLATE-SLVERR
|During the window a read of the same `DIGEST_0` completes SLVERR, not OKAY, DECERR, or hang, and the beat does not carry the live digest. `periphs.adoc` names SLVERR for a transaction presented while isolated; it does not name the error data.
|CHK-ISOLATE-WR
|During the window a write to HMAC `CFG` completes SLVERR. The write target is the RW `CFG` register, not the RO digest.
|CHK-ISOLATE-SIBLING
|AES `DATA_OUT_0` answers OKAY with the held ciphertext in the same HMAC window (port-scoped, not a global bus kill).
|CHK-DRAIN-ORDER
|The HMAC gated reset does not assert until both AXI-Lite paths that domain depends on — the SEP host path and the Key Manager path — report isolated. Read from the two named TB probe bits, sampled every cycle so the assert edge cannot be missed.
|CHK-HOST-DRAIN
|Host reads the fabric accepted before the reset request resolve OKAY or SLVERR — never DECERR, never a hang — and at least one resolves OKAY, so accepted traffic provably drained rather than being dropped.
|CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL
|A read issued *inside an observed drain window* also resolves. The beat is issued on the first cycle a TB probe shows an isolate bit asserted while the gated reset is still released, and the probe values at issue are recorded in the log, so the beat's position in the window is evidence rather than a timing assumption. It may drain (OKAY) or be terminated (SLVERR); it may not hang. A domain that asserts its gated reset with no such cycle at all fails this checker rather than re-scoring `CHK-HOST-DRAIN`'s property.
|CHK-ISOLATE-REOPEN
|After release the same `DIGEST_0` answers OKAY again. This is also what shows the drain did not leave the path wedged.
|CHK-ISOLATE-RESET-DEFAULT
|After release `DIGEST_0` reads its reset value 0.
|CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ORDER
|The KMAC gated reset does not assert until both the host and Key Manager AXI-Lite paths report isolated, sampled in the same cycle as the observed assert edge. Guarded by a check that the reset is still released when the request is issued, so a domain that asserted with no drain window fails rather than scoring a steady state.
|CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ARRIVAL
|A read issued on the first cycle a probe shows the KMAC drain window open also resolves OKAY or SLVERR. Fails explicitly if no window was ever observed, so the property cannot be satisfied by re-scoring the pre-request batch.
|CHK-TRNG-NEIGHBORS
|Parking the shared TRNG reset holds the TRNG `SW_RESET_N` bit and leaves the five accelerator bits released (OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, ABR). HMAC DIGEST and AES DATA_OUT stay unchanged in that window. KMAC STATE, OTBN `LOAD_CHECKSUM` and the ABR datapath are not sampled here.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: live-result isolation walks AES, HMAC, KMAC and OTBN. Drain-before-reset and the JTAG override walk Adams Bridge on this leaf; ABR live-result isolation is on <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>. The Key Manager bit stays at its reset default (a different mechanism) and is not claimed.

*Drain-before-reset*: claimed for the HMAC domain by `CHK-DRAIN-ORDER`,
`CHK-HOST-DRAIN` and `CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL`, for the Adams Bridge domain by
`CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ORDER`, `CHK-ABR-HOST-DRAIN` and `CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ARRIVAL`,
and for the KMAC domain by `CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ORDER` and `CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ARRIVAL`.
The KMAC leg does not claim accepted-traffic drain: pre-request reads retire
before the host path isolates on that domain, so none remain in flight at
isolate close and there is no accepted traffic for the isolate to complete. Adams Bridge does carry that property, on a full-AXI isolate. HMAC
drain runs against a live golden-checked result on a path this entry isolates.
ABR drain counts outstanding host reads at isolate close so it grades traffic
the isolate has to retire. The shared TRNG domain grades drain-before-reset in
<<sep_trng_reset_recovery_test>>.

*Not claimed*: drain-before-reset for the AES and OTBN domains — the
mechanism is shared, but a per-domain demonstration is not made here. Key
Manager isolate termination is not claimed here: `CHK-DRAIN-ORDER` reads the
`km_hmac` isolate bit, so it shows the KM path reaching isolation, not what a
KM transaction receives. That response is graded for `km_hmac` in
<<sep_km_isolate_termination_test>>, which runs a KM CPU image so the
transaction comes from the master that owns the path. SMC CPU-MMIO isolate is
out of this subsystem.

'''''

[[sep_trng_reset_recovery_test]]
===== `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Proves the one `SW_RESET_N` bit that resets ESRC, CSRNG and EDN
together behaves as a single coordinated domain: the reset waits for all three
AXI ports to isolate, the entropy pool scrubs whatever it held, the external
source path stays outside the domain, and recovery admits only fresh entropy.

*Test Coverage*: Shared TRNG `SW_RESET_N` drain-before-reset and ordered recovery.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the internal chain up and fill the fabric entropy pool through
  ESRC → CSRNG → EDN, recording the pool level and that `FIPS_LOCK` is set.
. Write a source-select value that is not the RDL reset and confirm the write
  took. That non-reset value is the pre-reset baseline the later check must
  still see.
. Hold every entropy consumer (KM, OTBN, AES, KMAC), then request the TRNG reset
  while concurrent reads are in flight on all three internal CSR ports.
. Through the reset, observe the isolate probe, the fate of the in-flight
  accesses, and the pool's packer depth.
. With the domain in reset, read the three internal ports and the
  source-select register; then exercise the JTAG reset pair to hold and
  release the same coordinated reset.
. Reinitialize ESRC, CSRNG and EDN with consumers still held, require a fresh
  seed and fresh pool progress, and only then restore the consumer resets.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TRNG-FILL
|The initial DRBG produces genbits and `FIPS_LOCK` reads set, so the pool level the later checks compare against is real entropy rather than a reset default.
|CHK-TRNG-ISOLATE-ALL
|While the reset is requested the isolate probe reads all three ports isolated. This is the drain-before-reset evidence: the reset does not assert until ESRC, CSRNG and EDN have all reported isolated.
|CHK-TRNG-DRAIN
|Every in-flight pre-reset access completes either OKAY or SLVERR — never DECERR and never a hang — and at least one completes OKAY, so accepted traffic provably drained rather than being dropped.
|CHK-TRNG-PACKER
|The entropy pool's packer depth is zero, so a half-packed word does not survive the reset.
|CHK-TRNG-SLVERR
|All three internal CSR ports answer SLVERR while isolated.
|CHK-TRNG-EXTERNAL
|After the packer is parked the test writes a source-select value that is not the RDL reset of `sel[2:0]` (`0x7` in `sep_cpu_ctrl.rdl`), confirms the write took, then asserts the same value after the internal reset. A match on the reset value would be vacuous. The `0x1091_7000` passthrough is an adopter aperture; intra-window decode is not graded.
|CHK-TRNG-JTAG
|The JTAG override holds the coordinated reset asserted and releases it again, the same pair used for the other domains.
|CHK-TRNG-CONSUMERS-HELD
|The `SW_RESET_N` readback shows the four consumers still held and the TRNG bit released, so the recovery ordering is observed rather than assumed.
|CHK-TRNG-CSR-RESET
|After the reset `FIPS_LOCK` is clear, the CSRNG and EDN interrupt enables return to zero from the non-zero masks written before the reset, and reserved ESRC `CTRL[0]` reads zero before a write of 1 and still reads zero after — RAZ and WI, not write-ignore alone. The interrupt enables are driven off reset first, so this is a 1->0 traversal rather than a read of two registers that were never written.
|CHK-TRNG-STALE
|A pop from the empty pool answers SLVERR with zero data, so pre-reset entropy cannot be read back. A DECERR here would mean the aperture had fallen into the reset domain, which is the opposite of what this proves, so the response is pinned rather than tested as any non-OKAY.
|CHK-TRNG-FRESH
|ESRC produces a fresh seed, `FIPS_LOCK` sets again, and a pool read succeeds, all before the consumer resets are restored to their saved value.
|CHK-TRNG-RECOVERY
|Closing summary line over the eleven rows above: the pool went full, emptied and refilled, the stale read was rejected, fresh data was delivered, and the consumer resets were restored to their saved value. It carries no contract of its own.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Why the pool is checked from outside the domain*: the entropy pool's AXI
responder is not in the TRNG reset domain, which is what makes the scrub
observable at all — the port stays live while its contents are invalidated.

*The RTL clear and isolate assertions are VCS-only*: the six
`ClearDropsRequest_A` / `ClearScrubs*` / `ClearResetsStall_A` /
`NoEntropyAcceptedDuringClear_A` / `ClearScrubsPendingRead_A` assertions in
`sep_entropy_fifo.sv`, the two `AxisEdnNo*DuringClear_A` in
`drbg_axis_edn_adapter.sv`, and `ResetOnlyWhenIsolated_A` /
`IsolateHeldThroughReset_A` in `sep_isolate_rst_seq.sv` all expand to nothing
under Verilator, because `prim_assert.sv` and `ocah_assert.svh` both gate on
`ifndef VERILATOR`. Since a kept Verilator PASS is the sign-off gate, those ten
contribute no evidence on it. CHK-TRNG-ISOLATE-ALL and CHK-TRNG-PACKER are the
Verilator-live stand-ins for the drain-order and packer-scrub contracts, which
is why they are asserted from Python probes rather than left to the SVA.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test]]
===== `sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the ML-DSA-87 key-generation path through the ABR register aperture: identity and reset status, an ACVP keyGen known-answer public key, completion status, the PIC error and notification legs, `ZEROIZE`, and single-bit seed sensitivity. Randomization is RANDCFG — the masking entropy words, and the seed word and bit flipped in the sensitivity leg, come from the run seed (`SepAbrKeygenCfg`).

*Test Coverage*: ML-DSA-87 keyGen known-answer, PIC legs, `ZEROIZE`, and seed sensitivity.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The aperture is a `sep_crypto` demux rule at `ABR_REG_MAP_BASE_ADDR` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_pkg.sv`), reachable from the CPU-LSU master the way the OTBN tests already reach their block.
. It is *not* behind an `axi_lite_isolate` unit: unlike the ESRC, CSRNG and EDN legs, the external TRNG passthrough in `hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_axi_interconnect.sv` carries none.
. Accesses are 32-bit beats (`AxSIZE=2`) because this path has no width converter.
. The Key-Manager-side key CSR is the generated PeakRDL `abr_wrapper_key_reg`. The ABR register block behind this aperture is real RTL.
. This leaf writes the seed window over the bus. The key-vault seed path is not claimed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NAME
|Identity registers read `MLDSA-87`.
|CHK-RESET-STATUS
|Reset STATUS is READY, not VALID, not ERROR.
|CHK-NIST-PK
|648-word public key matches the vendored ACVP keyGen vector.
|CHK-STATUS-VALID
|Completion STATUS is VALID with ERROR=0.
|CHK-PIC-ERROR
|Aggregate `[34]` traverses `0 -> 1` via `error_intr_trig`, then `W1C` readback 0, before the KAT. A stuck-low probe fails this row; the later `[34]=0` at completion is then a live observation.
|CHK-PIC-NOTIF
|Aggregate `[35]` high and `[34]` low at completion.
|CHK-PIC-NOTIF-W1C
|`notif_internal_sts` W1C clears `[35]`.
|CHK-ZEROIZE
|`ZEROIZE` drops VALID and every word of the pubkey window then reads zero. `MLDSA_STATUS.READY` is low through the `ABR_ZEROIZE` walk (`abr_ready = (abr_prog_cntr == ABR_RESET)`), so a READY-high wait would hang rather than pass vacuously. Scope: the pubkey read port is gated on `mldsa_valid_reg`, so a zero window proves the key is no longer readable, *not* that the RAM was wiped. The memory walk is not claimed; proving it needs a probe on the pubkey RAM or on `zeroize_mem_done`.
|CHK-SENSITIVITY
|Flipping one seed bit yields a different public key, and that key is not an all-zero window — an inequality against the vector alone would be satisfied by a cleared window.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the public key is a cryptographic function of the seed words
written over the bus. The sensitivity leg flips one seed bit after the NIST
match, so a cached or constant engine passes the first leg and fails the second.
`[34]` is driven high through `error_intr_trig` and cleared before the KAT, so a
stuck-low aggregator cannot satisfy both `CHK-PIC-ERROR` and `CHK-PIC-NOTIF`.

*Not claimed*: the key-vault seed path — a green keyGen does not close it, and
this leaf writes the seed window over the bus. ML-KEM-1024 is equally not closed
by a green keyGen, and is owned by <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>; sign and verify by
<<sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test>>.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test]]
===== `sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the two ML-DSA-87 consume commands the keyGen leaf does not
reach. `MLDSA_CTRL.CTRL` encodes SIGNING (0x2) and VERIFYING (0x3) beside KEYGEN
(0x1), and this leaf owns both, together with the signature and verify-result
windows they drive. Randomization is RANDCFG — the masking entropy words come from the run
seed (`SepAbrKeygenCfg`); the vectors are fixed.

*Test Coverage*: ML-DSA-87 sigGen known-answer, sigVer accept and reject, and
`ZEROIZE` between commands.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The aperture is the same `sep_crypto` demux rule at `ABR_REG_MAP_BASE_ADDR`
(`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_pkg.sv`) the keyGen leaf uses, with the same 32-bit
beats and no `axi_lite_isolate` in front of it.
. Both vectors are ACVP external-mu groups, so `MLDSA_CTRL.EXTERNAL_MU` is set
and mu is written to `MLDSA_EXTERNAL_MU` rather than a raw message being hashed
on the way in.
. Signing is the deterministic variant, so the signature is a fixed function of
the private key and mu and is compared word-for-word. A randomised signature
could only be checked by verifying it, which is the weaker claim.
. `MLDSA_STATUS.VALID` is sticky and READY does not re-assert until a `ZEROIZE`,
so every command is separated by one.
. The private key and public key are written over the bus. The key-vault seed
path is not claimed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NAME
|Identity registers read `MLDSA-87`. A dead aperture would read zero and every
window compare below would be against a silent bus.
|CHK-SIGN
|The 1157-word `MLDSA_SIGNATURE` window matches the vendored ACVP sigGen vector
word-for-word, with STATUS VALID and ERROR=0, and PIC `[34]` low.
|CHK-VERIFY-ACCEPT
|The ACVP case that must verify completes VALID and returns a non-zero
`MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` — zero is what an engine that never ran would report.
|CHK-VERIFY-REJECT
|The ACVP case that must not verify returns a `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` different from
the accepting case. The claim is that the two are distinguished, not an absolute
encoding: the register description does not state one, and transcribing a value
would be checking the document rather than the engine.
|CHK-ZEROIZE
|`ZEROIZE` between commands drops VALID and the signature window then reads
zero. Same scope limit as the keyGen leaf: the read port is gated on the valid
register, so a zero window proves the signature is no longer readable, *not*
that the RAM was wiped.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the rejecting sigVer case is the load-bearing half. An engine
that reported success unconditionally, or a result register that was never
updated per command, passes the accepting case alone and fails
`CHK-VERIFY-REJECT`. On the signing side, deterministic external-mu signing
makes the signature a fixed function of inputs written over the bus, so a
cached, constant or non-running engine cannot match 1157 words.

*Not claimed*: `KEYGEN_SIGN` (0x4), the key-vault seed path, and
`CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` consume after a live ABR capture. The absolute encoding of
`MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` is not pinned, only that accept and reject differ. ML-KEM is a
separate vehicle, <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test]]
===== `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the ML-KEM-1024 commands. ML-KEM is a second register block
inside the Adams Bridge aperture, with its own `MLKEM_CTRL` and `MLKEM_STATUS`
beside the ML-DSA pair, and this leaf owns it: the encapsulation key,
decapsulation key, ciphertext and shared-key windows are driven and read here.
Not randomized -- every input is a published vector.

*Test Coverage*: ML-KEM-1024 keyGen, encapsulation, and decapsulation known
answers, decapsulation walked as both the valid and the implicit-rejection case,
`ZEROIZE` between commands, and the Adams Bridge per-IP `SW_RESET_N` domain
graded on a live encaps result.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The aperture is the same `sep_crypto` demux rule at `ABR_REG_MAP_BASE_ADDR`
(`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_pkg.sv`) the ML-DSA leaves use. The ML-KEM windows
sit higher in the same 64 KB region; offsets resolve from the vendor
`abr_reg.rdl` by symbol.
. `MLKEM_CTRL` is writable only while `MLKEM_STATUS.READY` is set, so each
command waits for READY first. A write to a busy engine is dropped with no
error, and the previous `VALID` would otherwise be read as this command's
completion.
. keyGen takes the two seeds d and z over the bus and produces both keys.
. Encapsulation takes the message randomness m over the bus as well as the
encapsulation key, which is what makes the ciphertext a fixed function of
published inputs rather than a value only a round trip could check.
. Decapsulation is run twice, with the ACVP case that decapsulates normally and
the case whose ciphertext was modified. Under FIPS-203 implicit rejection the
modified ciphertext produces a different shared key rather than an error, and
ACVP publishes that key.
. `MLKEM_STATUS.VALID` is sticky, so every command is separated by a `ZEROIZE`.
. A final encapsulation re-establishes a live, vector-matched shared key. A
sibling engine (HMAC) is then pulsed through `SW_RESET_N`, and afterwards the
ABR bit itself. The pulse uses `SW_RESET_N.abr_sw_rst_n`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KEM-NAME
|The ML-KEM identity registers read `KEM-1024`. Every window compare below reads
back over this aperture, so a dead decode would turn all of them into compares
against zero.
|CHK-KEM-KEYGEN
|For the ACVP seeds, the 392-word encapsulation key and the 792-word
decapsulation key both match the published vector word-for-word.
|CHK-KEM-ENCAPS
|For that encapsulation key and the published message randomness, the 392-word
ciphertext and the shared key both match the vector. Because the randomness is
supplied over the bus this is an absolute value, not a self-consistency check.
|CHK-KEM-DECAPS
|The valid ACVP decapsulation case recovers the published shared key.
|CHK-KEM-DECAPS-REJECT
|The modified-ciphertext case returns the published *implicit-rejection* key --
a different value, still exact. An engine that reported an error, returned
zeros, skipped implicit rejection, or reused the previous shared key fails here
while passing the accepting case.
|CHK-ABR-NEIGHBOR-SURVIVES
|A live encaps result -- `STATUS.VALID` set and the shared key bit-exact against
the ACVP vector -- is intact after a sibling HMAC `SW_RESET_N` pulse. The
pre-pulse state is asserted first, so a run in which no result was live fails
rather than passing on an empty compare.
|CHK-ABR-ISOLATE
|The `MLKEM_STATUS` address that just returned OKAY with a live result returns
SLVERR while the ABR `SW_RESET_N` bit is held. This is the full-AXI host isolate;
every other accelerator host path is AXI-Lite.
|CHK-ABR-SELF-RESET
|The live result does not survive ABR's own reset: after release the engine
returns READY with `VALID` clear. `VALID` is hardware-driven, so a reset that
never reached the datapath leaves it set. The shared-key window is read-gated on
`VALID`, so its post-reset zeros are *not* offered as evidence of a wipe.
|CHK-KEM-ZEROIZE
|`ZEROIZE` between commands drops `VALID` and the shared-key window then reads
zero. Same scope limit as the ML-DSA leaves: the read port is gated on the valid
register, so a zero window proves the key is no longer readable, *not* that the
RAM was wiped.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the ML-KEM read ports return zero before a command completes, so
a compare against zero would pass on an engine that never ran -- every expected
value here is non-zero, and the vector loader refuses an all-zero expectation at
import. The rejecting decapsulation case is the load-bearing one: implicit
rejection has no error to observe, so the only thing that can fail is the value.

*Not claimed*: `KEYGEN+DECAPS` (0x4), the key-vault seed and message read lanes,
the shared-key write lane to the key vault, and ML-DSA.

'''''

[[sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test]]
===== `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — extra accepted pops come from the run seed (`SepEntropyPoolCfg`). The alias set and all three unique-dead offsets are walked every seed, so the decode coverage is seed-independent.

*Objective*: The fabric entropy pool answers only its three live registers. Occupancy, the live `pool_low` flag and fill-stall status reach software and the interrupt aggregator, a pop drains one word, an empty pop is refused, and every write and every unmapped offset inside the 64 KB window is refused with zero data.

*Test Coverage*: Three live pool registers; pop, empty-refuse, unmapped refuse, and the write refusal under all three AW/W arrival orders.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Every seed walks the high-bit mirrors of the three live registers, plus all three unique-dead offsets, so a truncated decode cannot hide behind a seeded pick.
. The block is a 64 KB crossbar target at `entropy_fifo.main` (`0x1095_0000`).
. The write refusal is driven three ways: AW and W in the same cycle (the backend default), AW leading, and W leading, the latter two by arming an `AxiTimingProfile` on the master.
  Fill waits on the live `pool_low` flag, not on an occupancy watermark.
  `StallThresh` is the wait bound for the fill-stall flag.
. It fills from the native EDN endpoint after the shared entropy bring-up.


*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-POOL-LOW-HIGH
|Aggregate `[36]` high on empty (level 0).
|CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-LOW
|`0x08=0x1` at empty (`pool_low`), not a copy of status `0x40`.
|CHK-POOL-FILL
|`pool_low` clears after fill and `fifo_level` is greater than 0. `pool_err` reads 0. `interrupts.adoc` source 39 says the pointer-integrity check is not built in the current configuration, so that source is tied to zero; the zero is not a fault-free claim and no pool integrity fault is claimed.
|CHK-POOL-LOW-LOW
|Aggregate `[36]` low after fill.
|CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-IDLE
|`0x08` traverses `0x1 -> 0x0` after fill, not a copy of status.
|CHK-STALL-DURATION
|Holding the same stall three times past `StallThresh` leaves `[37]` asserted and the irq-cause code still reading the fill-stall value. The counter saturates and the flag clears only on forward progress, so a counter that wrapped or a flag that self-cleared on saturation would drop the fault while the outage continued.
|CHK-WRITE-ORDER
|A write whose AW leads W, and one whose W leads AW, each answer a single BRESP=SLVERR and leave `fifo_level` unchanged. AW and W carry no ordering requirement between them (AMBA IHI 0022 A3.3) and `sep_entropy_fifo.sv:390` states the module accepts either order; the backend presents both in the same cycle, so the split-arrival arms of that handshake need the master armed with an `AxiTimingProfile`. A slave that assumed same-cycle arrival would wedge or answer twice.
|CHK-WRITE-SLVERR
|Status write returns SLVERR and does not change `fifo_level` while the pool is not full (a full FIFO cannot show a refused fill).
|CHK-UNMAPPED
|Every high-bit mirror of a live register, plus all three unique-dead offsets, returns SLVERR with RDATA=0. All three are walked every seed: `0x18` is the unused `[4:3]=11` code and catches a decode class the other two do not, so a seeded pick of one could miss it.
|CHK-STALL-ASSERT
|`[37]` high after `StallThresh` with the pool not full.
|CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-STALL
|`0x08=0x2` at stall (`fill_stall`, `pool_low` still clear after the fill). Must not copy the raw status word.
|CHK-STALL-CLEAR
|`[37]` low after EDN and ESRC re-enable.
|CHK-POP-DATA
|Accepted pops OKAY, level minus one, nonzero distinct words.
|CHK-POOL-LOW-REHIGH
|`[36]` high again after drain (high-low-high).
|CHK-POOL-LOW-EDGE
|On every status read the test makes, the `[36]` aggregate agrees with `status[6]`. Occupancy is not compared against a FIFO watermark. The high-low-high walk of `[36]` is the live-flag evidence.
|CHK-EMPTY-SLVERR
|Pop at `0x10` on empty returns SLVERR, after an accepted pop.
|CHK5_pool
|Native EDN beats reached the pool adapter (observe-mode floor). Bit-exact per-beat routing stays with `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, not this row.
|CHK-ALERTS-ZERO
|CSRNG/EDN error and recoverable-alert status are clear after the aperture walk.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: fill is observed as `pool_low` clear and `fifo_level` greater than 0 before any refusal is
scored. An accepted pop happens before the empty-pop SLVERR. `[36]` is seen
high, then low, then high. `[37]` is the first fill-stall after the fill path is
starved for `StallThresh` with the pool not full. At least one popped word is
nonzero and two popped words differ.

*RTL fact that arms `[37]`*: ESRC CTRL `MODULE_ENABLE=0` does not drop AUTO-mode
EDN acks while CSRNG still has a seed, and after that disable the pool runs to
full (level 32). `fill_stall` needs `req_pending && !edn_ack` for `StallThresh`,
and a full pool holds `req_pending` low. The stall wait therefore also writes
EDN_CTRL `EDN_ENABLE=False` (legal mubi4, other fields unchanged) and pops
leftover words until the pool has room and acks have stopped.

*Not claimed*: full PIC delivery (claim / mask / ISR). Drain-under-fill and
recovery stress are not exercised here. Stall duration is graded by
`CHK-STALL-DURATION`; what remains unclaimed is a stall that outlasts a
recovery attempt.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), *real PROD fuse sense* — no `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-REP — entries 0 and 7 × two windows × `{rw, r, w}` at match-all, plus entry 0 / window 0 source-ID match and mismatch, run every seed. Seed varies the window data, the entry-15 granule window, and the bound-probe registers.

*Objective*: Brings the lifecycle controller up in PROD so the inbound filter is genuinely active (`sep_debug=0`, value-checked against the lifecycle golden), then walks entries 0 and 7, two address windows, and `{rw, read-only, write-only}` at `src_id=0` (match-all), plus entry 0 / window 0 x `{rw, r, w}` at `src_id=0x5` with a matching AXI `user[3:0]` and one src-mismatch cell — 16 cells in one invocation — then the same-page `allow_burst=1` granule, page-bound, and `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` on entry 15.

*Test Coverage*: Sixteen inbound-filter cells plus burst granule, page-bound, and lock.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the lifecycle controller up in PROD so the inbound filter is active.
. Walk entries 0 and 7, two windows, `{rw, r, w}`, and the source-ID match / mismatch cells — 16 cells.
. Walk `allow_burst` deny then allow on AR and AW of the scratch window (2-beat INCR, AxLEN=1). The allow window spans two 4 KB pages. The allowed burst becomes two Lite singles; the denied burst reaches none.
. Program an 8-byte `allow_burst=1` window inside one 4 KB page on entry 15. Prove the page grant on the bus, both page edges, then freeze `allow_burst` with `FILTER_CONFIG.locked`. The lock is sticky, so this step is last.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ALLOW-RULE
|An allowed external read returns OKAY *and* the exact staged value. A denied read returns DECERR *and* the error-slave sentinel `0xBADCAB1E` (`axi_err_slv` default `RespData` low half; `axi_filter_wrap` does not override it), so allow and deny are distinguished by data as well as by response code.
|CHK-BLOCK-DEFAULT
|A non-allowed external read in the *same CSR page* returns DECERR — so region reachability is proven by the allow case and cannot explain the deny.
|CHK-READ-ALLOWED
|The read-allowed bit gates the matched read in both directions.
|CHK-WRITE-ALLOWED
|The write-allowed bit gates the matched write in both directions, and the allowed write lands (confirmed by a CPU-side readback).
|CHK-OWNERSHIP
|The CPU-LSU reads inbound CFG, outbound CFG[0], alias/AP/STEE remap bases, `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR`, and `SEP_REGION_SIZE`. The external master completes DECERR on read and write of each address, and the denied write does not land. Those CSRs stay outside every allow window (firmware must not allow-list them). `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` and `SEP_REGION_SIZE` share the window-0 (`SEP_SW_DEBUG`) 4 KB page, so they also prove the live window is START/END, not the page.
|CHK-NONVAC
|Allow and block were both observed under one lifecycle state.
|CHK-SRC-ID
|A non-zero `FILTER_CONFIG.src_id` matches only the external master's `user[3:0]`. The match cells keep r/w-allow gating; the mismatch cell DECERRs with both allow bits set.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Entries 0 and 7, two address windows, `{rw, r, w}` at match-all, plus entry 0 / window 0 source-ID match and mismatch — 16 cells in one invocation. Entry 15 carries the granule, bound, and lock cells.
|CHK-BURST-DENY
|With `allow_burst=0`, a single-beat external read of the scratch window is OKAY (anti-vacuous) and a 2-beat INCR (AxLEN=1) to the same address is DECERR. `traffic_filter.sv` `pass_burst` is not vacuously true.
|CHK-BURST-ALLOW
|With `allow_burst=1` and a programmed window that already spans two 4 KB pages, the same 2-beat INCR is OKAY and returns the staged scratch value. START/END read back as programmed (the same-page 4 KB granule widen does not fire). The granule widen itself is `CHK-PAGE-WIDEN`.
|CHK-BURST-WRITE-DENY
|The write-side `traffic_filter` (`aw.len`) with `allow_burst=0`: a single-beat external write is OKAY and lands; a 2-beat INCR write is DECERR and does not land.
|CHK-BURST-WRITE-ALLOW
|With `allow_burst=1` and the same two-page window, a 2-beat INCR write is OKAY and the poke lands on a CPU-LSU readback. START/END stay the programmed range.
|CHK-BURST-TO-SINGLE
|A filter-allowed 2-beat INCR (AxLEN=1) becomes two AXI-Lite singles on the system-CSR bridge (`u_system_csr_a2l_1`): AR/AW at start and start+4. A denied AxLEN=1 produces zero Lite handshakes (filter before the converter). `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`: convert burst to single.
|CHK-PAGE-WIDEN
|A same-page `allow_burst=1` window grants the whole 4 KB page (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`: `START_ADDR` rounds down, `END_ADDR` rounds up, `allow_burst` selects 4 KB). Measured on the bus rather than in a register mirror: an external access to an address inside the granted page but outside the programmed `START..END` answers OKAY for read and write and carries the staged value. The hardware-adjusted `START_ADDR`/`END_ADDR` readback is the setup step showing the rewrite took effect; a CSR mirror is not evidence of what the filter passes.
|CHK-PAGE-BOUND
|The 4 KB grant stops at both page edges. Under a scratch-page grant an access one page below is refused; with that page granted instead it answers OKAY while the scratch address turns DECERR. Both probes are proven reachable first, so neither refusal can be a decode hole rather than a filter decision. This is the security contract: 4 KB is the block pitch for the DMA CSR, watchdog and scratch-bank blocks, so a page-crossing grant would reach a neighbouring block.
|CHK-CONFIG-LOCK
|`FILTER_CONFIG.locked` freezes `allow_burst`. Once locked, clearing `allow_burst` and clearing `locked` both answer SLVERR, the field keeps its value, and the frozen granule still grants the widened page. The freeze is not in the register block -- `locked` is a W1S bit with no write-enable over other fields -- but in `sep_system_csr.sv`, where a per-entry demux routes a locked entry's writes to an error slave while reads pass through. Asserting the response alone would not prove the field held, so the readback and the still-granted access are checked with it.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the walk is inbound-filter entries 0 and 7, two address
windows (`SEP_SW_DEBUG` and scratch-cold[0]), `{rw, read-only, write-only}` at
`src_id=0`, plus the source-ID product on entry 0 / window 0, then the
`allow_burst` deny/allow pair on AR and AW of scratch and the burst-to-single
Lite split, then the same-page 4 KB granule, page-bound, and `FILTER_CONFIG.locked`
on entry 15. `CHK-OWNERSHIP` keeps inbound CFG, outbound CFG, the remap banks, and `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` / `SEP_REGION_SIZE` outside every allow window. WRAP, FIXED and
`AxLEN>1` are not walked: the Lite bridges convert those bursts to singles, so the
filter only ever sees `tx_len==0`.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: R/W, 64-bit, RO, valid, and write-once-set across the alias/AP/STEE remap and inbound/outbound filter CSR banks, and that every entry of each bank holds its own value. Each filter entry and remap region is an independent rule with its own storage, so a decode that aliased two entries, or a bank that implemented fewer entries than the map declares, would be invisible to a sweep that walks one entry per group.

*Test Coverage*: Remap and filter CSR banks: R/W, 64-bit, RO, valid, write-once-set, and per-entry storage independence across all 16 alias regions, 16 inbound and 32 outbound filter entries.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Write each bank and require the readback to differ from the observed pre-write.
. Prove RO `data_bus_width` ignores writes on both filters.
. Write a distinct pattern into every entry of each bank, then read all of them back, and require each to hold its own pattern.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|A written register's readback differs from its *observed* pre-write value, and the neighbouring field is undisturbed. The pre-write sample is required: asserting only that the written pattern is non-zero is a property of the stimulus constructor, not of storage.
|CHK-ALIAS-RW
|Region start, end and attribute registers are read/write, including the 64-bit upper word.
|CHK-AP-STEE-RW
|The output-remap CSRs are read/write across both banks.
|CHK-INFILT-CFG / CHK-OUTFILT-CFG
|Filter-configuration read/write fields (`rd`, `wr`, `ns`, `burst`, `src_id`, `group_id`) read back exactly on both the inbound and the outbound bank.
|CHK-BANK-INDEP
|Every R/W word of every bank entry holds its own distinct pattern: 224 words across 16 alias regions (`REGION_START` lo+hi, `REGION_END`, `REGION_ATTRS` lo+hi) and 48 filter entries (`FILTER_CONFIG`, `START_ADDR`, `END_ADDR`) are each written a value carrying their bank number, entry index and word number, then all are read back and required to match through the same mask the write used. The bank term matters because the two filter banks are instances of one RDL array type and share stride and word list: without it, inbound entry *k* and outbound entry *k* would carry the same pattern and a decode that aliased one onto the other would still read back what it wrote. A bank that decodes two entries to one storage, or that implements fewer entries than the address map declares, fails here and the message names the entry and word; the walked word count is asserted against 224 so a short walk cannot pass on whatever it reached, and every entry count comes from the register export rather than a sequence literal. The per-group field sweep above picks one random entry per seed, so this is the only leg that shows the other entries exist and are distinct. Deterministic: the same words are covered on every seed, so the three `reseed` runs repeat this leg rather than sampling different slices. `FILTER_CONFIG`'s hi word is not walked -- its only field above bit 31 is `locked[63]`, which `CHK-WOSET` owns; the read-only `data_bus_width` is masked out here and graded by `CHK-RO`.
|CHK-RO
|The data-bus-width field reads its fixed value and ignores writes of the inverse pattern, on both filters.
|CHK-VALID-RW
|The region valid bit is read/write in both directions.
|CHK-WOSET
|The write-once-set lock *actively rejects* a clear attempt with SLVERR rather than silently ignoring it — the strongest check in this entry.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: one region or entry per bank per seed. Cross-region isolation —
that writing region *k* leaves region *j* untouched — is not checked beyond the
immediate neighbour, and nothing records which indices a regression covered.

'''''

[[sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test]]
===== `sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: 64-bit TX FIFO, status, threshold IRQ, empty/full errors, and flush on the outbound mailbox aperture.

*Test Coverage*: Outbound mailbox TX FIFO, status, threshold IRQ, errors, and flush.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Confirm empty FIFOs in STATUS, then push native 64-bit WRITE_DATA beats.
. Overfill and empty-read must SLVERR with the documented error flags; flush drains TX.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|The empty transmit FIFO is reflected in STATUS, compared field-by-field against the mailbox golden. Receive-FIFO bits stay at their empty-reset values because this aperture never fills RX.
|CHK-64B
|Native 64-bit writes each occupy exactly one FIFO entry, so `depth` pushes reach exactly full. Occupancy is compared against the golden after every push, which is what makes the one-entry-per-beat claim real — a 32-bit split would trip `full` early. The transmit FIFO is write-only on this aperture, so payload survival is not claimed.
|CHK-WIRQT
|The write-threshold interrupt state matches the golden prediction, the pending register is gated by the enable (IRQP drops when IRQEN is cleared, IRQS stays), and the interrupt re-asserts after write-one-to-clear while still above threshold.
|CHK-ERR-RD
|A read of the empty FIFO returns SLVERR, the `0xFEEDDEAD` sentinel, and a sticky read-error flag with read-clear semantics. Both the sentinel and the `read_error` flag are the documented contract (`hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/doc/interface.adoc`). The golden takes those values from `env/sep_spec_tables.py`. Deny is distinguished three ways.
|CHK-ERR-IRQ / CHK-ERR-WR / CHK-ERR-WR-IRQ
|The error interrupt sets and clears on write-one-to-clear; a write to the full FIFO returns SLVERR and sets the write-error flag, with its own interrupt.
|CHK-FLUSH
|The control flush drains the transmit FIFO below the write threshold: not-full afterwards, and the threshold interrupt clears. Occupancy to empty is not observable on this aperture.
|CHK-WDATA-RD
|The push register is write-only: a read of it returns the documented `0xFEEDC0DE` constant (`hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/doc/interface.adoc`) with an OKAY response — not a FIFO entry and not an error — and leaves the transmit occupancy unchanged, re-compared against the golden afterwards. The golden takes the sentinel from `env/sep_spec_tables.py`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the read half of the threshold pair. `RIRQT` and
`STATUS.read_level_above` need the receive FIFO filled from the peer side,
which this aperture cannot do, so the golden predicts `read_level_above` as
constant false and no vehicle grades it. Data round-trip readback is not
claimed for the same reason. Both need the external inbound master — see
<<known-limitations>>.

'''''

[[sep_mailbox_peer_rx_rirqt_test]]
===== `sep_mailbox_peer_rx_rirqt_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and mailbox

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`) plus the external SMN-inbound master, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_mailbox_peer_rx_rirqt_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU and external master)

*Objective*: `fabric.adoc`: the mailbox registers sit on an 8-byte stride and a register's low and high halves alias to the same register, so "a read of `READ_DATA` at either half pops one FIFO entry. The mailbox drives the whole entry; the CPU keeps the half selected by address bit 2 and discards the other. Reading both halves consumes two entries." That, the data round-trip readback, and the read half of the threshold pair all need a value in the receive FIFO, which only the peer aperture can place there.

*Test Coverage*: Peer-filled receive FIFO: round-trip readback, pop-per-read at either half with the discarded half lost, the `RIRQT` read threshold with its interrupt, and the inbound filter gating that live peer path both ways.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped and ungate the mailbox clock.
. Program one inbound-filter entry to allow reads and writes over the peer aperture. The filter blocks by default, so without this the external master cannot reach the register and every check below would be vacuous.
. Read `STATUS` and `READ_DATA` before the peer writes anything, and read `ERROR_FLAGS` twice.
. Push two 64-bit entries whose four halves are all distinct, from the external master, at the peer aperture's `WRITE_DATA`. A non-OKAY response here fails immediately rather than being carried into the checks.
. Read `READ_DATA` at the low half, then at the high half, and compare each against the entry halves.
. Program `RIRQT`, enable `rtirq`, fill above the threshold, read `STATUS`, `IRQS` and `IRQP`, then drain back to exactly the threshold.
. Close the allow window, repeat the peer write, and read `STATUS` either side of it. Then reopen the window and repeat the write once more.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RX-EMPTY
|Before the peer writes, `STATUS.empty` is set, a read returns SLVERR with the documented empty sentinel, and `ERROR_FLAGS.read_error` is set and then read-clears. This is the control for everything below: if the receive FIFO answered the same way whether or not the peer had written, none of the following checkers could fail.
|CHK-PEER-RX
|The low-half read returns the low word of the first entry the peer pushed. This is the data round trip the host aperture alone cannot show, and because CHK-RX-EMPTY established the FIFO was empty first, a match cannot come from a stale or constant read.
|CHK-POP-PER-READ
|One 4-byte `READ_DATA` beat pops one whole entry. The second read, taken at the other half, returns the second entry rather than any part of the first, and `STATUS.empty` is set again after two reads of two entries.
|CHK-HIGH-HALF-LOSS
|The high-half read does not return the high word of the first entry. This is the specified behaviour and it is the opposite of the intuitive one: two 32-bit reads do not reassemble one 64-bit entry, they consume two entries and the discarded half is lost. A DUT that packed two reads into one entry fails here, and it is the reason the two entries are chosen with four distinct halves.
|CHK-RIRQT
|`RIRQT` reads back as written; `STATUS.read_level_above` is clear on an empty FIFO, set once the fill exceeds the threshold, and clear again at exactly the threshold, matching the strict greater-than the write half already uses. With `rtirq` enabled, `IRQS.rtirq` is raised and reaches `IRQP` through the enable gate.
|CHK-FILTER-DENY
|With the allow window closed, the same peer write that succeeded a moment earlier takes DECERR from the filter error slave and `STATUS` does not move. The refusal is measured on a path a healthy design has just been shown to complete, rather than against a bus that might be dead, which is what makes it a real deny rather than an absence of traffic. A DECERR that still enqueued would fail the second half.
|CHK-FILTER-REOPEN
|Reopening the window restores the peer write and the receive FIFO moves again. This is the positive control for CHK-FILTER-DENY: without it, a permanently broken peer path would satisfy the deny.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''


[[sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test]]
===== `sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Simultaneous IP-IRQ fan-in into the aggregator: one-hot map, no alias, RW1C clear.

*Test Coverage*: Simultaneous IP-IRQ fan-in: one-hot map, no alias, RW1C clear.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Fire a single source and require exactly one aggregate bit.
. Fire several together; the `[8:33]` region must equal exactly the driven mask.
. Re-sample the region raw and require every bit in it to be resolved.
. Write-one-to-clear each source and require the region to return to zero.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|A single source lights exactly one aggregate bit — the real per-bit map proof.
|CHK-FANIN-MAP
|Several sources asserted together light exactly their own bits, compared as an exact equality against the driven mask, and each source's own IP interrupt-state register reads 1. The per-IP read is independent of the aggregate.
|CHK-ANTI-ALIAS
|The `[8:33]` region equals exactly the driven set. That equality is the Verilator proof. X/Z resolution (neither X nor Z on any bit in the region) is claimed only on a four-state simulator — VCS default, or Verilator `--x-assign unique --x-initial unique`. The default Verilator regression is two-state and does not grade the X half. The region polls read through a helper that maps unknown bits to zero, so an undriven aggregator leg is indistinguishable from a quiet source there; the four-state path re-samples without that mapping.
|CHK-CLEAR
|All driven sources clear on write-one-to-clear and the region returns to zero.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Resolution limit*: the region poll matches on *some* sample, so a transient
smear that clears before a later sample would pass. The checker wording reads as
an always-property; it is not one. A two-state Verilator log that prints
`CHK-ANTI-ALIAS PASS` has proven the driven-set equality only.

*Not claimed*: CPU-ISR delivery of the simultaneous set, and the full 32-source
cross-product. One seed asserts one subset of at least two of the four
cross-IP sources; the `[8:33]` contract is identical for every subset, but no
single run covers every combination.

'''''

[[sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test]]
===== `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware `pic_irq_source_map_test`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Prove the PIC delivers a seeded subset of interrupt sources to the CPU with one-hot claim and RW1C clear (MUST trio every seed).

*Test Coverage*: PIC delivers a seeded source subset with one-hot claim and RW1C.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Arm the MUST PIC sources plus the seeded extras and wait a quiet window with no ISR.
. Deliver OTBN done to the CPU; complete when the source de-asserts.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|No spurious interrupt service before any trigger, over a quiet window with every selected source's interrupt enable already armed. The default vector-table handler increments `sep_dummy_int_count`; a non-zero count fails this checker, so an unregistered source cannot be swallowed silently.
|CHK-DUMMY
|After the source walk the same dummy-handler count is still 0, so an unregistered source did not fire during delivery either.
|CHK-DELIVER
|Each source's interrupt reaches the CPU and its handler runs. This also *carries the source-to-PIC-id map*, but by the claim id rather than by which handler ran: the firmware registers ONE shared service routine for every non-mailbox source, so the identity of the running handler distinguishes nothing among them. The routine reads the claim id out of `meihap` and increments the per-source delivery counter selected by that id, and the test waits on that specific counter. A wrong id therefore resolves to no selected source, or to a different one, and the wait expires either way.
|CHK-ONEHOT
|Triggering one source does not fire another *selected* source. Every selected PIC source stays interrupt-enabled throughout, including after the mailbox ISR (WIRQT is raised past occupancy rather than masking IRQEN). Sources outside the per-seed selection are not observed, so this is not a claim about the full aggregator.
|CHK-IP-RW1C
|Event-type sources and the mailbox clear by write-one-to-clear of `INTR_STATE` / IRQS. DMA is Status-type: `INTR_STATE` is read-only, and the ISR writes INTR_TEST=0 (and W1C `STATUS`). The witness is the IP state bit re-read as 0 inside the handler.
|CHK-PIC-COMPLETE
|No source re-fires after its handler cleared it — the claim completed, no storm. An unexpected PIC claim during that window also fails. The quiet window runs with every selected source still interrupt-enabled.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|Firmware consumed the patched MUST trio plus two seed-selected extras: the logged `SCENARIO` line must match the Python config exactly, which is what rules out an inert patch running only the compiled-in MUST trio. The firmware's own line here reports only that its parameters resolved — the compiled default satisfies that — so the host-side needle owns this contract.
|CHK-FW-REPORTED
|Every firmware checker above appears in the console, AND the per-source checkers appear once per selected source. The pass magic cannot show that a source was skipped or that a check was compiled out; a line count short of the selected-source count can. The pass verdict itself is asserted before any summary line is logged, so a kept log cannot carry a PASS summary under a failing verdict.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the MUST trio (mailbox, OTBN done, HMAC done) walks
every seed; two extras are drawn per seed from an eight-member pool (DMA done/chunk/error, HMAC-err, KMAC, CSRNG, EDN, KMAC-err), so one run
exercises two of the eight and six go untouched. With three seeds a run covers
at most six draws and is not guaranteed to reach all eight. DMA chunk/error and HMAC-err are walked every seed on the aggregator leaf. The map contract is
identical for every draw; which extras are graded here is seed-dependent and is not
tallied.

*Not claimed*: "wake on wait-for-interrupt, not by polling". The wait helper loops
on a `wfi` and a counter, so a `wfi` implemented as a no-op degenerates to a spin
poll and yields the same pass.

'''''

[[sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test]]
===== `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware `spi_ot_dma_tx_test`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Drives SRAM through the secure DMA into the OpenTitan SPI host transmit FIFO and on to the flash BFM, then reads the flash back and compares against the cocotb-seeded random source data. After the walk, a TXDATA write past `STATUS.TXFULL` must latch exclusive `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW` and the host must run again after recovery.

*Test Coverage*: DMA SRAM to SPI TX to flash, readback compare, and TX overflow recovery.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Walk the seeded length and trigger cells: DMA SRAM into the OpenTitan SPI TX FIFO, page-program the flash BFM, read the flash back and compare to the SRAM source; W1C-clear DMA done.
. Write TXDATA past `STATUS.TXFULL`; expect exclusive `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW`; `CTRL.SW_RST` plus write-one-to-clear; a flash status read must complete.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TRIGGER
|The transmit watermark tracks FIFO occupancy across the threshold, with the exact polarity asserted and the observed values logged. `EVENT_ENABLE.TXWM` is programmed.
|CHK-DMA-TX
|The flash content equals the SRAM source, compared against cocotb-seeded random data.
|CHK-DMA-DONE
|The DMA done bit clears on write-one-to-clear and reads back clear. Handshake mode does not raise `STATUS.chunk_done` (RTL: chunk_done is the inverse of cfg_handshake_en); that status is `sep_dma_basic_test`.
|CHK-SPI-IDLE
|The SPI host returns to idle with a clean error status.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The flash readback holds content that is not the erased 0xFF pattern, so the content compare above is not erased-against-erased. Scanned on the value the flash returned, not on the staged source, which the config forces non-erased and could never fail.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|The cocotb golden walked the deterministic length and trigger cells, requiring a literal floor of write-enable and page-program transactions on the bus and a per-case address and data match.
|CHK-MULTICHUNK
|Every PAGE PROGRAM the device received carries more *payload* than one 16-byte DMA chunk, so the transmit-watermark refill loop iterated. Counted from the bus, not from the scenario table, and counting payload only: adding the four opcode and address header bytes would let a transfer that delivered exactly one chunk and stopped clear a 16-byte bar.
|CHK-ERR-OVERFLOW
|A TXDATA write past `STATUS.TXFULL` latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW`; `CTRL.SW_RST` plus write-one-to-clear then lets a flash status read complete.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test]]
===== `sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware `spi_ot_flash_cmd_test`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: JEDEC, WREN/RDSR, WRDI, page program, FAST_READ, and sector erase against the flash BFM, with a neighbour-sector intact check, write-protect and status-register breadth, and the three host `ERROR_STATUS` classes with recovery.

*Test Coverage*: JEDEC, program, read, erase, write-protect, and host error-status recovery.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. READ ID, then prove WEL set by WREN and cleared by WRDI.
. Program, FAST_READ, and sector-erase with neighbour intact.
. After the erase: prove WEL consumed; a page program with WEL clear must not land; opcode `0x35` returns the seeded status-register-2 value.
. `ERROR_STATUS` is zero after that sequence. Then inject underflow, reserved `CMD.SPEED`, and an out-of-range chip-select, each exclusive and recovered; READ ID works again.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PROGRAM / CHK-READ
|A page program followed by a read returns the exact random pattern at the exact random address.
|CHK-ERASE
|A sector erase leaves the programmed region reading all-ones and leaves the adjacent 4 KiB neighbour sector intact. The region is proven non-erased earlier in the walk, by the page program and read-back pair that `CHK-PROGRAM`/`CHK-READ` covers, so the all-ones result is a change of state rather than a region that was never written.
|CHK-NO-ERROR
|The SPI host error status is zero after the sequence.
|CHK-JEDEC
|READ ID returns the BFM JEDEC identity.
|CHK-RDSR
|RDSR sees WEL set after WREN. A controller timeout is a distinct failure, not a status byte that looks like WEL.
|CHK-WRDI
|WRDI runs on a WEL that RDSR just proved set, then RDSR sees WEL clear.
|CHK-FAST-READ
|FAST_READ returns the programmed pattern.
|CHK-WEL-AUTOCLR
|The erase consumes the write-enable latch. The latch is state of the SPI flash device model, not of SEP: what this grades is that the controller drove a well-formed erase the device accepted and retired, and it fails if the controller leaves the device armed. It is not RTL coverage of a SEP register, and takes no feature credit as such.
|CHK-WP-PP
|A page program issued with the write-enable latch clear does not land. The firmware reads the sector back over SPI and sees all-ones; the golden then reads the device model's stored array directly, off the SPI read path, so a program that landed while the read path returned a stuck all-ones value fails. The model records no payload on that transaction. The model drains to chip-select-high without decoding the address phase when the latch is clear, so this cannot separate a device refusal from a truncated command.
|CHK-RDSR2
|Opcode `0x35` returns the seeded non-zero status-register-2 value. A decode that folds `0x35` onto `0x05` returns the write-enable SR1 encoding (`0x02`) and fails. A receive path that returns all-zero also fails. The firmware does not re-read SR1 on this step; the fail cases are the two wrong bytes, not a live SR1 compare.
|CHK-ERR-UNDERFLOW
|Reading `RXDATA` with the receive FIFO empty latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.UNDERFLOW`, then write-one-to-clear releases it.
|CHK-ERR-CMDINVAL
|A command with the reserved speed encoding latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.CMDINVAL`, then write-one-to-clear releases it.
|CHK-ERR-CSIDINVAL
|A segment whose chip-select is outside `NumCS` — driven at the top of the 32-bit `CSID` field, so the stimulus assumes no particular `NumCS` — latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.CSIDINVAL`, then write-one-to-clear releases it.
|CHK-ERR-RECOVER
|READ ID completes with the BFM JEDEC identity after the three injections, so the host was released rather than left disabled.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|The seed varies the programmed address, word count and payload. The opcode sequence is fixed, and the BFM golden requires that exact observed order.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the transaction-order golden requires the BFM-visible opcode sequence
(JEDEC, WREN, RDSR, WRDI, program/read, FAST_READ, neighbour program/read, erase,
primary and neighbour read, post-erase status, the ignored write-protect program,
RDSR2, and the recovery READ ID). The three `ERROR_STATUS` injections put nothing
on the bus: the host disables its core the cycle the error latches. Dual and quad
modes stay infrastructure-gated and are not exercised. They are BFM-gated, not a
feature-scorecard hole.

'''''

[[sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test]]
===== `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: OpenTitan SPI host CSR reset, R/W, interrupt test, and error-status W1C.

*Test Coverage*: SPI host CSR reset, R/W, interrupt test, and error-status W1C.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read the eight control registers at documented reset with STATUS idle.
. Walk each read/write register: write, read back, then drive its unimplemented bits and require them to read zero without disturbing the writable ones.
. Drive the interrupt test register with the enable clear (logged as an observation) and then set.
. Induce each of the five claimed error conditions in turn and write-one-to-clear it.
. Fill the transmit FIFO across the programmed watermark.
. Measure the enabled command drain, then hold the same command off with the core disabled for a multiple of that measurement.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RESET
|Eight control registers read their documented reset values and the status register reads idle. Six of the eight expect zero, which a tied-off decode would also return — mitigated within this entry by the writability, interrupt and error checks below, each of which drives a register non-zero.
|CHK-REG-RW
|Each read/write register accepts a write and reads it back exactly. Each register that HAS unimplemented bits additionally has those bits driven to one and confirmed to read back zero, with the writable bits undisturbed. The probe pattern is derived from the register's implemented mask, not from the walk mask: `CTRL.SW_RST` is implemented and outside the walk, since driving it would soft-reset the core mid-walk. `CSID` implements all 32 bits and so has no unimplemented half to probe.
|CHK-NONVAC
|EVERY walked register's readback differs from its own observed pre-write value. Where a seeded value happens to equal what the register already holds, the test flips the lowest writable bit instead, so no register on any seed can satisfy this by writing back what was already there.
|CHK-INTR
|The interrupt test register drives interrupt status while the enable is set, and clearing the source deasserts it with no write-one-to-clear. The same raise and release are sampled on aggregator bit 13 (PIC source 14). Whether the enable masks the status register itself is logged as an observation, not graded: the register description does not state it.
|CHK-ERR-W1C
|Five error conditions (UNDERFLOW, CMDINVAL, CSIDINVAL, OVERFLOW, CMDBUSY) are each induced by their own trigger, observed set, and cleared by write-one-to-clear, with a clean precondition asserted first. Each trigger must set its OWN bit and no other: the clean precondition makes exclusivity checkable, and without it a device that raised every error bit on any stimulus would satisfy all five legs. The OVERFLOW and CMDBUSY triggers find the boundary from the DUT's own `STATUS.TXFULL` / `STATUS.READY` report and then write one beat past it, so no FIFO depth is imported as a golden. The sixth bit the register block defines, ACCESSINVAL, is *not claimed*: its documented trigger is a TXDATA write with no bytes enabled, which the AXI master on this path cannot drive.
|CHK-WATERMARK
|`STATUS.TXWM` follows the documented polarity (`spi_controller.rdl` `STATUS.TXWM`: set while TXQD is below `CTRL.TX_WATERMARK`): set while empty and at occupancy `wm-1`, clear at the exact watermark and after filling past it.
|CHK-ENABLE
|Clearing the enable holds the queued command off; setting it drains the transmit FIFO. The negative window is not a constant: the test first measures how many polls the same one-word command takes to drain with the core ENABLED, then requires the disabled command to survive a fixed multiple of that measurement, with `TXQD` still 1 and `TXEMPTY` clear at the end of it. A device that ignored the enable but merely executed slowly would pass a hand-picked window; it cannot pass a calibrated one.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test]]
===== `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: SRAM strobe, pattern, sequential, and partial-write breadth on the 64-bit port.

*Test Coverage*: Every contiguous WSTRB mask, the 64-bit pattern set, sequential distinct words, and the base plus last-valid SRAM word.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Walk every contiguous WSTRB mask against a Python strobe model.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WSTRB
|Every contiguous byte-strobe mask changes only its own byte lanes, with neighbours preserved — compared against a Python strobe model. A strobe-ignoring memory would zero the neighbours and fail, because unselected lanes carry zero data.
|CHK-PATTERN
|A set of 64-bit data patterns — all-ones alternating, walking one, walking zero — reads back exactly.
|CHK-SEQ
|Consecutive single-beat 64-bit words hold distinct per-address values, which is the real address-decode proof in this entry.
|CHK-BOUNDARY
|The base address and the last valid SRAM word both read and write exactly. The extent is the generated map, not a doc literal: `OCH_SEP_TOP_SEP_SRAM_BASE_ADDR` = `0x10000000` and `OCH_SEP_TOP_SEP_SRAM_SIZE` = `0x40000` in `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/sv/sep_addrmap_pkg.sv`, matching SRAM_N_ENTRIES = 256 * 1024 / 8 in `hw/top/sep_ip_integration.sv`, so the top valid 64-bit word is `0x1003fff8`. One-past-top decode-error is `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`, not this row: that address is the ROM base.
|CHK-NONVAC
|Two addresses are written with complementary patterns and both are read back and value-checked, so a read path returning the same value for both fails. Requiring an *unwritten* neighbour to differ from a non-zero pattern is not claimed — that difference is already guaranteed by the pattern constructor; see <<audit>>.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Infrastructure gate*: non-contiguous byte strobes are not checked — the AXI
extension derives the strobe from address and length — and are not
claimed.

'''''

[[sep_dma_basic_test]]
===== `sep_dma_basic_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware `dma_basic_test`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_dma_basic_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Secure-DMA control and copy breadth over the CPU LSU: documented reset values, the configuration lock reading locked while the engine is observed busy, the range-valid gate and its rw0c lock each with a positive control, FIXED/INCR/WRAP against a per-mode golden, 1/2/4-byte widths, `STATUS.done` RW1C, one invalid-opcode error with recovery, the address, address-space-id and transfer-width refusals, the inverted-range refusal, a copy that leaves SRAM for the instruction memory and back, a DMA-issued host-path command-integrity inject, and a fabric DECERR dest that raises `host_path_err` with the pin low. The mode and width walk is directed.

*Test Coverage*: Secure-DMA locks, address modes, widths, errors, and host-path integrity.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read DMA config registers at documented reset, then prove CFG_REGWEN lock while BUSY.
. While the range registers are still writable: `RANGE_VALID=0` raises `range_valid_error`, and an enabled range whose limit sits below its base raises exclusive `base_limit_error` with `STATUS.done` clear. Then lock `RANGE_REGWEN`.
. Walk INCR / FIXED / WRAP × 1B/2B/4B; consume the seed-patched SCENARIO line.
. Provoke one invalid opcode, then four exclusive address-alignment refusals; a clean copy after each error class proves the engine recovers.
. With `dma_host_intg_inject_i` high, one legal SRAM copy raises exclusive `host_path_err` and aggregator bit 41. `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` returns STATUS and bit 41 to 0. A legal copy with the pin low then matches the source snapshot.
. With the pin low, one copy whose destination is the first word past the DMA CSR window raises exclusive `host_path_err` from the fabric DECERR. CLEAR and a legal recovery copy follow.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RESET
|The DMA configuration registers read their documented reset values.
|CHK-CFG-REGWEN
|The model check in this entry: the configuration lock reads locked while the engine is busy — with busy actually observed, failing loudly if the copy finished too fast — and the *same* write is rejected while locked and lands while unlocked.
|CHK-RANGE-REGWEN
|The range-valid gate produces the documented error, an enabled range whose limit sits below its base raises `base_limit_error` *exclusively* and sets no `STATUS.done`, and the range base register rejects a write once locked. A positive control precedes the lock — a non-reset value is written and proven to land first — so "unchanged after the lock" cannot pass identically for a read-only register or a missing decode.
|CHK-COPY-MODE
|Increment, fixed-source, fixed-destination and wrap addressing each produce their expected image against an independent local snapshot of the source fill, with a sentinel neighbour check per iteration.
|CHK-WIDTH
|Each transfer-width encoding is written *and read back*, and the copy is byte-exact. Without the read-back the three iterations were indistinguishable — 16 bytes is a whole multiple of 1, 2 and 4, so a register stuck at its reset produced identical output.
|CHK-DONE-RW1C
|The done bit is observed set and then cleared to zero.
|CHK-ERR-OPCODE
|An invalid opcode raises exactly the opcode error — asserted *exclusively*, so no other error bit may be set — with the done bit required clear, then cleared by write-one-to-clear. The recovery copy after the error matches the independent source snapshot.
|CHK-ERR-ADDR
|The address validation the engine applies before it moves a byte. Four refused descriptors, each asserting its `ERROR_CODE` bit *exclusively*: a 4-byte transfer whose source is not 4-byte aligned raises `src_addr_error`, the same on the destination raises `dst_addr_error`, a 2-byte transfer on an odd source raises `src_addr_error`, and a 2-byte transfer on an odd destination raises `dst_addr_error`. None may set `STATUS.done`, and a clean copy after the four proves the engine recovers. The inverted-range leg sits in `CHK-RANGE-REGWEN`, which must run before the one-way range lock closes.
|CHK-ERR-ASID
|An address-space id outside the three encodings the IP register specification enumerates (`OT_ADDR` 0x7, `SYS_ADDR` 0x9, `SOC_ADDR` 0xa) is refused. Walked on the source field and on the destination field separately, each asserting `asid_error` *exclusively* with `STATUS.done` clear, then cleared by write-one-to-clear, then a clean copy at the legal id. The legal set is a DV-owned table in `fw/drivers/sep_dma.h`, since the register description carries only the reset value.
|CHK-ERR-SIZE
|A transfer width outside the 1/2/4-byte encodings raises `size_error` *exclusively* with `STATUS.done` clear, then clears, then a clean copy at a legal width. Distinct from `CHK-ERR-ADDR`, where the width is legal and the address is not: this is the width decode itself refusing.
|CHK-ICCM
|A copy that leaves SRAM. Every other transfer in this entry is SRAM to SRAM, so an engine that cannot reach the instruction memory passes all of them. The data goes SRAM to ICCM and back to a second SRAM region, and the returned words are compared against the source over a sentinel pre-fill, with the count of words that changed required to be non-zero so a transfer that moved nothing fails.
