The round trip is used because the core takes a store access fault on a direct write to ICCM, so the processor cannot pre-fill or read back that memory; only the DMA touches it, which is what the checker is about.
|CHK-HOSTINTG
|A DMA-issued host command under `dma_host_intg_inject_i` raises exactly `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS.host_path_err`. Aggregator bit 41 is 1 and bit 40 stays 0. `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` returns STATUS and bit 41 to 0. A legal copy with the pin low then matches the independent source snapshot. Each leg also requires the transfer to terminate, so a wedged engine cannot pass on a latch raised from its first command.
|CHK-HOSTFABRIC
|A DMA transfer whose destination is the first word past the DMA CSR window, with the inject pin low, raises exactly `host_path_err`. `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` returns STATUS to 0. The CLEAR register is a write-only single-pulse field that always reads 0, so its read value is not graded. A legal copy then matches the independent source snapshot.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Mode and width cells walk every seed; src/dst/length/fill come from the run seed.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: lockstep punch-through. The register path (`reg_path_err`) is not
exercised by this leaf at all; the unmapped-window term is proven on the
`sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` card. Persistence across a CPU-only reset, and
the rule that a fault arriving in the clear cycle still latches — both stated in
the register description — are unproven here.

'''''

[[sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test]]
===== `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware `rom_lsu_read_test`

*Source Module*: `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: LSU reads of the Boot ROM match the loaded image; stores to ROM are ignored.

*Test Coverage*: LSU reads of the boot ROM match the image; stores to ROM are ignored.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. LSU-read ten half-words of the loaded Boot ROM image.
. Store to ROM; content must stay the image literal and the CPU must not trap.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ROM-READ
|Load-store reads of ten half-words across five distinct 64-bit ROM words match the loaded image, against hard-coded literals. Nine of the ten expectations are non-zero, so a stuck-at-zero read path fails.
|CHK-ROM-BOUNDARY
|The last valid ROM word reads back exactly. One-past-top decode-error is `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`, not this row.
|CHK-ROM-WRITE-IGNORED
|A store to ROM leaves the content unchanged, compared against the *image literal* rather than against a pre-write read of the same address, and the CPU does not trap. The store's AXI response is not sampled on this firmware path.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test]]
===== `sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Warm vs cold scratch: warm bank clears, cold bank retains, both writable after release.

*Test Coverage*: Warm bank clears, cold bank retains, both writable after release.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Write both scratch banks, then pulse warm reset.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|Both scratch banks hold written values before any reset, so the retain and clear checks below are observations rather than reset-value reads.
|CHK-WARM-RST
|A warm reset source drops the CPU reset observable (assert then release). Isolation of the system-reset observable is not this row: `wdt_rst_ni_i` has no fan-out to it.
|CHK-WARM-RECOVER
|Both banks are writable again after the reset releases.
|CHK-COLD-REINIT
|A cold reset clears the cold bank too.
|CHK-WARM-CLEAR / CHK-WARM-RETAIN
|The warm-reset scratch bank clears while the cold bank retains its value across the same event — the pair that makes the domain split real.
|CHK-BANK-ALIAS
|Each of the sixteen implemented registers holds its own pattern, so an index-to-index or bank-to-bank alias fails.
|CHK-WARM-BANK
|A warm reset clears every warm register and leaves every cold register at its pattern.
|CHK-COLD-BANK
|A cold reset clears every register of both banks. Both banks are re-armed with non-reset values immediately before the cold reset, so no assertion in the sweep compares a zero against a register the earlier warm reset had already cleared.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Independence limit*: the front-door and back-door values are not independent —
the probe is assigned from the same flops the register read returns — so their
agreement catches a read-mux fault, not an independent confirmation that the
reset landed.

'''''

[[sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test]]
===== `sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: the CPU and system reset observables are released at rest, and the
CPU observable is live under a real reset source. `dbg_rstb_i` isolation is not
claimed: the pin has no netlist path to either observable in this build.

*Test Coverage*: CPU and system reset observables at rest; debug-reset independence.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Both reset observables released at rest with `dbg_rstb_i` high.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-BASELINE
|Both reset observables are released at rest.
|CHK-LIVE
|A real reset source drops the CPU reset observable and then restores it, so the observable is live rather than stuck at one. This is what keeps the entry worth running.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*No scorecard row.* The denominator rule is that the architecture
documents define the feature set. `dbg_rstb_i` is named in them — a port-table
line in `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` and a sentence in the integrator guide
describing a separate reset for the EL2 CPU debug module — but neither states a
behavioural contract, and nothing requires isolation from the system reset. A feature the specification does not define is not a
row, so this entry earns none and its absence is not an oversight. The two
checkers above are worth running as a reset-tree liveness guard; they are not a
feature credit. Closing the isolation claim needs both a run mode where
`dbg_rstb_i` reaches `sep_cpu` and a specification statement to check it against.

'''''

[[sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test]]
===== `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Wakeup and watchdog tick, threshold expiry, wakeup cause and its RW1C, the watchdog pet, and the REGWEN lock on the AON timer. Bark and bite are not claimed here: `sep_nmi_sanity_test` and `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` own those legs, and no checker in this entry observes either.

*Test Coverage*: AON timer tick, threshold, wakeup cause RW1C, pet, and REGWEN,
plus that the `clk_ref_i` reference counter advances and its crossing onto
`clk_i` hands the value over. The software load of that counter is not claimed.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Expire the wakeup threshold and W1C-clear the interrupt.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WKUP-COUNT
|The wakeup counter advances on its own tick, and `INTR_STATE.wkup_expired` stays 0 under the high threshold for that window.
|CHK-WKUP-EXPIRE
|The wakeup threshold expiry sets its interrupt, which then clears on write-one-to-clear.
|CHK-WKUP-CAUSE
|The wakeup cause register is still set after COUNT is reset, then write-0 clears it.
|CHK-WDOG-PET
|Petting the watchdog resets its counter. The observation window must run long enough that the pre-pet count exceeds the clock-crossing tolerance by a wide margin, and the post-pet value must be clearly below it. A tolerance wider than the count the window reaches passes when the pet is ignored — see <<audit>>.
|CHK-NONVAC
|A pre-lock threshold write lands, so the lock check below is not reading a reset value.
|CHK-REGWEN-LOCK
|Once locked, both `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` and `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` reject writes.
|CHK-WKUP-PRESCALE
|`WKUP_CTRL.prescaler` divides the wakeup count rate. The measurement window is sized from the programmed divisor so the divided run must advance (a frozen counter fails the lower bound) and must stay at or below the divisor bound (an ignored prescaler fails the upper bound). Both bounds come from the divisor, not from the undivided measurement. The divisor is the OpenTitan AON Timer specification's cycles-per-tick rule (one more than `WKUP_CTRL.prescaler`), carried by `sep_spec_tables`; it is not taken from the timer RTL.
|CHK-INTR-TEST
|`INTR_TEST` forces `INTR_STATE.wkup_expired` with the wakeup counter off, then write-one-to-clear releases it.
|CHK-REGWEN-SCOPE
|With the watchdog lock closed, `WKUP_THOLD_LO` and `WDOG_COUNT` still accept writes. The lock covers the watchdog configuration only. `WDOG_COUNT` is free-running and `WDOG_CTRL` is itself lock-covered, so the write is proven by LOWERING the counter, which free running cannot do: the counter first climbs past a literal floor, and the write to zero must then read back below the value it held. Raising the counter instead would be satisfied by free running alone on a blocked write, and a target near the seeded bark threshold would trip a bark.
|CHK-REFCNT-RUNS
|`SEP_CPU_CTRL.REFERENCE_COUNTER` advances across an observation window. This is the only SEP counter clocked by `clk_ref_i` rather than `clk_i`: it counts on the reference edge and resynchronises the value onto `clk_i` for the CSR read, so a reference clock that is not running and a crossing that never hands the value over both present as a perfectly stable CSR. The 64-bit read takes the high half, the low half and the high half again, and repeats the low half if a carry landed between them, so a carry is not reported as a count that went backwards.
|===

*Not claimed*: the software LOAD of `REFERENCE_COUNTER`. The written value is
lost whenever `clk_i` runs far faster than `clk_ref_i`. At a 4 ns core period
against the 40 ns reference the write never reaches the counter; at 8 ns and
16 ns it does. The update crosses on a depth-1 async FIFO whose source comment
states that an update arriving before the previous one has crossed is "dropped
with no error indication", and that primitive's guard assertion is compiled
out of this build, so the loss is silent. A checker here would claim a
contract this elaboration does not honour at every clock ratio the
environment generates. Needs a designer ruling before it is claimed.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_otbn_compute_rand_test]]
===== `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden (strict CHK1..CHK4 plus `CHK5_otbn_urnd` in observe mode)

*Objective*: Loads a tiny RV32I program into OTBN IMEM, stages two seeded DMEM operands, issues EXECUTE, and checks `ERR_BITS==0` plus the DMEM result against an independent Python golden, while the strict entropy scoreboard runs against OTBN's live URND draw.

*Test Coverage*: OTBN ADD/XOR/AND vs an independent golden, with URND observed.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up `no_cpu`, park AES/HMAC/KMAC, bring up entropy for OTBN URND wipe.
. Walk ADD, XOR, AND against the independent golden.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ERRBITS
|Every compute cell retires with `ERR_BITS==0`.
|CHK-DMEM
|Every compute cell's DMEM result equals the Python golden for that op and those operands. The result word is pre-seeded with the golden's exact complement, so a program that never stores its result fails here rather than passing on a seed whose golden happens to be zero.
|CHK1..CHK4 / CHK5_otbn_urnd
|This entry also runs the strict entropy scoreboard against the chained golden, with `CHK5_otbn_urnd` in observe mode, because OTBN draws real URND entropy while it computes.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|ADD, XOR, and AND ran in one invocation. Operands come from the run seed.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_clock_gate_control_test]]
===== `sep_clock_gate_control_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_clock_gate_control_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` implements one bit (`pka_cg_enable[0:0]`); RTL sinks it into an unused net.

*Test Coverage*: `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` storage and reserved-bit RAZWI; no live per-IP gate.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Write `pka_cg_enable` 1 then 0 and read it back.
. Read AES / HMAC / OTBN / SW_DEBUG CSRs with the bit 0 and 1.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-STORAGE
|The implemented bit writes and reads back in both directions.
|CHK-STUB-CONST
|AES STATUS, HMAC INTR_STATE, OTBN STATUS and SW_DEBUG read the *same value* with `pka_cg_enable` set and clear, not merely OKAY on both: the first read of each witness is kept and the second, taken with the bit flipped, must match it. A witness whose value moved with the enable would answer OKAY either way, so completing the access is not the contract. Bound: these CSRs are constant in this build, so the compare catches an enable that perturbs them and cannot by itself distinguish "the bit gates nothing" from "these witnesses never change".
|CHK-RSVD-RAZWI
|An all-ones write to both 32-bit halves of `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` reads back only the implemented bit. An unimplemented bit that stores, or an alias onto the upper half, fails here.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: the implemented contract is the designer placeholder, not a live
per-IP clock gate.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Program × lock on every legal spare field in one invocation.

*Test Coverage*: Program then lock on every legal spare field in one invocation.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Walk `SPARE0` through `SPARE7` in order. For each spare, program one seed-selected bit while unlocked; OTP readback and resense shadow match the independent image golden.
. Program that spare's own `LOCKS_SPARE` bit, then reject a second bit (`PROGRAM_ERR`). OTP and resense golden keep the rejected bit 0.
. Spare `k+1` is programmed only after spare `k` is locked, so a lock bit wired past its own slot fails the next spare's unlocked-program cell.
. The seed selects only the bit offsets inside each spare. Slot coverage does not depend on reseed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-UNLOCK-PROGRAM
|An unlocked spare bit programs and reads back in OTP and after resense. Logged once per spare.
|CHK-LOCK-REJECT
|A write-locked spare rejects a later program; OTP and the post-resense shadow match the independent golden (lock bit set, rejected bit 0). Logged once per spare.
|CHK-SPARE-LOCK-SLOTS
|All eight spares ran both cells in one invocation. A lock of spare `k` must not block spare `k+1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test]]
===== `sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`), `LC_PROD`

*Source Module*: `sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Stage the spare field's lock bits in the OTP image (same spare-slot geometry as <<sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test>>), sense at `LC_PROD`, then access the *shadow* aperture.

*Test Coverage*: Locked-field write refuse and the matching interrupt path.

The shared config draws four distinct spares and stages `SIP_DIS` /
`SYS_DIS` at 0. That is this leaf's seed-to-image map.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage write-lock and read-lock on two seed-selected spare fields, plus an unlocked spare as contrast, in one OTP image, and sense at `LC_PROD`.
. Access the shadow aperture for each of the three, watching the interrupt probe for the duration of each access beat.
. This is not an OTP program: the program-lock vehicle already owns `PROGRAM_ERR`.
. An unlocked spare in the same image is the negative control.
. Walk write-lock then read-lock every seed — they are two different branches in `efuse_shadow_reg_access_control`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-UNLOCKED
|Unlocked spare write/read never raises `[33]`; readback matches the write.
|CHK-WRITE-LOCK
|Bit [33] during the write, BRESP OKAY, following read equals pre-write, bit [33] low after retire.
|CHK-READ-LOCK
|Bit [33] during the read, RDATA sentinel, OKAY, bit [33] low after retire.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Datapath facts that decide the checkers*: the lock decision is made in
`efuse_shadow_reg_access_control` (`hw/ip/efuse/rtl/`) from the sensed `LOCKS` /
`LOCKS_SPARE` vector: a write to a write-locked field or a read of a read-locked
field is answered with pslverr low, prdata matching the sentinel, and
`locked_field_access_interrupt_o` high, which aggregates into
`sep_internal_interrupts[33]`. Consequently:

* *The AXI write has no sentinel on BRESP* — APB `prdata` is not a
  write-response field. The write-lock denial proof is `[33]` high during the
  write, BRESP OKAY, then a read of the same field (write-lock does not block
  reads) returning the pre-write value. Do not expect SLVERR; JTAG deny is a
  different path (DECERR plus the same sentinel).
* *A read-lock has no following legal read* — `LOCKS` are OTP W1S, so the field
  stays read-locked. The read itself is the denial proof: RDATA=`0xbadcab1e`,
  OKAY, `[33]` high during the beat.
* *The interrupt is combinational and pulse-only* — there is no sticky status bit
  and no `W1C` on this path, so the clear contract is deassertion after the
  access retires. `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` must be watched continuously
  across the beat; a sample taken after the access completes is always 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the locked and unlocked accesses are the same driver, the same
aperture and the same run, differing only in the lock bits of the field, so a
probe stuck low fails the assert leg and a probe stuck high fails the unlocked
leg. The deassert leg is credited only after the assert leg has been observed
high. The denial conjunct (unchanged write-lock readback / sentinel on read-lock)
attributes `[33]` to the locked path.

*Not claimed*: end-to-end delivery to the CPU — PIC claim, masking and ISR entry
— which needs `cpu` mode and a firmware image. This entry is
generate-and-deassert on both lock flavours of one seed-selected spare; the
`SECURE_TM_LOCK` fields are graded by <<sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test>>.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`), `LC_PROD`

*Source Module*: `sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test` (`+secure_tm_lock`)

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Prove the field map's `SECURE_TM_LOCK` term refuses a shadow write while the latched TEST_EN strap is high, and that the identical write lands with the strap low.

*Test Coverage*: The `secure_tm` arm of the shadow-write lock, on the five apertures the lifecycle chapter names: `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE`, `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Sense at `LC_PROD` with the TEST_EN strap low.
. Write a seeded payload to `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE`, `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` and read each back: the strap-low half, where every write must land.
. Raise `test_en_strap_i` and re-sense, so the strap is latched into `secure_tm`. The re-sense reloads every shadow word from the OTP image, so each field's pre-write value in the half below is the sensed value again, not what the half above wrote.
. Write the same payload to the same five fields, watching the interrupt probe across each beat, and read each back.
. Write a `SECURE_TM_UNLOCK` spare with the strap still up.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SECTM-CONTROL
|With the strap low, the readback is the OR-merge of the pre-write value and the payload, the value actually moves, and `[33]` stays low.
|CHK-SECTM-BLOCK
|With the strap high, the stored value is unchanged, `[33]` pulses during the beat and is low after it retires, and the response is OKAY rather than SLVERR.
|CHK-SECTM-CONTRAST
|A `SECURE_TM_UNLOCK` spare still takes a write with the strap high, and its readback equals what was written.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Datapath facts that decide the checkers*: the write-lock term is
`secure_tm_i & lock[3]` in `efuse_shadow_reg_access_control`
(`hw/ip/efuse/rtl/efuse_shadow_reg_access_control.sv`), where `lock` is the
static per-field entry of the compile-time field map in
`hw/sys/sep/rtl/efuse/sep_efuse_pkg.sv`, not a fuse bit. `LOCKS` and
`LOCKS_SPARE` are one map entry, so a refusal on either is the same lock.
A refused write is terminated in the access-control block itself, so it never
reaches the shadow storage and the response carries no error. `LC_STATE` is
written in bytes [31:8], which OR-merge as ordinary shadow bytes and leave the
lifecycle nibble alone.

*Anti-vacuity*: each field takes the same address and the same payload in both
conditions within one run, so a write path that never worked would fail the
strap-low half, and a lock that never engaged would fail the strap-high half.
The strap-low half additionally asserts that the value moved, so a payload that
sets no new bit fails as a test bug rather than passing as a refusal. The
`SECURE_TM_UNLOCK` contrast separates the lock from a bus that stopped
accepting writes when the strap went up.

*Not claimed*: the JTAG and TRoot writers of the same registers — this entry
drives the frontdoor only. Nor is this a walk of every `SECURE_TM_LOCK` entry
in the compiled field map: the map also marks `RMA_CHIPLET_TOKEN_DIGEST` and
`RMA_SIP_TOKEN_DIGEST`, each carrying a source note that the lifecycle chapter
does not list them. The five apertures here are the set the chapter names; the
specification does not state which list is normative.

'''''

[[sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test]]
===== `sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Owns the demote product, including the demote lock.

*Test Coverage*: Demote matrix vs the feature-control golden, including debug-disable bits.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Walk TEST_DEV and PROD at DIS=0 through the four demote cells.
. W1S `sep_fuse_dbg` / `smc_fuse_dbg` and check PROD with both demotes set, so those bits close while Case 2 and Case 3 stay open.
. W1S a seed-pinned DIS pair and walk the four demote cells at PROD.
. Every cell's inbound probe is OKAY iff `sep_debug=1`, else DECERR.
. Every cell's `dbg_disable` vector matches the DTP gating ladder for that same `FEAT_CTRL`.
. Every cell's `sep_fuse_dft_disable` / `smc_fuse_dft_disable` ports match the compose of that same `FEAT_CTRL`.
. Lock one seed-selected `DEMOTE` at 0; a later demote write is ignored.
. `rst_ni` releases the lock; the same demote write then lands.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-FEAT-CTRL
|`FEAT_CTRL` equals the spec-derived golden for every walked cell.
|CHK-LIVE-GATE
|External access of the LC shadow is OKAY when `sep_debug=1` and DECERR when `sep_debug=0`. Not CSR storage alone.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Thirteen demote cells ran in one invocation (eight DIS=0, one fuse-dbg compose, four pinned DIS).
|CHK-DEMOTE-LOCK
|With `lock` set and `demote` still 0, a later demote write is ignored and `feat_ctrl` stays at the undemoted golden.
|CHK-DEMOTE-LOCK-RESET
|After `rst_ni` the lock is 0 and the same demote write lands.
|CHK-DBG-DISABLE
|All eleven `dbg_disable` bits match the DTP gating ladder for the same `FEAT_CTRL` the cell just verified. `dft_secure` is Case 3 and `dfd` is Case 1.
|CHK-FUSE-DFT-DIS
|`sep_fuse_dft_disable` equals Case 3 AND `sep_fuse_dbg`, inverted. `smc_fuse_dft_disable` equals Case 2 AND `smc_fuse_dbg`, inverted. The compose cell keeps Case 2 and Case 3 open while bits 2 and 3 are closed, so a golden that ignores those bits fails there.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Standalone RMA token matrix plus the token-comparator redundancy fault path.

*Test Coverage*: RMA token compare, match, mismatch, and lifecycle update.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start in PROD; LC shadow and `FEAT_CTRL` match the independent golden.
. Wrong SIP then CHIPLET token: no match code, program `PROGRAM_ERR`, LC unchanged after resense.
. Legal SIP then CHIPLET token: match code, LC advances, shadow and `FEAT_CTRL` match the independent golden after resense.
. Common-mode invert of all three RMA_SIP comparators on a valid token: legal mismatch code, no sticky bit, IRQ low.
. Common-mode invert of all three RMA_SIP comparators on a wrong token: legal match code, no sticky bit, IRQ low (fail-open hole).
. Collapse instance 0, then two-instance disagree: match status `6'b111111`, SIP sticky bit, `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`. Valid retry plus write-0 leave the bit and IRQ asserted.
. A cold-reset re-sense clears `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` and releases the interrupt.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-LC-FEAT
|LC shadow and LCC `FEAT_CTRL` match independent goldens after each resense.
|CHK-MISMATCH
|A wrong token does not update LC.
|CHK-MATCH
|A legal token updates LC (PROD → `RMA_SIP_1` → `RMA_CHIP_1`).
|CHK-RANDCFG
|SIP and CHIPLET both walked match and mismatch; tokens from the seed.
|CHK-COMMON-MODE
|All three comparators flipping a match to a legal mismatch does not set a sticky bit or raise `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`.
|CHK-COMMON-MODE-MATCH
|All three comparators flipping a mismatch to a legal match does not set a sticky bit or raise `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`.
|CHK-COLLAPSE
|Inject `3'b001` forces `6'b111111` and the SIP sticky bit. That encoding also makes instance 0 disagree with the others, so this check does not isolate the collapse detector.
|CHK-DISAGREE
|Two instances that disagree force `6'b111111` and keep the sticky bit.
|CHK-ERROR-CODE
|A redundancy fault writes `6'b111111` on every match-status bit.
|CHK-STICKY
|A valid-token retry and a write-0 leave `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` and irq39 asserted (`sw=r` until reset).
|CHK-IRQ-39
|`sep_internal_interrupts[39]` (PIC source 40) asserts with the sticky bit.
|CHK-WHICH-TOKEN
|Collapse on CHIPLET and SEC_DISABLE sets bits 8 and 16 (OR with SIP bit 0).
|CHK-JTAG-FAULT
|After `RMA_CHIP_1`, a JTAG read of `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` matches the AXI read. PROD/RMA_SIP JTAG token-block allow is `sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test`, not this check.
|CHK-FAULT-RESET
|A cold-reset re-sense clears `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` and releases `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`. The earlier sticky checks cannot tell a resettable latch from one that never clears. After the two zeros the detector is re-armed once, so the phase ends on a zero-to-one transition.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_illegal_state_fail_closed_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_illegal_state_fail_closed_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_illegal_state_fail_closed_test`

*BFM / Model*: none; DUT probes and a one-cycle state force

*Objective*: The eFuse read and program interfaces fail closed. Two contracts: from an illegal state encoding an accepted command is withdrawn rather than carried to the fuse bank (shown on the read interface; see the recorded gap for the program interface), and the machine retires reporting an error rather than resuming; and `read_error_o` is set by a failed or timed-out read and cleared by the next legal request. The word is `cleared by`, not `cleared only by`: the leaf shows the flag is still set once the timeout budget and the enable have been dropped, so the clear is attributable to the request, but it does not enumerate everything that cannot clear it. An interface that resumed from a corrupted state, or that carried a stale error into the next read, could return or mislabel fuse data it never legitimately fetched.

*Test Coverage*: Both FSMs, both illegal encodings: retirement on both, and request suppression on the read interface only. Plus the read error lifecycle across success, timeout, re-request and guard refusal.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Sense an `LC_PROD` image and wait for fuse sense to complete.
. Positive controls: a frontdoor OTP read and a frontdoor OTP program must each present a command to the fuse bank, on the same signals the refusal checks grade. Fuse sense does not drive either interface, so it is not a control for them.
. Measure the read interface's in-flight window: start a real read and count the cycles its bank request stays asserted. The injection below is only evidence if a machine left alone would have kept the request high across the same edge.
. For each of `2'b00` and `2'b11`, and for each of the read and program machines: force the state register for one cycle. The read injection lands while a real read is in flight with its bank request asserted, so the request must drop because of the injection. The program injection lands in an idle window, and therefore claims NOTHING about request suppression -- see the observation below. It grades the program interface's retirement only.
. While the injected encoding is held, require the read interface's in-flight command to be withdrawn.
. Release, and on the next edge require no command issued, `error_o` and `done_o` set, `busy_o` clear, and the read-back data not equal to the sensed control-word value, on both interfaces. The state register value is logged, not asserted.
. Repeat the live-read control after all injections, so a machine left wedged by the recovery is caught.
. Read error lifecycle, on the same interface. A completed read reports done with no error. A read given a four-cycle timeout budget -- too short for the SHIM to answer inside -- retires with the error set. A fresh legal request then clears it. Finally, with the `TEST_EN` strap latched and the image re-sensed, a read refused by the guard retires with the error set and zero data.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-READ-ALIVE
|A frontdoor OTP read drives the read machine out of idle, before and after the injections. Fuse sense does not use this machine, so only a real `EFUSE_READ_CTRL` read is a control.
|CHK-PROGRAM-ALIVE
|A frontdoor OTP program presents a command on the program interface, before and after the injections. Every `SPARE7` bit used as program stimulus is already set in the golden image. The interface issues its bank command either way -- that is the only thing these legs grade -- and the array cannot change, so an aborted operation cannot leave the OTP image disagreeing with the golden the end-of-test resense compares against. Both controls are load-bearing: every suppression check below holds on an interface that never issues a command.
|CHK-READ-HELD
|Control for CHK-READ-SUPPRESS. A real read holds its bank request asserted for the whole bank latency, measured on unforced outputs and required to be more than one cycle. Without it, "no command" could hold because the request had fallen on its own across the injection edge, which is true on any RTL.
|CHK-READ-SUPPRESS
|The read interface presents no command to the fuse bank while `read_state_q` holds an illegal encoding. The encoding is injected while a real read is IN FLIGHT with its request asserted, so the request drops as a consequence of the injection: a design that carried an accepted command through a corrupted state fails here. Graded on that interface's own request, because the two interfaces drive separate requests.
|CHK-READ-FAILCLOSED
|One edge after the illegal encoding the read interface issues no bank command, reports done and not busy with the error set, and does not return the sensed control-word value. The recovered state is a legal encoding (`2'b01` idle or `2'b10` wait).
|CHK-PROGRAM-FAILCLOSED
|The program interface reports the injected illegal encoding on `*_state_o` and recovers to a legal encoding (`2'b01` idle or `2'b10` wait), with no bank command issued. *Suppression is not graded on this leg.* The injection lands in an idle window -- a program command cannot be kicked mid-flight without burning a fuse -- and an idle interface has already retired its last command, so `error_o` and `done_o` are both 1 and `busy`/`req` both 0 before the injection. The data term compares the interface's fixed refusal sentinel against an image word, which are never equal. Those four terms are logged and none is asserted as a result. The recovered-state legal-encoding check is asserted. The in-flight read leg (`CHK-READ-FAILCLOSED`) is where the 1/1/0 verdict is earned, and its terms are asserted low before the injection.
|CHK-READ-ERR-CLEAR-SUCCESS
|A read that completes normally reports done, not busy, and no error.
|CHK-READ-ERR-TIMEOUT
|A read that outlives its configured timeout budget retires with done set, busy clear and the error set, rather than hanging or reporting success.
|CHK-READ-ERR-CLEAR-REQUEST
|A fresh legal request clears the error left by the timed-out read. Two controls make the attribution real: the preceding check establishes an error was actually set, and the flag is re-read after the timeout budget and the enable are dropped and required to be STILL set, because either of those writes could otherwise be the thing that cleared it.
|CHK-READ-ERR-BLOCKED
|A read refused by the guard while `secure_tm` is latched retires with the error set and returns zero, never fuse content.
|===

The waits in the error legs observe `read_done_o` on the interface rather than
polling the `EFUSE_READ_CTRL` status field. The legs grade interface outputs, so
the wait has to watch the same signals; a status poll would let a read that
never retired look like one that did.

Both illegal encodings are walked rather than one. `2'b00` and `2'b11` fail the legal-set test for different reasons, and a decoder written as a comparison against a single value would recover from that value only.

*Gap, recorded not waived*: request suppression is NOT verified on the program
interface. Showing it needs the illegal encoding injected while a program is in
flight, and aborting a program the SHIM has already accepted parks the SHIM
write FSM in its APB wait state until the next fuse resense, so every later leg
would resense across a stalled transaction. The cause is in
`efuse_interface_shim` alone: its read path copies the bank address into a
register at acceptance and drives APB from that copy, and its write path instead
tracks the live request for the whole transfer. That is why aborting a read is
harmless. Both interfaces behave the same, so there is nothing here for the
controller, and `efuse_interface_shim` is an example shim, so the behaviour is
not pursued. Injecting into an idle program interface was considered and
rejected: the graded request is already low, so the check would pass with the
RTL's suppression term deleted. An unverifiable property is left unclaimed
rather than covered by a check that cannot fail.

*Scope limit*: the illegal encoding is reached by forcing the state register, which no frontdoor stimulus can do -- that unreachability is the property the design relies on, so the behaviour asserted for it cannot otherwise be provoked. The force targets the state register only, for one cycle, and never the error, data, busy or request outputs the checkers read; the recovery those checkers observe is the design's own.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_digest_latch_fault_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_digest_latch_fault_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_digest_latch_fault_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip; `SepRmaTokenMatchSeq` drives the frontdoor token compare

*Objective*: The RMA-SIP token digest latch captures a frontdoor hash, holds it while idle, and stops capturing while the DFT test-enable is asserted -- so a scan-visible sticky register cannot be loaded with a real token digest during test.

*Test Coverage*: Digest latch capture, idle hold, and the test-enable freeze on both the digest and its valid bit.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Sense a `LC_PROD` image carrying the seeded RMA_SIP token.
. Present the correct token over the frontdoor. Watch `digest_latch_en_pre` and `vld_latch_en_pre` -- the pre-gate antecedents -- and require both to activate, then record the sticky digest and its valid bit.
. Hold for two idle cycles: both post-gate latch enables stay low and neither sticky field moves.
. Assert the DFT test-enable through the `token_digest_test_en_inject_i` hook and present a *mutated* token, so a capture would land a different digest than the one recorded.
. Throughout that presentation require the pre-gate antecedents to fire while both post-gate enables stay low and both sticky fields keep their recorded values.
. Release the test-enable.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DIGEST-LATCH
|A frontdoor hash captures into the sticky digest and its valid bit; two idle cycles leave both unchanged; and while the DFT test-enable is asserted the two `prim_and2` gates hold `digest_latch_en` and `vld_latch_en` low, so a second frontdoor operation -- proven to have run by the pre-gate antecedents -- neither latches its own digest nor clears the valid bit.
|===

The freeze leg is falsifiable in both fields, which is what makes it evidence rather than a quiet pass. Without the valid gate, `start_i` clears the sticky valid bit at the start of the second operation; without the digest gate, the mutated token latches a digest that differs from the recorded one. The pre-gate antecedents are the positive control: they show the operation genuinely ran, so the frozen outputs are not frozen because nothing happened.

*Scope limit*: the test-enable is asserted through a testbench force on `u_sha256_rma_sip_token.test_en_i`, which is DFT stimulus with no frontdoor path and is tied low at the SEP boundary. The force targets the enable only -- never the latch enables or the sticky outputs the checker reads.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Closes the PIC story the no_cpu RMA vehicle cannot prove: EL2 claims source 40, reads the SEC_DISABLE sticky bit, and masks `meie[40]` so the level-high line does not re-enter.

*Test Coverage*: Token-match fault to PIC source 40; mask quiets the request.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Firmware arms PIC source 40, publishes READY on the cold-scratch register, then presents a SEC_DISABLE token so a compare is in flight.
. Host injects a differential-pair collapse on the SEC_DISABLE comparator once READY is observed.
. The service routine records the claim id and the fault register, then masks `meie[40]`.
. Firmware holds a quiet window and requires the source to still be requesting throughout it.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PIC-CLAIM
|ISR `meihap` claim id is 40.
|CHK-PIC-FAULT
|`TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` secure-disable bit is set.
|CHK-PIC-MASK
|After `meie[40]` is masked the service routine does not re-enter, AND the source is still asserting its request throughout the quiet window. `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` is software-read-only and clears only on reset, so the line cannot drop by itself; without the still-requesting leg a quiet window would also be satisfied by a request that went away on its own, or by a sticky latch that never set. The check is additionally gated on an interrupt having been delivered at all, so it cannot report a mask result on a run where nothing arrived.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the other two comparators. This entry is the CPU/PIC half only —
the three-comparator breadth (`RMA_SIP`, `RMA_CHIPLET`, `SEC_DISABLE`), the
collapse / disagree / common-mode inject matrix, the software-read-only
behaviour and the JTAG read are graded on
<<sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test>>, and are not re-credited here.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the seed selects one bank, one remap region and one filter entry; the checkers and the addresses they compare follow from that choice. Coverage is one region of the bank per run, so the entry runs at a single seed and the card claims one cell, not a sweep.

*Objective*: Live remap plus outbound-filter drop.

*Test Coverage*: Live outbound / AP remap datapath, not CSR storage alone.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program one region to `0x8000_0000` and allow-list the remapped beat. CPU-LSU read of the window is OKAY.
. A second region, left at offset 0, returns DECERR.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-REMAP-TRANSLATE
|The programmed window completes OKAY at the remapped target. Identity would miss the allow window.
|CHK-FILTER-DROP
|A beat outside the remapped allow set is DECERR.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test]]
===== `sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — all five `HT_WATERMARK_NUM` encodings walk every
seed; the illegal selector and the low mode allowed to fall come from the run
seed.

*Objective*: Health-test watermark arming: each supported selector arms to `0x0000` high or `0xFFFF` low on `MODULE_ENABLE` 0->1, a selector write leaves `HT_WATERMARK` at the entry value, and an illegal selector resolves to `REPCNT_HI`. Scope: the ESRC → DRBG → EDN datapath row owns CHK1–CHK4 and stays where it is.

*Test Coverage*: Health-test watermark arming and the matching status.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Every supported selector arms on MODULE_ENABLE 0→1.
. Changing the selector does not re-arm. An unsupported value becomes `REPCNT_HI`.
. A low watermark falls once `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE` is allowed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ARM-MEN
|Each supported selector arms to `0x0000` (high) or `0xFFFF` (low) on MODULE_ENABLE 0→1.
|CHK-NO-REARM
|A selector write leaves `HT_WATERMARK` at the entry value.
|CHK-UNSUPPORTED
|An illegal selector resolves to `REPCNT_HI` and arms `0x0000`.
|CHK-LOW-FALL
|After arming a low mode, enabling health tests drops `HT_WATERMARK` below
`0xFFFF`. Both `APT_LO` and `MARKOV_LO` take this path on every seed: they drive
separate event counters in `entropy_source.sv`, so choosing one per seed would
leave the other permanently unproven.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The number of selectors whose DUT readback matched its documented arm value
equals the size of `enum WATERMARK_TEST`, read from `entropy_source.rdl` at
import. The bound comes from the RDL rather than from the walk generator, so a
skipped mode fails here. The same invocation covers the seed-selected illegal
selector and both low-mode falls.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the shared TRNG reset path. It clears the selector and the
watermark together; no vehicle asserts that pair.

'''''

[[sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test]]
===== `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — three seeds (`reseed = 3`). The seed varies the block order, the complement-vs-ones order and every touch value, never which registers are swept: the cell set is identical on every seed, so coverage is not seed-dependent. Reseeding is what exposes a register that accepts one mask-legal value and refuses another. The reset walk is the inventory after reasoned skips. Full-mask write-lands is 19 registers; 32 inbound START/END use the wrap model.

*Objective*: `env/sep_reg_meta.py` exports `addr`, `reset`, `mask` (software-usable field bits) and `mask_all` (storage, reserved included) from the generated SystemRDL.

*Test Coverage*: Seeded CSR bit-bash across the LSU-reachable register map.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Not a UVM RAL model.
. `env/sep_reg_meta.py` exports `addr`, `reset`, `mask` (software-usable field bits) and `mask_all` (storage, reserved included) from the generated SystemRDL. `iter_register_walk` counts OFFSET symbols that lack DEFAULT/struct (`nometa`) instead of dropping them silently.
. `SepRegBitBashCfg` is the single source of truth for both the walk and the skip reasons. ENTROPY_SOURCE registers that hardware advances while the noise source runs are excluded from the reset walk by `sep_reg_meta.reg_hw_updating()`: every register whose fields all declare `sw = r` in the generated JSON model, minus the constants named in `_CONSTANT_RO`. That set is 21 registers, of which 13 are tallied under `hw-owned; read value is not the POR value` -- the other 8 are claimed first by the `STATUS` and `RDATA` name suffixes. `sw = r` alone is not the criterion: COMPONENT_ID is software-read-only but hardwired (`entropy_source.rdl` `hw = na`, no storage in `entropy_source_reg.sv`), so it is opted back in and stays in the walk. The dead-space store compare uses the same accessor, so the two checks cannot drift apart.
. Write-lands is the anti-vacuity control: a complement write must move exactly the software-usable mask.
. Inbound-filter START/END stay in the write sweep under the full export mask; readback is checked against the `allow_burst=0` 8-byte granule (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`, `filter_ctrl.rdl`) with the peer at reset (`END_ADDR` reset `0x7`): START rounds down and END rounds up when they share a beat. The 4 KB granule is `CHK-PAGE-WIDEN` on `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`.
. The inventory is seed-stable. Measured on the current export: export 1921, inventory 1764, nometa 157, reset 481, write-lands 50, storage touch 129 -- the walk line carries the live tally and the checkers assert against configuration-derived totals, not against these figures. Write-lands is the full-mask registers (scratch-cold, scratch-warm, `SEP_CPU_CTRL`) plus the inbound START/END pairs under the wrap model. Storage touch is every admitted side-effect-free register. `TOUCH_BLOCKS` is the anti-vacuity list (AES/HMAC/KMAC/OTBN/DMA/SPI/WDT and both mailboxes); the sweep fails if any of those nine IPs stops offering a candidate.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RESET
|Every reset-eligible register reads its exported reset value.
|CHK-WRITE-LANDS
|A complement write moves exactly the software-usable mask bits: 18 registers over the full mask, plus 32 inbound START/END over ``mask[31:3]`` whose ``[2:0]`` are pinned by the same-beat wrap model instead.
|CHK-RO
|Bits outside the mask stay unchanged across the write bash. Counted only where the register has out-of-mask bits: 48 of the 50 have an all-ones mask, so the compare cannot fail there and is not claimed. Falsifiable on 2 (`PKA_CTRL`, `SEP_NMI_VEC`).
|CHK-RESERVED
|Reserved bits (`mask_all` minus `mask`) read back 0. Counted only where the register has a non-zero reserved field: 1 of the 50 (`SEP_NMI_VEC`, reserved `0x1`).
|CHK-BLOCK-TOUCH
|Every side-effect-free export RW storage register — 129 of the 1764-register inventory on the current export, not one per IP: write a seed-derived ``x``, assert ``(readback & mask) == (x & mask)``, restore reset. Shadowed names take a dual write. The gate is the per-register safety predicate AND the side-effect predicate; the second is required, because the first admits registers whose write drops live beats. `TOUCH_BLOCKS` is the anti-vacuity list, so the sweep fails if any of those nine IPs stops offering a candidate. Excluded registers each carry a tallied reason and the sweep accounts for the whole inventory. Not storage: hardware-owned status and state-machine observability words, the CSRNG reseed counters, an identity register, two read ports, `ENTROPY_SOURCE.FIPS_LOCK` (a sticky lock is never a storage touch), and two selectors that refuse a random mask-legal value — `HT_WATERMARK_NUM`, whose unsupported encodings sanitize, and `NOISE_OBS_CTRL`, whose mask contains a write-only singlepulse field.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The whole seed-built configuration ran in one invocation: the reset, write and storage-touch walks each covered every register the cfg named. `TOUCH_BLOCKS` is the anti-vacuity list, not the third walk. A completeness control over the sweep itself, not evidence about the DUT -- it fails when a walk silently drops work, not when a register misbehaves. The walk sizes (`export`, `inventory`, `nometa`) are logged with it so a shrunken inventory is visible rather than inferred from a PASS.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_axi_order_sweep_test]]
===== `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every (register x ordering) cell runs on every seed, and every (ordering x size) cell with it. The seed varies the staged data, the channel separation and the response backpressure, never which cell runs.

*Objective*: AXI write address and write data are independent channels with no ordering requirement between them (IHI 0022 A3.3), so AW-first, W-first and same-cycle are all legal stimulus and each must leave the written value in the addressed register without displacing its neighbour. The ordering is driven by holding the trailing channel until the leading one asserts VALID, and the ordering each cell actually presented is read off the bus and asserted against what was requested. Every block behind the crossbar terminates AXI at its own register adapter, so the contract has to hold at each adapter rather than at one scratch word. The sweep configures all three orderings at every write-safe register and crosses them with the 1/2/4-byte sizes on the scratch words, where a narrow write has no side effect. The narrow cells compare the whole software-usable word against the AXI lane rule; `sep_axi_strobe_window_test` owns the same rule at default timing.

*Test Coverage*: LSU-splice AXI burst order and response sweep.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Take the register inventory and the exclusion reasons from `sep_reg_bit_bash_seq`: `write_reason() is None` for the no-side-effect blocks, plus `touch_reason() is None` restricted to `TOUCH_BLOCKS` for plain RW storage inside the other major IPs. A second list would let two sweeps disagree about which registers are safe to write.
. Drop the blocks where the access itself would decide the result. `WDT_TIMER` is that block: aon_timer runs on a clock 1000 times slower than the system clock, so the AXI timeout spans about ten of its edges and whether a round trip fits depends on the phase the access arrives on. A probe there measures CDC latency against the timeout rather than channel delivery. `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test` covers that block's storage.
. Prime the register with the complement under its write mask at default timing, and read it back, so a write that never lands reads as the prime rather than looking plausible.
. Apply the cell's timing profile through the VIP master (`AxiTimingProfile`, `ocah_axi_master_config`) to hold AW or W back, write the staged value, and read it back at default timing so the compare tests the write and not the read.
. Read a second register in the same block, write it back with one software-usable bit flipped at default timing, read it again, then re-read the swept register. Deriving the value from the current contents keeps a write that never arrived from reading back correct.
. A shadowed register commits on the second identical write. The profile arms a one-shot release, so it shapes the first write only and the committing second write goes out same-cycle. The ordering under test is the one the register sees first; the commit itself is not an ordered access.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ORDER-LAND
|The written value is in the register after the ordering write. An adapter that latches a pending-write flag on AW accept and reads that flag in the same cycle the W arrives answers OKAY while dropping the data, so the compare is on the readback, never on the response. Every cell must produce a data compare; a cell that could not run is named with its reason, and the count then fails the test rather than reading as a full sweep.
|CHK-ORDER-DISPLACE
|The next write in the block lands on the register it addresses, and the swept register does not change when its neighbour is written. A pending flag left set by the ordering commits the following W handshake to the wrong target, which CHK-ORDER-LAND cannot see: it reads the register that was written, not the neighbour that was corrupted. The follow-on value is derived from what the neighbour currently holds and flips one software-usable bit, so a write that never arrived cannot read back correct. A refused follow-on write or readback fails this checker rather than being recorded as a skip. A cell in a single-register block offers no neighbour to write, so the required witness count is the number of cells that have one.
|CHK-ORDER-XSIZE
|All nine (ordering x size) cells produced a data compare, so the write lands at every transfer size and not only at the bus width.
|CHK-ORDER-STIM
|Each cell presented the ordering it requested, read off the AW/W valid assertions rather than taken from the configured profile. Asserted per cell. The slave's handshake is logged separately and is allowed to coincide: a slave that holds WREADY until AW answers a W-first presentation same-cycle, which is its own contract, not a stimulus failure.
|CHK-COVERAGE
|The cell count is asserted against a floor and against the cells the config presented, then the per-ordering tally, the blocks reached, the (ordering x size) grid and the witness count are logged. Every skipped register is counted under its reason on the configuration line the test logs first. A silent skip would let a shrinking sweep keep reporting PASS.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The cell count for the seed is logged against the configured total, so a shrunken sweep is visible.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test]]
===== `sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- every (register x ordering) cell runs on every seed. The seed varies the staged data, the channel separation and the response backpressure, never which cell runs.

*Objective*: The same ordering contract as `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`, driven from the SMN inbound master instead of the CPU-LSU splice. Both masters reach the same eleven IP block adapters, so an adapter that mishandles a channel ordering has to be caught from either. This path also grants a W-first handshake: the CPU-LSU register adapters hold WREADY until AW, so a W-first presentation is accepted same-cycle there, while the inbound path accepts W before AW.

*Test Coverage*: SMN-inbound AXI burst order and response sweep.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Assert the cell floor when the walk completes, and require the compare count to equal the cells the config presented, so a walk shrunk by a map or routing change fails rather than reporting a clean pass over fewer cells.
. Program the inbound filter from the CPU-LSU side: the filter denies by default, so five allow windows are opened over the swept extent. The window bounds follow `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` (Address Range Granule): with `allow_burst = 0` the granule is the data bus width, address bits [2:0] are ignored, `START_ADDR` widens down and `END_ADDR` widens up.
. Walk the cells exactly as the CPU-LSU sweep does.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ORDER-LAND
|The written value is in the register after the ordering write, compared on the readback rather than on the response, so an adapter that answers OKAY while dropping the data fails. Every cell must produce a data compare; a cell that could not run is named with its reason and the count then fails the test.
|CHK-ORDER-DISPLACE
|The next write in the block lands on the register it addresses and the swept register does not change when its neighbour is written, so a pending flag left set by the ordering cannot commit the following W handshake to the wrong target. The follow-on value is derived from what the neighbour currently holds with one software-usable bit flipped. The required witness count is the number of cells whose block offers a neighbour.
|CHK-ORDER-XSIZE
|All nine (ordering x size) cells produced a data compare, so the write lands at every transfer size and not only at the bus width.
|CHK-ORDER-STIM
|Each cell presented the ordering it requested, read off the AW/W valid assertions rather than taken from the configured profile. On this path a W-first presentation is accepted before AW, which is the inbound adapter's own contract and is logged rather than treated as a stimulus failure — the opposite of the CPU-LSU register adapters, which hold WREADY until AW and answer W-first same-cycle.
|CHK-COVERAGE
|The cell count is asserted against a floor and against the cells the config presented, then the per-ordering tally, the blocks reached, the (ordering x size) grid and the witness count are logged. Every skipped register is counted under its reason on the configuration line the test logs first, including the sixteen `INBOUND_FILTER_CTRL` blocks this path cannot sweep. A silent skip would let a shrinking sweep keep reporting PASS.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The cell count for the seed is logged against the configured total, so a shrunken sweep is visible.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the sixteen `INBOUND_FILTER_CTRL` blocks are not swept here and are counted in the skip tally. Those CSRs are the filter the access is passing through, so writing one mid-walk would reprogram the path under the test and measure filter behaviour rather than adapter ordering. They are swept in full on `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`, whose bus has no inbound filter. No allow window covers the filter CSR bank, so the ownership hole `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test` asserts stays shut.

'''''

[[sep_axi_id_routing_test]]
===== `sep_axi_id_routing_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Phase*: 1

*Bucket*: Fabric / AXI

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_id_routing_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: a response comes back to the request that owns it. The crossbars
prepend the master index to the incoming `AxID` and the demux keeps one
outstanding counter per ID, so the ID field is address-like state on the
response path, and a master matches its responses by it.

*Test Coverage*: the transaction-ID dimension of the SEP slave-port crossbar
path, with several reads outstanding at once on distinct IDs, including the
top ID the port's width allows.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Prime the eight `SCRATCH_COLD` words, each with a value that names its own
word, and read each back sequentially.
. Issue eight reads, one per word, each on its own `AxID`, and hold them all
outstanding together by driving the VIP master sequence directly -- the SEP
AXI sequencer awaits each item to completion, so nothing overlaps through it.
. Require each read to return the value of its own word. A response carrying
another word's value is reported with the word it actually came from.
. Grade separately that the IDs issued covered the whole width of the field,
so a shortened list cannot pass the routing compare silently.

*Randomization*: none. The proof is a value compare between an ID and the word
it asked for; a random ID or a random value on either side would remove it.

*Scope*: the SEP slave port only, whose `AxID` is three bits wide. The wider
inbound port and write-response (`BID`) routing are not claimed here.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ID-PRIME
|The eight scratch words are independent storage each holding its own distinct value. Without this a block that aliased every word onto one register would let the routing compare pass for whichever value was written last.
|CHK-ID-ROUTE
|With eight reads outstanding together on eight distinct IDs, each returns the value of its own word. An ID that is mangled, truncated or dropped delivers a read's data to a different outstanding request, and every access still answers OKAY while it happens -- only the value compare sees it.
|CHK-ID-TOP
|A breadth guard on the stimulus, not a second routing claim: the set of IDs the routing compare was given covered the whole width of the port's ID field. `CHK-ID-ROUTE` only compares the IDs it was handed, so an edit that shortened the list would keep passing over a subset; an ID field carried in too few bits only surfaces as a data mismatch if a read was actually issued on the top ID.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the prime leg establishes eight distinct values in eight
independent words before any routing claim is made, so a misrouted response is
distinguishable from a correct one. The concurrency is what gives the fabric
several responses to place: with one access in flight there is one response to
deliver and any routing would look correct. A read that never returns is
reported as such rather than counted as a pass.

'''''

[[sep_axi_strobe_window_test]]
===== `sep_axi_strobe_window_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_strobe_window_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the size and lane axes are walked exhaustively rather than seeded, so every AxSIZE at every legal lane offset runs on every seed and no run can issue zero narrow writes. The seed varies the data.

*Objective*: A write narrower than the bus beat must change only the byte lanes its strobe selects (IHI 0022 A3.4.3), and a memory window must return the data that was written to it. A stage that widens the strobe, or writes the whole beat, changes bytes the master never asked for; only a data compare sees it, because the response is OKAY either way.

*Test Coverage*: Write-strobe windows on the LSU splice.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Prime a scratch word with a known value.
. Issue a write narrower than the bus beat at each legal lane offset for each AxSIZE, and read the whole word back.
. Compare against the AXI lane rule: the addressed lanes take the new data, every other lane keeps the primed value.
. Write and read back the OTBN IMEM and DMEM windows.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-STROBE
|Each narrow write changed only its own byte lanes, at every AxSIZE and every legal offset. The line reports the count, so a run that issued no narrow write cannot read as a pass. The compare is the addressed 32-bit word: the generated SEP C headers contain no 4-byte-adjacent register pair, so a 64-bit beat has no neighbour CSR in bytes 4–7 and a widened strobe has no second register to corrupt.
|CHK-WINDOW-DATA
|Each memory window returned the value written to it. A window behind an SRAM-style TileLink adapter applies the mask on reads, unlike a register behind a register adapter which ignores it, so a bridge driving that read mask from the write strobe returns zeros with a correct response. A window not writable in this configuration is named with its reason. At least one window must have been verified: a walk where none was writable proves nothing and fails.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test]]
===== `sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test`

*BFM / Model*: TB-instantiated `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` (`u_tbadp_vehicle` in `tb/tb_top.sv`), driven pin-level from cocotb

*Randomization*: none. The three orderings are fixed and all three run on every seed; the contract is a cycle alignment, not a data pattern.

*Objective*: AW, W and AR are independent AXI channels (IHI 0022 A3.3). In idle the adapter accepts each write half independently until that half is pending, and accepts a read only when neither write half is pending. This leaf presents all three legal orderings at the port and requires both accesses to retire under each.

*Test Coverage*: All three legal AW/W/AR orderings at the TB-owned adapter port.

*Scope*: the MODULE's arbitration, not the SEP integration. The DUT's own lane adapters sit behind the crossbar, which delivers W one cycle after AW and re-serializes every write to that order whatever a master presents -- `CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL` on the two fabric-driven leaves measures the gap at both ends and reports it as fabric-introduced. A same-cycle AW/W/AR presentation and a W-before-AW presentation are therefore not producible at a DUT adapter port from the fabric side, which is why this vehicle exists. It is a second instance of the same RTL module with its own reset and no fabric in front of it: every legal ordering is presentable to the cycle, and a wedged cell is cleared without resetting the DUT, so the three verdicts are independent. It claims nothing about the integration; the two leaves that follow own that half.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read the live CSRNG lane and require OKAY before the vehicle is touched (`CHK-PORT-ANCHOR`).
. On the vehicle, retire a lone write, a lone read, and an AW-then-W gap with no AR, each answering OKAY (`CHK-PORT-CONTROL`).
. Present each of the three legal AW/W/AR orderings at the vehicle port. Confirm the port presented the named ordering (`CHK-PORT-STIM`) and that both accesses retire in bound (`CHK-PORT-PROGRESS`). Reset the vehicle between cells.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,4"]
|===
|Checker |Contract

|CHK-PORT-ANCHOR
|A plain read of the live CSRNG lane answers OKAY before the vehicle is touched. The vehicle is a TB-owned instance, so without this the leaf could report a pass against a DUT that never left reset; it also puts a transaction through the AXI agent, which the scoreboard requires as positive evidence before it will allow a pass at all.

|CHK-PORT-CONTROL
|Three legs, all required to retire AND to answer OKAY, before any overlap cell runs. A lone write and a lone read show the vehicle and the AXI-Lite-32 responder behind the adapter are alive at all -- without them a broken driver would report every ordering as stalled and imitate the defect exactly. A third leg drives AW and then W four cycles later with no AR, the same separation the `aw-then-ar` and `w-then-ar` cells use, so the separation itself is excluded as the cause of their stall by observation rather than by reading the design under test. The response code carries the other half: an access the adapter rejects as unsupported is answered SLVERR straight out of `StIdle` with nothing forwarded and retires just as promptly, so OKAY is what shows the forwarding leg is alive, and OKAY at the 64-bit port cannot be produced without the downstream handshake. The access itself is AMBA-legal by construction -- aligned, with the byte-lane strobe its address selects (IHI 0022), computed by `sep_spec_tables.axi_lane_strobe` -- so an adapter whose supported-access rule disagreed with the protocol answers this stimulus for itself rather than selecting it.

|CHK-PORT-STIM
|The ordering the port actually presented, read off `tbadp_chan_o` rather than taken from the offsets requested. A cell whose presentation does not match is a stimulus failure and is reported as one, never scored as coverage -- a cell that did not overlap says nothing about the arbitration in either direction.

|CHK-PORT-PROGRESS
|Both accesses retire: BVALID and RVALID are both seen within a bounded cycle count, and both responses are OKAY. The bound also records the longest run of held-valids-with-every-ready-low, so a hang that `ASSERT_KNOWN` would miss (a stable `1'b0` is not X) still fails the leaf.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the SEP integration. That a fabric master can present the one ordering the fabric can produce is proven separately.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test]]
===== `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- the seed varies the staged register value only. The channel placement is measured per build, not drawn, so a seed replays the cell without pinning it to a latency the fabric may have changed.

*Objective*: The same arbitration contract as <<sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test>>, but on the DUT's own CSRNG lane adapter and reached the way software reaches it. The two lane adapters sit on the CSRNG and EDN control buses (`drbg.sv` `u_csrng_axil_adapter` / `u_edn_axil_adapter`, fed from `sep_crypto.sv` `csrng_axil` / `edn_axil`), which is the path every SEP-level CSRNG and EDN register access already takes. The cell writes the block's `INTR_ENABLE` and reads its `INTR_STATE` concurrently, so both accesses are behind the same adapter.

*Test Coverage*: One AW-then-AR overlap at the DUT CSRNG lane adapter.

*Scope*: one lane, one ordering, one leaf. A stalled adapter cannot be recovered inside a simulation -- the port stays held and the master stays stuck on it -- so a second cell after a wedge would measure the wedge rather than itself.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Measure the CSRNG `INTR_ENABLE` writable width, then a lone write and a lone read, and place the AW-then-AR overlap from the measured crossbar latencies (`CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL`).
. Present that overlap on `INTR_ENABLE` / `INTR_STATE`. Confirm the CSRNG adapter port (`drbg_csrng_axil_chan_o`) saw the named ordering (`CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM`).
. Require both accesses to retire and the write to land (`CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND`).

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,4"]
|===
|Checker |Contract

|CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL
|The register's writable width is measured, not assumed: all-ones is written and read back, and the seed's pattern is narrowed to the bits that stick. The two blocks implement different interrupt counts, so a hardcoded width is wrong for one of them and a non-existent bit reads back 0 -- indistinguishable from a dropped write. A lone write and a lone read then measure when AW, W and AR arrive at the adapter port through the crossbar. The overlap is placed from those measured latencies: a placement the measured latency cannot reach is reported unreachable rather than fired blind. The same measurement records the AW-to-W gap at the master and at the port and states which side introduced it -- master 0 cycles, port 1 cycle, so the stagger is the fabric's. Plain untimed traffic already puts AW and AR in the same cycle whenever a store and a load are in flight together.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM
|The ordering the ADAPTER PORT presented, read off `drbg_csrng_axil_chan_o`, not the ordering requested and not the presentation at `s_axi`. The crossbar restaggers the channels, so a presentation at the TB master is not evidence of one at the arbitration point. The leaf is covered only when that presentation is the named ordering. A different overlap, or a cell that missed its overlap, is reported unreachable and does not count as this leaf.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND
|Both accesses retire and the write lands. The check is a bounded timeout plus a data compare against a complement prime; the prime makes a dropped write read back as the prime rather than as a plausible value. The check also records the longest run of held-valids-with-every-ready-low, so a hang that `ASSERT_KNOWN` would miss still fails the leaf.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the other two orderings. A true same-cycle AW+W+AR presentation and a W-before-AW presentation are not reachable at a DUT adapter port from a SEP master, for the reason `CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL` measures. They are graded on <<sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test>> instead, at the module's port. `CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL` logs the measured arrivals, so a fabric change that closes the stagger would make them placeable here and visible.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test]]
===== `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- as <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>.

*Objective*: The same contract on the second lane adapter, the EDN control path. `drbg.sv` instantiates `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` twice, so the two lanes are separate hardware and a defect in one instance is not evidence about the other. The checks are those of <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>, with the probe on `drbg_edn_axil_chan_o` and the access pair on the EDN block's own registers.

*Test Coverage*: One AW-then-AR overlap at the DUT EDN lane adapter.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Measure the EDN `INTR_ENABLE` writable width, then a lone write and a lone read, and place the AW-then-AR overlap from the measured crossbar latencies (`CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL`).
. Present that overlap on the EDN block's own registers. Confirm the EDN adapter port (`drbg_edn_axil_chan_o`) saw the named ordering (`CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM`).
. Require both accesses to retire and the write to land (`CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND`).

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,4"]
|===
|Checker |Contract

|CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL
|Same contract as <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>, on the EDN interrupt registers. The writable width is measured on this block; the lone write and lone read measure arrival at `drbg_edn_axil_chan_o`.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM
|The ordering the EDN adapter port presented, read off `drbg_edn_axil_chan_o`, not the ordering requested and not the presentation at `s_axi`.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND
|Both accesses retire and the write lands. The stall signature is the same held-valids-with-every-ready-low bound as the CSRNG leaf.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

[[sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test]]
===== `sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Testlist*: `system.toml` (`reseed = 3`), also in `all.toml` / `cpu_stub`

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — `SepAxiPartialLaneReadCfg` is the single source of truth for stimulus and goldens. Required cells walk every seed: all 36 contiguous (offset, length) byte-run slices of a 64-bit word (`8+7+…+1`), all 14 aligned narrow-ARSIZE cells (AxSIZE 0/1/2 at every legal lane offset: `8+4+2`), and the all-zeros / all-ones CSR patterns. The seed varies walk order, SRAM address, the 64-bit word, and extra CSR patterns only.

*Objective*: A read narrower than the 64-bit CPU-LSU beat only obligates the responder to drive the addressed byte lanes: a 32-bit CSR endpoint leaves RDATA[63:32] undriven, and a narrow-ARSIZE beat leaves every non-addressed lane undriven. The VIP master samples the full beat and extracts the addressed bytes, so the value a test receives must be a pure function of the addressed lanes — exact against an independent golden and identical on re-read — regardless of what the undriven lanes carry. `sep_axi_strobe_window_test` owns the write-side lane rule; here the write is trusted and the read extraction is on trial. The contract is pinned through the public VIP read path alone, with no cocotb or cocotbext-axi global state involved.

*Test Coverage*: Partial-lane reads on the LSU splice.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Log the seed, word, addresses and cell counts, and hold the walked counts against the 8-byte-beat floors (36 slices, 14 ARSIZE cells) plus the two required CSR extremes.
. Write a pattern to the 32-bit `SW_DEBUG` CSR, read it back twice as 4-byte reads with a checked read of the non-zero RO `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` register between them; repeat for the all-zeros, all-ones and seed-random patterns.
. Prime a seed-random 64-bit SRAM word and its neighbour with the complement value.
. Read every contiguous (offset, length) byte run of the primed word, re-reading each slice after a checked full-width read of the complement neighbour.
. Read the primed word at every aligned narrow-ARSIZE cell (AxSIZE 0/1/2 at each legal lane offset), re-reading each cell after the same complement-neighbour disturb.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RANDCFG
