|Walked slice and ARSIZE counts equal the combinatorial floors (`36`, `14`), and the CSR walk starts with all-zeros then all-ones. Seed, word, addresses and counts are logged, so a shrunken walk fails rather than reporting PASS over fewer cells.
|CHK-CSR-LANE
|Each pattern reads back exactly and identically on the repeat, through a beat whose upper four lanes the CSR endpoint never drives. All-zeros always runs next to all-ones and random patterns on the same register, so a dead write path or a zero-filled read cannot pass the set.
|CHK-SRAM-SLICE
|Every byte-run slice equals the Python slice of the primed word, and re-reads it exactly after the disturbing read. An extraction that starts at the wrong byte, takes the wrong length, or depends on leftover channel payloads fails the compare; the disturbing read is itself compared against the complement word, so the disturbance is proven to have happened.
|CHK-ARSIZE
|Every aligned AxSIZE-0/1/2 cell returns exactly the addressed lanes of the primed word, and re-reads them identically after the complement-neighbour disturb, on beats where the responder legitimately drives nothing else.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: write-side strobe masking (`sep_axi_strobe_window_test`); AxSIZE=3 (that is the slice walk's full-width baseline).

'''''

[[sep_axi_map_refuse_test]]
===== `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every directed anchor that is not on the unnamed-refuse exclude list runs on every seed, plus seed-selected addresses from the reserved rows. Excluded spans are skipped with a counted reason, and a row that could not yield its full quota is reported in the per-row shortfall line.

*Objective*: Rows marked reserved in the DV-owned table in `env/sep_axi_decode_map.py` allocate nothing, so an access there must not answer OKAY. The refusal expectation is taken from that table. Named reserved spans whose refuse flavour is unnamed are excluded from the probe set. Scope is the gaps BETWEEN block windows; `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` walks the dead tail INSIDE one, and neither subsumes the other.

*Test Coverage*: Unmapped / refuse addresses complete with the specified error response.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Classify from the DV-owned table in `env/sep_axi_decode_map.py`; a reserved row, and any address in no row at all, allocates nothing.
. Drop the named unnamed-refuse spans from the probe set.
. Read and write the surviving anchors and the seed-selected reserved addresses, and report the per-row shortfall where a row yielded fewer probes than its quota.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-MAP-REFUSE
|Every probed address is refused. The map does not mandate which flavour, so DECERR and SLVERR both satisfy it and the line reports the split. The probe set is the map-reserved addresses this test asserts; unnamed-refuse spans are excluded before the walk with a counted reason.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The DECERR beats the monitor observed on the bus equal the count the master reported, so the refusals are evidence from the pins and not only from the driver.
|CHK-OKAY
|A known-mapped CSR on the same bus returns OKAY and its generated reset value, read before any refusal probe. Without it a wedged or dead bus would refuse everything and read as a clean pass. The address and the expected value come from the generated SystemRDL export, and the register's reset is non-zero so the compare cannot pass against a tied-off decode.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The probe and anchor counts for the seed are logged, so a shrunken walk is visible.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the twelve directed wrap offsets on every seed, plus two seed-selected dead offsets per block window.

*Objective*: Negative control on intra-block dead space. Each block owns an address window and populates only part of it; an access past the allocated size owns no register and must be refused. Window ends come from `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`, which states the rule: the fabric refuses an address past the extent a unit allocates, and such an access never reaches a unit. Landing on a live register instead is a hard failure. A later beat of a burst that ends past the window's extent must be refused on the same terms as a single beat.

*Test Coverage*: Dead-space decode refuses with zero data.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Each block owns an address window and populates only part of it.
. An access past the allocated size owns no register and must be refused.
. Window ends come from `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`, which states the rule: the fabric refuses an address past the extent a unit allocates, and such an access never reaches a unit.
. A refusal that still writes the register is a failure, so every probe reads back the window's live registers as well as the response.
. A later beat of a burst that ends past the extent is held to the same rule as a single beat.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WINDOW-LIVE
|Each of the ten watched windows (secure_dma, wdt_timer, aes, otbn, csrng, edn, entropy_src, km_mailbox, lifecycle, spi_controller) has a non-empty allocated-register snapshot, so the no-alias compare can fail. The line reports readable and armed separately: the store compare drops hardware-updating addresses, so armed is the falsifiable count.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-REFUSE
|Every probed address past the allocated size is refused (not OKAY), including the directed wrap anchors (KM `0x10920ff8`, DMA `0x108008a8`). A single accepted probe fails the checker; there is no tolerated count.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-NO-ALIAS
|No allocated register in that window changes across a probe, the directed wrap anchors included, and a read of a dead offset never returns a live register's value. The alias compare is not gated on the response: `memory_map.adoc` says an access past a unit's extent never reaches a unit, so a refused read that still hands back live data has reached it. Inside the extent the same passage grounds non-aliasing in exact-match register decode rather than in refusal, so an accepted read that returns a live value is the failure there. A single aliased probe fails the checker.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-BURST
|A burst that ends past the window's allocated extent and answers OKAY, where the same address is refused as a single beat, fails. A beat inside the extent is also compared against the value the single-beat path reads. A burst that times out fails the audit rather than passing. A window whose dead space is 4KB-aligned, or that has no live words before it, carries no legal burst into it: it is named as not auditable rather than counted as a pass, and a run where no window was auditable fails.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT
|Each beat of the burst carries its own `RRESP`, kept in order by the bus monitor, so the refusal can be attributed to a beat rather than to the burst. No beat past a block extent may answer OKAY. The rule is asserted in that direction only: refusing the whole burst is legal AXI and stricter than the specification, so a window that refuses every beat is recorded, not failed. A second assertion requires at least one window that serves its in-extent beats and refuses only the tail — without one, the checker shows that nothing past an extent is served but not that the fabric can tell the beats apart. A captured vector whose length does not match the beats issued is treated as no evidence, since unrelated traffic on the bus would lengthen it. Read bursts only; see <<known-limitations>> for why writes cannot be.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`; omit `+skip_fuse_sense` so the shadow bank is live

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the eFuse shadow-register write path on every `periphs.adoc` fuse-field row except `LC_STATE`: set-only rows OR-merge so a bit that is already set cannot be cleared by a write of zero, and SW-writable rows overwrite. Randomization is RANDCFG — every walked row is visited every seed, word index and bit patterns come from the run seed, and the write-policy contract itself is directed.

*Test Coverage*: Every walked fuse-field row follows its specification write policy. The lifecycle nibble stays with the LC W1S leaves.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Scope is the shadow-register write path.
. Every `hw/sys/sep/doc/periphs.adoc` fuse-field row except `LC_STATE` is walked. Set-only rows OR-merge. SW-writable `true` rows overwrite, then a write of 0 clears.
. `LOCK` is both RDL windows: `LOCKS` word 0, `LOCKS` word 1, and `LOCKS_SPARE`. Assigned lock slots stay 0 at sense and are written after every other row so they do not write-lock a field this invocation still writes. `LOCKS_SPARE` stages ones only in the unassigned half; the assigned half is set from zero in that same late cell.
. `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` stay on their function-group word.
. Program × lock is a different mechanism.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SENSE
|The staged value is read back. Staged set-only words carry a known 1. Assigned `LOCKS` words sense 0.
|CHK-CLEAR-REJECT
|A frontdoor write of 0 leaves a sensed 1. Assigned `LOCKS` words start at 0, so this cell is not claimed there; `LOCKS_SPARE` claims it on the unassigned half.
|CHK-SET-STICKS
|A write that sets a previously-0 bit sticks; readback is the OR. Both `LOCKS` words and the assigned `LOCKS_SPARE` half take this cell from zero.
|CHK-OR-MERGE
|A later write of 0 cannot clear the OR.
|CHK-WRITABLE-CONTRAST
|Every SW-writable row overwrites, then a write of 0 clears, so the OR is field-local.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|Forty-one fuse-field rows walked in one invocation: six early set-only cells (`SIP_DIS`, `SYS_DIS`, `CHIPLET_PUBK_REVOKE`, `BL1_VERSION`, `BL2_VERSION`, `REQUIRED_ALGS`), three late `LOCK` cells (`LOCKS` word 0, `LOCKS` word 1, `LOCKS_SPARE`), and the 34 SW-writable rows. `LOCK` is counted only when both RDL windows are written. `LC_STATE` is not this leaf. `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` stay on their function-group word so the walk does not OR `sep_debug`, `chiplet_dbg` or `sip_debug` into `FEAT_CTRL`. This test drives the CPU-LSU master, which has no inbound filter.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_fips_lock_test]]
===== `sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every certified-configuration class every seed; pre-lock value and rejected poke come from the run seed.

*Objective*: The FIPS lock makes ESRC configuration write-once: a pre-lock write moves the field off reset, the same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`, write-0 cannot clear the lock, and `rst_ni` is the only way back, with the BIW observe enable still writable under the lock. The advisory RCT/APT cutoffs derived from `MIN_ENTROPY_H` are graded against an SP 800-90B oracle. Scope: health-test *quality* is not claimed.

*Test Coverage*: FIPS lock and the matching entropy-source status.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. One of two vehicles on the ESRC lock-and-alert scorecard row.
. Health-test *quality* is not claimed.
. `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` is write-1-only.
. Observe FIFOs stay writable under lock.
. Walks every field `entropy_source.rdl` marks `swwel` -- the
certified-configuration inventory `FIPS_LOCK` freezes -- one target per
register, except `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`. The sequence declares its coverage and
reconciles it against that RDL inventory at import, so a lock added or dropped
there fails the test instead of leaving the walk quietly short.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PRE-LOCK
|A pre-lock write moves the field off reset.
|CHK-POST-LOCK
|The same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`.
|CHK-LOCK-SET
|`FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` reads 1 after the arming write.
|CHK-LOCK-W1S
|Write-0 leaves `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1`.
|CHK-CTRL-RSVD
|Writing reserved `CTRL[0]` before the lock leaves `CTRL` unchanged, where the
other `CTRL` fields are demonstrably writable so an unchanged register isolates
bit 0. Repeated after the lock, the same write leaves `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1`.
|CHK-RST-NI
|`rst_ni` clears `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` to 0.
|CHK-POST-UNLOCK
|After `rst_ni` the released target reads its register reset, then takes the
poke that the lock rejected. The target is chosen so its poke differs from the
reset, otherwise the reset value alone would satisfy the readback.
|CHK-OBS-ENABLE-WRITABLE
|`BIW_OBS_CTRL.RAW_ENABLE` stays writable under lock. This is the control bit
only. `BIW_OBS_STATUS` and `BIW_OBS_RDATA` are not read, so the observe FIFO
itself is not claimed live.
|CHK-REC-THRESH
|`RECOMMENDED_THRESHOLDS.RCT_LIMIT` tracks `MIN_ENTROPY_H` across a sweep of
eight Q4.4 points, compared against SP 800-90B 4.4.1 `C = 1 + ceil(20/H)`
re-derived in the test. The oracle is the standard, not
`entropy_source_rec_thresh_lut.sv`, which the checker never reads. `APT_LIMIT` is
an exact binomial cutoff with no closed form; what is claimed for it is that it
stays inside the 1024-sample window and falls as the assessed min-entropy rises.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|26 locked registers walked in one invocation, covering 34 of the 35 fields
`entropy_source.rdl` marks `swwel`. Every register that locks two
fields moves both off reset in the pre-lock write, so a field inside the compare
mask is never left unwritten.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`. It is locked too, but clearing it stops the
block for the rest of the walk, so its lock belongs to the reset-recovery
vehicle.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test]]
===== `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — directed persistent-failure trip.

*Objective*: A short window plus stuck forced noise trips `ALERT_THRESHOLD`,
and the alert bookkeeping the RDL defines is read at that trip. An opening
`INTR_TEST` sweep, before the trip, drives every interrupt source the RDL defines.

*Test Coverage*: Entropy-source alerts reach status and the aggregator.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Second vehicle on the same scorecard row.
. From the clean pre-trip state, pulse each `INTR_TEST` source on its own.
. A short window plus stuck forced noise trips `ALERT_THRESHOLD`.
. Read the alert counters and the health-test latch at the trip.
. Health-test *quality* is not claimed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NO-ALERT
|ALERT, PERSISTENT_FAILURE, and PIC source 16 are 0 before the trip.
|CHK-INTR-TEST
|Each `INTR_TEST` source `ENTROPY_SOURCE.fields("INTR_TEST")` names sets only
its own `INTR_STATUS` bit, raises PIC source 16, and clears on W1C. Swept
before the trip: `INTR_STATUS` bits are re-driven from their live cause, so a
source whose real condition is latched cannot clear. The expected count is
that field list, not a literal.
|CHK-ALERT
|`MAIN_SM_STATUS.ALERT` asserts after the threshold.
|CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE
|`INTR_STATUS.PERSISTENT_FAILURE` asserts. Note it is the OR of the AlertHang
trip, a main-FSM error and a counter-integrity error; CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO is what
makes this one attributable.
|CHK-PIC-16
|`sep_internal_interrupts[15]` (PIC source 16) asserts. Only PERSISTENT_FAILURE
is enabled for the trip, so this adds the aggregate slot wiring on top of
CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE rather than an independent cause.
|CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO
|`MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR` stays 0 across the trip, so PERSISTENT_FAILURE is
attributable to the alert threshold.
|CHK-ANY-FAIL-WINDOWS
|`ALERT_SUMMARY_FAIL_COUNTS.ANY_FAIL_COUNT` sits inside `[ALERT_THRESHOLD,
ALERT_THRESHOLD + 8]` at the trip and stays at or below the summed per-lane fail
pulses. `ALERT_THRESHOLD` is 3: at 1 the alert comparator already implies the
lower bound, and no denomination is distinguishable at a count of one. The upper
bound against the per-lane pulse total is what rejects a counter denominated in
events rather than in failing *windows*.
|CHK-FAIL-ATTRIB
|`REPCNT_TOTAL_FAILS` is non-zero, attributing the trip to the repetition test
under stuck noise with `REPETITION_LIMIT` = 5, and each lane's 32-bit total
covers its 4-bit `ALERT_FAIL_COUNTS` counter. The two take the same fail pulse
but carry different widths and different clears, so the compare is a
DUT-to-DUT contract. The reverse does not hold and is not claimed:
`alert_cntrs_clr` also fires on a passing window.
|CHK-HT-STATUS
|`HEALTH_TEST_STATUS.HEALTH_STATUS` latches a failing test while the alert is
asserted, and is W1C. No reserved bit latches: `entropy_source.rdl` allocates
repetition [0], APT [3], Markov high [4] and Markov low [5], leaving [1:2] and
[6:7] reserved.
|CHK-GEN-HEALTH
|All twelve `GENERATOR_n_HEALTH_STATUS` registers are read at the trip. At least
one latches a failing lane, and none latches a reserved bit.
|CHK-W1C
|After leaving AlertHang, W1C clears both bits and the PIC line.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: `es_cntr_err` -> `MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR`. This is the health-test
counter-redundancy countermeasure: `prim_count` holds each count twice, up and
down, and reports an error only when `cnt_q[0] + cnt_q[1]` stops equalling the
counter maximum. No stimulus can break that invariant — both copies take the same
enable and the same step — so the path is reachable by a physical fault and by
nothing else. Proving it therefore requires fault injection, which no vehicle
in `all` performs — see <<phase-3>>.
CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO proves the bit is 0 on the alert path, which is what makes
PERSISTENT_FAILURE attributable to the threshold trip; it does not claim the
error path works.

'''''

[[sep_sec_dis_override_test]]
===== `sep_sec_dis_override_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`; omit `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_sec_dis_override_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — directed match / mismatch / restore.

*Objective*: A match is reachable because `tb_top` binds `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` to SHA-256 of the all-zero token. A match before sense-done must not release the sense-gated reset (`hw/ip/efuse/rtl/efuse_interface_controller.sv`).

*Test Coverage*: Security-disable match, mismatch, restore, and sense-gated reset hold.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. A match is reachable because `tb_top` binds `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` to SHA-256 of the all-zero token.
. The comparator hashes the written `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_I` and compares it to the metal expected digest reconstructed from `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` (`hw/ip/efuse/rtl/efuse_token_processing.sv`, `hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc`).
. After `release_no_cpu_reset`, write the all-zero token before `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`. Match must assert, and `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` must stay 0 while sense is still open (`ext_boot_seq_done_i` is already 1, so sense is the only held term).
. Wait for sense-done. Token `1` mismatches.
. On a later match, `sec_dis` asserts and `FEAT_CTRL` follows feat_ctrl_expected(..., sec_dis=1).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DIGEST-BIND
|DV consistency: independent SHA-256 of the all-zero token equals the TB digest constant. This is not a DUT or bind-port read. `CHK-MATCH` is the bind proof.
|CHK-SENSE-GATED-RESET
|A match with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0` and `ext_boot_seq_done_i=1` leaves `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` and `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` at 0. `reset_n` carries no SEC_DIS term; the release is a separate DTP `sep_reset_n` TDR step.
|CHK-MISMATCH
|Written token=1 returns `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH=0x2a`.
|CHK-NO-OVERRIDE
|After the mismatch, `sec_dis` stays 0; `FEAT_CTRL` equals the fail-closed PROD golden.
|CHK-MATCH
|Written all-zero token returns `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH=0x15`.
|CHK-OVERRIDE
|After the match, sec_dis=1 and `FEAT_CTRL` equals the override golden (all ones).
|CHK-DEACTIVATE
|A later token=1 returns mismatch again.
|CHK-RESTORE
|After that mismatch, `sec_dis=0` and `FEAT_CTRL` is fail-closed PROD again.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — region and source page from the run seed; the dest is `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` so the offset is determined and non-vacuous.

*Objective*: Scope is the sixteen programmable local-master regions ahead of the system-peripherals routing demux (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, `axi_alias_remap`).

*Test Coverage*: Local-alias remap datapath, not the CSR bank alone.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Distinct from the CPU high-alias window (`sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`).
. Scope is the sixteen programmable local-master regions ahead of the system-peripherals routing demux (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, `axi_alias_remap`).
. CSR programming stays on `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`.
. A programmed filter-bank page beat returns the marker written to `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL`.
. Local-master `REGION_ATTRS` has `valid` and `cacheable`.
. Cacheable overrides AXI `cache` on a hit; this vehicle does not observe that field.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-OFFSET
|A programmed region's live beat equals input plus the programmed offset (data matches identity `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL`).
|CHK-VALID-GATE
|`region_valid` gates the remap. The same window is reprogrammed with bounds and offset live and only the enable clear, and the beat must then read the value at the address the master issued rather than the remapped destination. Programming the window first is what separates a working gate from an address that was never in range, and the vacuity guard on `CHK-OFFSET` has already shown the two values differ. The cacheable attribute is not checked: `axi_alias_remap` drives the outbound cache bits from the region on a hit and this bench has no observable on them.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_km_isolate_termination_test]]
===== `sep_km_isolate_termination_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_iso.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_isolate_termination_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — one directed path, three directed legs.

*Objective*: What a Key Manager master transaction receives when its own AXI-Lite path to a destination is isolated. Every other isolation entry in this plan drives the SEP host port, which reaches the `host_*` paths and never the `km_*` ones, so the response a KM transaction gets was not graded anywhere. The KM CPU image stores to the same HMAC wrapper key address three times: clean while the path is open, an AXI error while `km_hmac` is isolated, clean again after release.

*Test Coverage*: `km_hmac` isolate termination, and that isolation does not wedge the KM path.

*Functional Coverage*: `sep_crypto_isolate_cg` in link:SEP_FCOV.adoc[SEP_FCOV]. This entry is the only leaf that fills all three bins — it isolates a KM path, presents a KM master transaction to it, and reopens it. The bins are edges rather than states, because both isolate bits hold high for the whole reset window and a state bin would fill even on a run whose reset preceded the drain.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Dedicated `km_rom_iso` on the KM CPU bus, not `rom_main`. The image publishes a phase marker in KM SRAM word0 and blocks on an inbound mailbox word between legs, so the host controls when each leg runs.
. Each leg W1Cs `IRQ_STATUS` AXI_SLVERR and AXI_DECERR and requires them to read back clear before its store. The bits are sticky, so without that precondition a leg would also be satisfied by an earlier fault anywhere on the KM CPU bus.
. Leg A with HMAC released: the store completes with neither bit set.
. The host parks HMAC by read-modify-write of `SW_RESET_N`, preserving the Key Manager's own release bit, then waits for the `km_hmac` isolate-completion bit before releasing the image into leg B. A leg B that raced the coordinator would store into a still-open path.
. Leg B: the same store raises AXI_SLVERR or AXI_DECERR.
. The host releases HMAC; leg C repeats the store and requires it clean.

[NOTE]
====
Legs A and C cannot fail on their own — "no error" is also what a dead poll loop
reports — and are not offered as evidence by themselves. They are the controls
that make `CHK-KM-ISO-TERM` discriminating: the same instruction at the same
address in the same image, differing only in whether the host holds HMAC in
reset. Without them the error leg would be satisfied by an address that always
errors.
====

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,6",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |What it proves
|CHK-KM-ISO-PRECOND
|Every leg began with both AXI error bits clear, so the bit a leg reports belongs to that leg's store.
|CHK-KM-PATH-LIVE
|With HMAC released, the KM store to the HMAC key aperture completes with no AXI error — the path is open.
|CHK-KM-ISO-TERM
|With `km_hmac` isolated, the same store raises AXI_SLVERR or AXI_DECERR. It neither completes nor hangs.
|CHK-KM-ISO-REOPEN
|After release the same store is clean again, so isolation did not leave the KM path wedged.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the other five KM destination paths (`km_otbn`, `km_aes`,
`km_kmac`, `km_abr`, `km_efuse`). They share the mechanism; only `km_hmac` is
demonstrated. The response code is graded as "an AXI error", not pinned to
SLVERR specifically, because the KM CSR status bit the image polls reports the
two codes as one field.

'''''

[[sep_km_key_policy_vault_test]]
===== `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_vault.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — slot, SRAM lock region and the seal/free slot pair from the run seed; extent endpoints 0 and 63 and the lock/W1C sequence are directed.

*Objective*: The key/policy vault refuses what it should: a store to a locked SRAM region is dropped with the readback unchanged, the matching `SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` bit sets, `IRQ_STATUS` raises AXI_SLVERR or AXI_DECERR, and the status bit clears W1C. The seal walk is here too: the same erase must retire a sealed slot and free an unsealed one.

*Test Coverage*: Vault lock, seal, retire, erase, and SRAM write-lock on the KM CPU bus.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Dedicated `km_rom_vault` on the KM CPU bus, not `rom_main`.
. The seal walk lives here rather than on the command set because no mailbox command seals a slot: over that surface an erase always frees, so the retire path has no vehicle there.
. PicoRV32 is RV32E (`x0`–`x15` only).
. A legal slot in 0..63 takes `CTRL.lock_write`; a store past `KM_KPV_SIZE` (`0x13108`, still inside the 8 KB KPV window) raises AXI SLVERR or DECERR; a write to a write-locked SRAM region (never region 0 or 15) is dropped and raises `SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` plus `IRQ_STATUS.SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_ERR`, then both W1C-clear to 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SLOT
|`CTRL.lock_write` sticks on slots 0, the seed slot, and 63.
|CHK-LOCKWR
|A key-data write to a write-locked slot raises AXI SLVERR. This is the LOCK path, not the decode path CHK-EXTENT grades: a legal key-entry address on a locked slot. The sticky error bits are cleared and confirmed clean first, so an earlier fault cannot satisfy it.
|CHK-LOCKUSE
|A key-data read of a `lock_use` slot raises SLVERR and returns zero data. Both halves are required: the refusal alone would not show the data path is also forced.
|CHK-EXTENT
|Store to `0x13108` sets `IRQ_STATUS` AXI_SLVERR or AXI_DECERR.
|CHK-DROP
|Write to the locked SRAM region is dropped (readback unchanged).
|CHK-VIOL
|`SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` reads EXACTLY the locked region's bit. Compared whole rather than masked, so a decoder that set every region's bit fails.
|CHK-IRQ
|`IRQ_STATUS.SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_ERR` is set and no ROM/SRAM parity or ROM-write error is raised alongside it.
|CHK-W1C
|Violation and IRQ bits read back 0 after W1C.
|CHK-SEAL
|One `CTRL` write on the seed's seal slot sets `seal` and raises `lock_write` with it.
|CHK-RETIRE
|Erasing that sealed slot leaves it retired: `lock_write` held and `lock_use` gained.
|CHK-RETSTICK
|A SECOND erase of the retired slot leaves it retired. Retirement is stated to hold until warm reset, so it is graded as a sticky property rather than a one-shot transition.
|CHK-FREE
|An unsealed slot is marked with `lock_write`, and the erase clears `CTRL` entirely, so the slot is reusable. The mark matters: the reset value is already clear, so without it a no-op erase would pass. The contrast is the point: the same erase, one sealed and one not.
|CHK-ERASEDATA
|The erase overwrites the stored key words, not just the CTRL bookkeeping. Two words are preloaded before the slot is marked; after the erase both differ from the preload AND from each other, so a fill that never ran or that is stuck at one value fails.
|CHK-IRQSET
|`IRQ_SET.DRBG_ERR_SET` raises `IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR`, and W1C clears it. This grades the software-inject path and the status plumbing ONLY -- it is not coverage of the DRBG fault source, and must not be recorded as such.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The ROM echoes an eight-bit fold of the WHOLE config word, so all four seeded operands are proven received. Echoing only the slot would let a ROM that ignored the region or either seal-walk slot produce an identical result word.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Aperture*: the vault is on the KM CPU bus, so the ROM evaluates these
contracts and the host reads one summary word from KM SRAM. That indirection is
real and is stated here rather than left implied: the host observes a result
word, not the individual registers. What keeps it from being a firmware
self-report is the seeded configuration -- the slot, region and slot pair are
chosen by the host, and the result word carries a fold of the whole config word
the ROM received, so a firmware that reported success unconditionally would
still have to have consumed every one of the host's operands.

'''''

==== Key Manager vault vehicles

[[sep_km_command_set_rand_test]]
===== `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: the KM mailbox command set is this vehicle's surface — generate,
transfer, revoke-or-shred, illegal-to-error, version queries, OTP cold lock,
recoverable-fault ACK, and a directed ABR seed transfer. The sideload KATs prove
only that a consumer can consume a key; none of them exercises the command
interface that produces, moves and destroys one.

*Test Coverage*: Generate, transfer, revoke-or-shred, version, OTP lock, recoverable ACK, ABR dest, and illegal-to-error on the KM mailbox.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image and sense it for real: the KM firmware reads OTP
at boot. Park OTBN, KMAC and HMAC, leave AES released as the consume witness, so
a transferred key can only land where the test says it lands.
. Bring entropy up strict, then release the KM and wait for `RESP_KM_READY`.
. Query `CMD_HW_VER`, `CMD_ROM_VER` and `CMD_SRAM_VER`.
. Generate a key for AES twice; check the handle and the echoed argument fields.
. Load a known key with `DEST_VALID = AES | xfer_dest`, transfer it to AES, and
encrypt: the ciphertext is compared against an independent AES-256-ECB golden.
. Transfer the same handle to the seed-selected dest (`xfer_dest`) in a
dedicated mailbox beat. When that dest is not AES, release that engine for
the key-bus write (OTBN waits idle after release), then park it again.
Consume of HMAC / KMAC / OTBN stays on the sideload KATs.
. Load an 8-word palindromic seed and transfer it to dest ABR ML-DSA seed
(`0x10`). Every seed walks this cell. Consume of that seed is
`sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`.
. Transfer the same handle to an engine outside its permitted set.
. Shred the AES key, then load and transfer a second, distinct known key and
encrypt again.
. Revoke the generated handle, then transfer on it.
. Revoke the reserved null handle; walk seeded undefined command IDs; send a
defined command with the wrong payload length. Probe with `CMD_STAT` after each
refusal.
. Lock one identity OTP bit with `CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD`; refuse a reserved bit
and a zero-length payload.
. Pulse SEP mailbox `CTRL.FLUSH`, receive `RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT`, ACK it, and
prove `CMD_STAT` clears the latched bit. The following shred is the key command
that must be accepted after the ACK.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. Generate / transfer / revoke-or-shred /
illegal-to-error walk every seed. The generated key size, the extra transfer
dest (one of HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN), the out-of-policy destination (outside
`AES | xfer_dest`), and the undefined command IDs come from the run seed. The
undefined IDs always include the five IDs immediately outside the defined
runs. One seed writes one classic dest cell plus the directed ABR cell; the
card does not claim all four classic dests in one invocation.

*Scope*: sideload KATs stay consume proofs; this vehicle owns the mailbox command
set. The mailbox PROTOCOL is its own entry: see
`sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`. The seal
walk is on `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`, not here: no mailbox command seals a
slot, so over this surface an erase always frees.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`.
|CHK-HW-VER
|`CMD_HW_VER` returns success and `RETURN_ARG` equals the KMCSR VERSION word 1.0.0.
|CHK-ROM-VER
|`CMD_ROM_VER` returns success and the packed ROM 1.1.0 word.
|CHK-SRAM-VER
|`CMD_SRAM_VER` returns the failure code: this image is ROM-only.
|CHK-GEN
|`CMD_KEY_GENERATE` returns success and a non-null handle, and its return argument echoes the requested size and destination mask.
|CHK-SAMPLER
|`CMD_KEY_GENERATE` raises the observed EDN->KM beat count on the DRBG scoreboard `CHK5_km` stream. The KMCSR sampler counters are not SEP-reachable.
|CHK-UNIQ
|A second generate returns a different handle.
|CHK-XFER
|`CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of a known loaded key succeeds and the AES ciphertext equals the independent golden.
|CHK-XFER-DEST
|A dedicated `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of that same handle to the seed-selected dest returns success and echoes the dest. Consume of HMAC / KMAC / OTBN is not claimed here.
|CHK-ABR-DEST
|A directed `CMD_KEY_LOAD` + `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of an 8-word palindromic seed to dest ABR ML-DSA seed returns success and echoes that dest. Every seed walks this cell. Consume is not claimed here.
|CHK-DEST
|A transfer to an engine outside the key's permitted set is refused with the failure code, not an argument-validation code. The refusal is attributable: the target engine is released from reset and waited to idle for that beat, so being parked is not the cause, and an unconditional control beat transfers the same handle to a destination inside its permitted set and requires success, so a handle that no longer exists -- which carries the same failure code -- is not the cause either.
|CHK-SHRED
|`CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` succeeds with its destination echoed, and the engine re-keys: a second distinct key transferred afterwards encrypts to its own golden and no longer to the first key's.
|CHK-REVOKE
|`CMD_KEY_REVOKE` succeeds with the handle echoed.
|CHK-CLOSED
|A transfer on the revoked handle is refused with the failure code: revoke fails closed.
|CHK-NULL
|Revoking the reserved null handle is refused with the invalid-argument code.
|CHK-ILLEGAL
|Every seeded undefined command ID returns the invalid-command code.
|CHK-LEN
|A defined command carrying the wrong payload length returns the invalid-length code.
|CHK-FRAME
|The three framing rejections, each with the value it carries: a corrupt header CRC-8 returns RC_HEADER_CRC with the CRC the KM computed, a wrong sequence number returns RC_CMD_NOSEQ with the number it expected, and a corrupt payload CRC-32C returns RC_PAYLOAD_CRC. The sequence-counter side effects differ between the three and are handled in the driver.
|CHK-OTP
|`CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD` of one identity bit returns success with that bit set in the echo; a reserved bit returns the invalid-argument code; a zero-length payload returns the invalid-length code.
|CHK-RECOV
|SEP `CTRL.FLUSH` posts `RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT` with the flushed-by-SEP code; `CMD_STAT` reports the latched bit; aggregator bit 31 is 1; `CMD_RECOV_ACK` clears the STAT bit and the aggregator bit. The following shred is the key command accepted after the ACK.
|CHK-GONE
|A shredded engine refuses to start. After a final `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` the AES produces no output within a bounded window, which is the only frontdoor evidence the shred reached the key: the engine key registers are write-only. Runs last, because an engine parked waiting for a key stays parked.
|CHK-ALIVE
|After every refusal `CMD_STAT` succeeds and reports no latched recoverable error. The second half is what gives CHK-DEST and CHK-CLOSED their meaning: while a recoverable fault is pending the KM refuses every key command with the same failure code those two expect, before it looks at the arguments at all, so a faulted KM would otherwise satisfy both without the policy they name being exercised.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: every frame is built with a correct header CRC-8, a correct
declared length and a correct payload CRC-32C, so a refusal is a verdict on the
command ID or its arguments and never on framing. The refusal checkers assert
the exact documented code rather than "not success", so a rejection for the
wrong reason does not read as a pass. The two known keys are checked to encrypt
the plaintext differently before either is used, so CHK-SHRED's
before-and-after comparison cannot be satisfied by two identical goldens.

*Not claimed*: the DRBG fault SOURCE. The organic timeout and AXI-Stream error
conditions cannot be provoked from the SEP fabric, and at SEP level such a fault
is unrecoverable -- the KM emits an unsolicited fault response -- so it
is neither a readable status nor a return code from here. The interrupt plumbing
for that same bit IS reachable and is graded on the vault vehicle's CHK-IRQSET,
via the architected `IRQ_SET.DRBG_ERR_SET` inject; the two must not be conflated,
and CHK-IRQSET is not coverage of the source.

The mailbox is a transport here, not the subject. Its register surface -- depth,
full, the four overflow/underflow stickies, `IRQ_STATUS`, and `SEP_CTRL`'s
response modes and flush -- is owned by `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`.

'''''

[[sep_km_wipe_reset_test]]
===== `sep_km_wipe_reset_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Phase*: 1

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_wipe_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: recover the KM after a `SW_RESET_N` warm pulse, then close it
with the SEP emergency wipe.

*Test Coverage*: KM warm-reset + `RESP_KM_READY`; `KM_WIPE_CTRL` unrecoverable fault.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`, and load a known key.
. Park then release the KM `SW_RESET_N` bit. Resynchronize mailbox sequence
counters. Wait for `RESP_KM_READY` and load the same known key again.
. Write `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state`. Receive `RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT`.

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE.

*Scope*: KPV-zero after wipe is not claimed -- those arrays have no SEP
frontdoor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`.
|CHK-PRE
|`CMD_KEY_LOAD` succeeds before the warm pulse.
|CHK-RESET
|After park/release the KM announces `RESP_KM_READY` and `CMD_KEY_LOAD` succeeds.
|CHK-WIPE
|A write of `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` posts `RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT` and aggregator bit 30 (PIC source 31) is 1.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the post-reset load is a value-bearing command, not only the
ready banner. Wipe is last: after the fault frame the test does not issue
another mailbox command.

'''''

[[sep_km_handover_test]]
===== `sep_km_handover_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Phase*: 1

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_handover_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: the handover mailbox contract, a SRAM load, and `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` after warm reset.

*Test Coverage*: `CMD_EXEC_ROM` inhibit; `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` 14-word stream; `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` after warm reset.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`.
. Refuse empty `CMD_SRAM_EXEC`, `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with `FW_WORDS=0`, and
`CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with reserved bits set.
. `CMD_EXEC_ROM` succeeds once; a later `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC`, `CMD_SRAM_EXEC`
and `CMD_EXEC_ROM` each return the failure code. A warm reset clears the
inhibit.
. On a fresh epoch, send `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with `FW_WORDS=14`, receive
success, then stream the 14-word blob plus CRC-32C.
. Warm-reset the KM. `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` returns success.

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE.

*Scope*: KMCSR `TEST_SIGNATURE` is not SEP-reachable.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`.
|CHK-EMPTY
|`CMD_SRAM_EXEC` with no image returns the invalid-argument code.
|CHK-ARG
|`CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with `FW_WORDS=0` or reserved `[31:16]` set returns the invalid-argument code.
|CHK-INHIBIT
|The first `CMD_EXEC_ROM` succeeds; later handover commands return the failure code.
|CHK-INHIBIT-RST
|A warm reset clears the inhibit; `CMD_EXEC_ROM` succeeds again.
|CHK-LOAD
|`CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` returns success before the stream; the 14-word image plus CRC-32C is posted.
|CHK-EXEC
|After a warm reset `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` returns success.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: inhibit cells assert the failure code, not "not success". The
load response is taken before the stream, matching the ROM handler. CHK-EMPTY
refuses `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` with no image, so CHK-EXEC success is against that
refusal, not an always-success command.

'''''

[[sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test]]
===== `sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Phase*: 1

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: a KM-sideloaded ABR ML-DSA seed produces the same public key as
a direct software-seed KEYGEN.

*Test Coverage*: KM dest ABR ML-DSA seed consume through the KV seed-read control.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`.
. Run a direct-seed ML-DSA-87 KEYGEN on the palindromic seed with
`cfg.entropy` and capture the public key.
. `ZEROIZE`. Repeat KEYGEN with the inverted entropy vector. The two public
keys must match -- `ABR_ENTROPY` is SCA masking and must not change the
outputs.
. `ZEROIZE`, then `CMD_KEY_LOAD` + `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` dest ABR ML-DSA seed.
. Write the same masking entropy, set the KV seed-read enable, KEYGEN, and
compare the public key word-for-word.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. `SepAbrKeygenCfg` is the single source of truth for
masking entropy on both compare KEYGENs. The seed words are directed so the
two KEYGENs stay comparable.

*Scope*: ML-DSA only. The ML-KEM dest bits and the `CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER`
consume are claimed by `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|A KEYGEN that writes `cfg.entropy` and a KEYGEN that writes the inverted vector produce the same public key. Both compare KEYGENs write `cfg.entropy`.
|CHK-DIRECT
|The direct-seed KEYGEN produces a non-zero public key.
|CHK-XFER
|`CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` dest ABR ML-DSA seed returns success and echoes the dest.
|CHK-PK
|The KM-sideloaded KEYGEN public key equals the direct-seed public key.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: both compare public keys must be non-zero. The inverted
vector is not equal to `cfg.entropy`. The palindrome absorbs the KV dword
reversal, so a CHK-PK mismatch is a sideload failure, not an ordering
artifact. `ABR_ENTROPY` is write-only; this row grades the RDL "no change on
the outputs" rule, not a consume probe.
'''''

[[sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test]]
===== `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Phase*: 1

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: every ML-KEM lane of the Key-Vault facade carries what the KM
pushed into the sideload CSR, and the shared key Adams Bridge posts back over
the write lane is consumable by the KM.

*Test Coverage*: the 16-dword seed read lane carrying D then Z, the message
read lane, and the shared-key write lane with its `KEY_VALID` latch and its
interrupt, plus the `CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` refusal when no key has been posted
and the vault-confidentiality rule that withholds the shared key from software.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`. Read `MLKEM_NAME` and require the KEM-1024 identity, so the
window compares below cannot be zero against zero.
. KEYGEN on the ALT `(D, Z)` pair written directly to the registers and keep
the encapsulation key. `ZEROIZE`, then KEYGEN on the REF pair and require a
different encapsulation key. The registers are left holding REF.
. `ZEROIZE`. `CMD_KEY_LOAD` + `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` the ALT `D` to dest
`abr_mlkem_seed_d` and the ALT `Z` to dest `abr_mlkem_seed_z`, set the KV
seed-read enable, and KEYGEN with no register write. The encapsulation key
must equal the ALT one and differ from the REF one.
. ENCAPS twice over that key, once on each message, written directly, and
require the two ciphertexts to differ. The first of the two also establishes
that the shared key is readable when nothing came from the vault. The register
is left holding REF.
. `ZEROIZE`, sideload the ALT message to dest `abr_mlkem_msg`, set the KV
message-read enable, and ENCAPS. The ciphertext must equal the ALT one, and
the shared key of that same ENCAPS must now read zero.
. With nothing yet posted on the write lane, `CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` must be
refused. Then set the KV shared-key write enable and ENCAPS again. The KM must
post the unsolicited `RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY` announcement, and the same
command must then succeed with a non-zero KPV handle.

*Randomization*: none. Every seed, message and command is directed: each leg
is a value-equality compare between a sideloaded and a direct run, and a
random input on either side would remove the compare.

*Scope*: the KV `read_entry` / `write_entry` and `dest_valid` fields are not
claimed -- SEP has no Caliptra Key Vault, so the facade serves one block per
lane and ignores entry selection. The read-error response a block raises while
its `KEY_VALID` is clear is not claimed here.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`.
|CHK-KEM-NAME
|The ML-KEM aperture returns the KEM-1024 identity words, so every window compare below reads a live decode.
|CHK-KEM-SEED-REF
|The ALT and REF `(D, Z)` pairs produce different encapsulation keys. Without this the sideload compare could not distinguish a delivered seed from a stale one.
|CHK-KEM-SEED-SIDELOAD
|A KEYGEN whose seed arrives only over the KV seed lane produces the encapsulation key of the direct-register KEYGEN on the same words, and not the key of the pair the registers last held. This is the only check that the lane's two blocks land in the right halves -- D on the low eight dwords and Z on the high eight.
|CHK-KEM-MSG-SIDELOAD
|An ENCAPS whose message arrives only over the KV message lane produces the ciphertext of the direct-register ENCAPS on the same message, and not that of the message the register last held. The compare is on the ciphertext, not the shared key: the ciphertext is public and a deterministic function of the encapsulation key and the message, while the shared key is not readable on exactly this path -- see the next row.
|CHK-KEM-SK-CONFIDENTIAL
|The shared key reads back over the bus after a fully register-driven ENCAPS, and reads zero once the message came from the vault. The ML-KEM shared key is consumed directly by the Key Manager and shared-key storage is secret-bearing Class 2 logic (`doc/crypto.adoc`, `doc/attack_countermeasures.adoc`), so a key the vault sourced must not be readable by software over the CSR aperture. The register-driven read is the positive control: without it a broken readback path would pass the zero check, and it is what makes this a conditional withholding rather than a readback that never worked.
|CHK-KEM-SK-NOTIFY
|The writeback raises the shared-key interrupt and the KM firmware posts the unsolicited `RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY` frame. The facade pulses `IRQ_STATUS.key_valid` on the final writeback dword and the wrapper gates it onto the shared-key interrupt, so this frame is the only host-side view of that path -- the consume below could succeed with the interrupt dead.
|CHK-KEM-SK-WRITEBACK
|`CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` is refused while the sideload block's `KEY_VALID` is clear, and succeeds with a non-zero handle after an ENCAPS with the write lane armed. The refusal is the negative control: without it the success would not show that the writeback set `KEY_VALID`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the identity gate precedes every compare. Every captured
ciphertext is required to be non-zero, and the withheld-shared-key check is
preceded by a readable one on the register-driven path. Each sideload leg is
preceded by a run on different words, so the register the lane bypasses holds
the wrong value when the lane is read -- a lane that delivers nothing fails on
the inequality before the equality is reached. Every captured encapsulation and
shared key is required to be non-zero. Seeds and messages are palindromes, so a
mismatch is a delivery failure and not the KM-versus-register dword ordering; D
and Z are *different* palindromes, so a swap between them still fails. The
shared-key consume carries its own refusal leg rather than resting on a single
success.

'''''

[[sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test]]
===== `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, KM held in `SW_RESET_N` (reset default).
No `+km_rom_hex`: this leaf grades the SEP-side registers, not a command stream.

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: the SEP-side KM mailbox as a REGISTER surface rather than a
transport. `STATUS` depth and full/empty, the two SEP-reachable sticky error
bits, `IRQ_STATUS` / `IRQ_ENABLE` gating onto aggregator `[14]`, both
`SEP_CTRL` response modes, and flush.

*Test Coverage*: SEP-side KM mailbox registers: depth, stickies, IRQ, response modes, flush.

*Why it is its own entry*: a transport only ever touches what a well-formed
exchange needs. `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` reaches 4 of the 7 SEP-side
registers and 2 of the 12 `STATUS` bits. Reaching the rest means reading an
empty FIFO and overrunning a full one, and both corrupt a frame in flight.

*Why KM is held*: the SEP-facing mailbox lives on cold reset, so the FIFOs and
SEP registers stay alive while the KM CPU cannot consume inbound words or push
outbound ones. Fill, overflow and underflow are then deterministic, and
firmware cannot flush or fault the mailbox under the host. A `SEP_CTRL` write
after a deliberate underflow is a register-block access, independent of the
FIFO error path, and must stay OKAY. The write is a 32-bit beat: a 64-bit
beat at `SEP_CTRL` (offset 0x18) spans past the 0x1C mailbox window and the
fabric refuses it. Negative FIFO accesses are marked `expect_error` so the
environment scoreboard does not fail them as real errors.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. The 16 inbound fill words and the overflow poke come
from the run seed. Every discrete cell (idle, underflow SLVERR, underflow
OKAY-mode, fill, overflow SLVERR, overflow OKAY-mode, IRQ enable/gate/W1C,
flush) walks in one invocation.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up no_cpu with fuse sense skipped. Require `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 clear.
. Compare reset `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS` against the empty golden.
. Read empty `SEP_READ_DATA`; require SLVERR, data 0, and `outbound_underflow`
  latched in both `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS`.
. Write `SEP_CTRL` immediately after that underflow; require OKAY and readback.
. Set `outbound_underflow_resp`; the same empty read must be OKAY with the
  stickies still set. Write-1-to-clear both copies.
. Write 16 seeded words; after each write, `STATUS` depth/full/empty and
  `IRQ_STATUS.inbound_write_space_avail` must match the golden.
. A 17th write must SLVERR, latch `inbound_overflow`, and leave depth 16.
. Set `inbound_overflow_resp`; the next overrun must be OKAY, drop the word,
  and leave the sticky set.
. Enable the two error IRQs; aggregator `[14]` must rise. Clear the enable;
  the pin must drop while `IRQ_STATUS` stays. Write-1-to-clear both stickies.
. Set flush. Both FIFOs empty, `flush` self-clears, and the overflow sticky
  survives until write-1-to-clear.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HELD
|`SW_RESET_N` bit 0 reads 0. Without this the fill/overflow goldens are a race against firmware.
|CHK-IDLE
|Reset `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS` match the empty golden; aggregator `[14]` is 0.
|CHK-UFL
|Empty `SEP_READ_DATA` returns SLVERR and 0, and `outbound_underflow` latches in both `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS`.
|CHK-CTRL
|A 32-bit `SEP_CTRL` write immediately after that underflow is OKAY and reads back. The register is not the FIFO error path. A 64-bit beat at offset 0x18 spans past the 0x1C window and is refused.
|CHK-UFL-RESP
|`SEP_CTRL.outbound_underflow_resp` turns the same empty read into OKAY; the sticky bits stay set.
|CHK-UFL-W1C
|Write-1-to-clear drops both sticky copies.
|CHK-FILL
|16 seeded writes fill inbound. After each write, `STATUS` depth/full/empty and `IRQ_STATUS.inbound_write_space_avail` match the golden.
|CHK-OVR
|A 17th write returns SLVERR, latches `inbound_overflow`, and leaves depth 16.
|CHK-OVR-RESP
|`SEP_CTRL.inbound_overflow_resp` turns the next overrun into OKAY; the sticky bit stays; depth stays 16 (the word is dropped).
|CHK-IRQ
|Enabling the two error IRQs raises aggregator `[14]`. Clearing the enable drops the pin while `IRQ_STATUS` stays. Write-1-to-clear drops both.
|CHK-FLUSH
|`CTRL.FLUSH` empties both FIFOs, self-clears, and does not clear the sticky error bits.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: `inbound_underflow`, `outbound_overflow`, and
`flushed_by_km` need the KM CPU as the peer. `STATUS.inbound_separator`
updates only when the KM pops the inbound FIFO, and `STATUS.outbound_separator`
only when the SEP pops a KM-pushed word, so both stay on the KM IP mailbox
vehicles. `outbound_read_data_avail` stays 0 for the same reason.

'''''

[[sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test]]
===== `sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_crc.parhex`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: each mode of the KM CRC engine produces the value an independently
written host-side model produces. The engine has no register interface, so the
proof has to come from KM firmware issuing the PCPI custom-0 instruction.

*Test Coverage*: KM CRC PCPI modes vs an independent host golden.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the KM on a dedicated service ROM. The ROM applies one CRC instruction
per request — mode, chaining state, data operand — and returns the raw result.
It decides nothing.
. Walk the seeded operand set through all three modes and compare each result
against the host-side golden.
. Send one word through the word instruction and the same four bytes through
four chained byte instructions.
. Send one state and byte through both polynomial families.
. Repeat each byte-mode operand with garbage in the upper three bytes.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the vector set, from the run seed.

*Aperture*: the CRC engine has no register interface at all. It is a PicoRV32
PCPI co-processor reached by a custom-0 instruction, so the only honest
activation is KM firmware, in the ROM pattern the vault test established. The
assembler has no mnemonic for the instruction, so the image emits encoded words;
the KM core is built without the upper register file, so the encodings target
two registers in the lower half and cannot name one the core does not have.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WORD
|Every seeded CRC-32C word vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-BYTE
|Every seeded CRC-32C byte vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-ROHC
|Every seeded CRC-8/ROHC vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-XCHAIN
|One word instruction equals four chained byte instructions from the same start state. Hardware on both sides, so it holds independently of the golden.
|CHK-POLY
|The two polynomial families return different results for the same state and byte, compared over the low byte so their differing result widths cannot satisfy it. The operand is screened for this: the two agree in the low byte for roughly one input in 256, which is correct hardware, so it is excluded from the draw rather than tolerated by a looser check.
|CHK-NARROW
|CRC-8/ROHC leaves the upper twenty-four result bits clear.
|CHK-UPPER
|Both byte modes ignore the operand's upper twenty-four bits.
|CHK-MOVE
|No result equals its own input state.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the golden is anchored to published check values for both
algorithms and to a byte-order self-test, all asserted at import, so a
transcription error fails before a run rather than agreeing with an equally
wrong DUT. The operand draw rejects an all-zero state with an all-zero byte,
which every reflected CRC maps to zero and which therefore passes against a dead
engine; words whose four bytes are not all distinct, which cannot separate
little-endian consumption from big-endian; and operands sitting on a fixed point
of their mode, which the engine correctly maps back onto their own input state.
A fixed point is legal hardware behaviour, not a defect -- in CRC-8/ROHC one
byte in 256 is a fixed point for any given state -- so it is screened out of the
draw with the independent golden rather than tolerated by CHK-MOVE, which would
otherwise be unfalsifiable for that vector. CHK-MOVE then fails a co-processor
that hands back its first operand, and CHK-XCHAIN and CHK-POLY need no golden at
all. All three modes walk in this vehicle; do not split them into siblings.

*Not claimed*: the illegal-encoding trap. An unrecognised instruction in this
family produces no handshake and traps the core, which would end the service
loop; the KM firmware's own regression owns that leg.

'''''

[[sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test]]
===== `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`,
`+km_rom_hex=km_rom_kpv_scrambler.parhex`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: the key-policy vault scrambler transforms both the stored word and
the address mapping while enabled. Pass-through while disabled is the
observation aperture this test relies on rather than a contract it asserts --
it is what makes the raw stored words readable frontdoor, and a vault that did
not pass through would fail the round-trip and the count instead.

*Test Coverage*: Vault scrambler enable, key transform, and sticky lock.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the KM on a dedicated observation ROM and post two scrambler keys and
three logical key-entry indices.
. With the scrambler off, zero all 1024 key-entry words. That reaches a known
starting state through a legitimate frontdoor, without a wipe and without
relying on power-up values.
. Program the first key, enable the scrambler, write one plaintext at each of
the three logical indices, then disable and scan the whole file.
. Repeat with the second key at the first index only.
. Re-enable and read the first index back.
. Take the sticky lock last, then read the key register and the first index
again.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — two scrambler keys and three logical indices, drawn
distinct, from the run seed.

*Aperture*: the vault is reachable only from the KM side, so this needs its own
ROM image, not `rom_main` — that image programs the key, enables the scrambler
and takes the sticky lock during boot, so the enable toggle is already gone
before the host can act. No hierarchical peek is needed: with the scrambler
disabled the vault addresses its register file with the logical index and passes
data through unmodified, so firmware can write plaintext with scrambling on,
disable it, and read the raw stored words back through a legitimate frontdoor.
The lock is taken last because it is sticky. The ROM reports raw observations
and the host holds every verdict.

*Precondition*: `km_kpv_regfile` has no reset, so power-up is undefined and a
whole-vault "exactly N non-zero" scan is not a contract until the file is
known-zero. The zero fill is *setup*, not a wipe security checker.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-COUNT
|After three writes through the enabled scrambler, exactly three of the 1024 words differ from the zeroed setup.
|CHK-DATA
|No stored word equals any plaintext written.
|CHK-ADDR
|The stored words do not all sit at their own logical index: the address mapping is tweaked, not identity.
|CHK-KEYED
|The same plaintext at the same logical index under a different key stores a different word, so the transform is keyed rather than a fixed obfuscation. The reported index is range-checked and the value required non-zero first, so a second-key pass whose write never landed fails here instead of being read as evidence of keying.
|CHK-RT
|With the scrambler re-enabled, the logical index reads back the exact plaintext.
|CHK-KEYRD
|Before the lock, the key register reads back the key that was written. This is the control for CHK-LOCK: without it a write-only or permanently-zero register would satisfy the zero-after-lock check for free.
|CHK-LOCK
|After the sticky lock the same register reads zero.
|CHK-LOCKRT
|The round-trip still recovers the plaintext after the lock, so the hardware kept the key it was using.
|CHK-SWWEL
|The lock's DOCUMENTED contract: after it, a write of a different key and a write clearing ENABLE are both refused -- the round-trip still returns the plaintext and ENABLE still reads set. CHK-LOCK grades behaviour the specification does not state; this grades the behaviour it does.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: "exactly three" is the load-bearing quantifier, not "at least
one". A write that never landed gives zero differences and fails; a read path
stuck at zero gives zero and fails; a register file left dirty gives more than
three and fails. A bare "the stored word differs from the plaintext" check would
pass in the first two cases, and under a simulator that defaults uninitialised
memory to zero it would also pass on a file that was never written. Round-trip
is a required conjunct, so a scrambler that destroys data cannot pass by looking
scrambled. The plaintext is chosen with mixed nibbles because the substitution
is nibble-wise and a uniform value would weaken the difference signal.

CHK-ADDR is "not all of them" rather than "each of them". The
address transform is a bijection on the 1024 indices, and a bijection may have
fixed points, so a single index mapping to itself is legal; an identity mapping
across three seeded indices is not.

CHK-LOCK asserts RTL behaviour the architecture document does not state. The
document requires only that a locked key cannot be modified; the hardware
additionally drives the key register to zero once the lock commits, while the
scrambler keeps using a shadow copy. CHK-LOCKRT is what makes the pair
meaningful: the key is unreadable and still in use. Recorded here because a
checker that outruns its specification should say so rather than read as
contracted behaviour.

*Not claimed*: the eraser's use of the shared scrambler, the seal and retire
contracts owned by the vault test, and the wipe security contract.

'''''

=== Regression Groups

[cols=",",options="header"]
|===
|Group |Meaning
|`smoke` |Short no-CPU bring-up subset (CI)
|`cpu_stub` |CPU not alive (`sep_cpu` stub); AXI-driver tests
|`cpu` |Firmware-boot tests
|`rom_fw` |Boot-ROM image class (separate owner); not a member of `all`
|`all` |Every test this VPLAN grades, and the coverage set: `cpu_stub` + `cpu` plus `sep_periph_bus_err_misaligned_reveal_test`, which is in neither class because it fails by design
|===

`README.md` owns how to run these groups, including which one to use when closing a change.

[[known-limitations]]
[[phase-3]]
=== Known Limitations

This table holds two different kinds of item, and the distinction matters when
reading a row: a *hardware* gap or spec-vs-RTL divergence, where the RTL or the
specification is expected to change; and a *firmware* constraint, where the
hardware is correct as built and software must program it correctly. A firmware
constraint is not an open defect.

[cols="2,4",options="header"]
|===
|Topic |Contract

|Token-comparator collapse inject (`3'b001`)
|`tb_top` mode `3'b001` forces instance 0 to a collapsed pair and leaves instances 1 and 2 as a legal match. The fault output is the OR of collapse and disagree, so `CHK-COLLAPSE` does not prove the collapse detector exists. Isolating that term needs all three pairs alike and `compute_comparison_vld_i=1`. SEC_DISABLE's valid is the sticky digest valid, not the RMA `digest_vld && fuse_sense_done` term, so an all-alike force with SEC valid still 0 is expected not to raise that rail.

|Inbound CFG allow-list (firmware constraint)
|Hardware does not hard-block allow-listing the inbound-filter CFG window, outbound CFG, the remap banks, or `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` / `SEP_REGION_SIZE`, and is correct as built: the filter applies the rules it is given. Firmware must keep those CSRs outside every allow window — allow-listing them is a programming error, not a hardware defect, and nothing is expected to change in the RTL. The suite proves ownership under correct programming (`CHK-OWNERSHIP`).

|Routed-but-reserved spans (specification)
|`sep_axi_map_refuse_test` drives reserved addresses whose refuse flavour the map names. Named reserved spans whose refuse flavour is unnamed are excluded and are not asserted.

|Dead-space per-beat refusal on writes (AXI)
|Read bursts are proven per beat: the bus monitor keeps each beat's `RRESP` in order, so `CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT` can name the beat that answered. Write bursts cannot be, and no tooling will change it — AXI returns one `BRESP` for a whole write burst, so a write burst that refused only its tail is indistinguishable from one that refused every beat. The write contract stays at burst granularity: the burst is refused and no live register moves.

|Inbound global-to-local remap consume (outside this DUT)
|`fabric.adoc` names `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` and `SEP_REGION_SIZE` as the global SEP aperture "used by the SMU crossbar and by SEP inbound global-to-local remap", which reads as a datapath this suite could drive. It is not one here. Bare SEP exports both CSR values on output ports (`sep_global_base_addr_o`, `sep_region_size_o`, `sep.sv:203-204`) and nothing inside the DUT consumes them to translate an address; the remapper is in the SMU integration logic above this level. Measured rather than assumed: with the reset aperture programmed, an external read at the aperture-relative address of a scratch CSR returns DECERR while the same register answers at its own address, so the external master presents SEP-local addresses and no in-DUT remap stands between them. The CSR read/write path is graded by `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`; the consume belongs to whoever integrates SEP, and a leaf here could only re-prove the register. The sixteen programmable `axi_alias_remap` regions are a different mechanism and are graded by `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`.

|Entropy pool pointer fault, PIC source 39 (hardware not built)
|`sep_internal_interrupts[38]` is reserved for the entropy pool FIFO's pointer-integrity error. The check is not built in this configuration, so the source is tied to zero, can never assert, and no vehicle grades it. `interrupts.adoc` documents the same. A built check is level-sensitive with no write-one-to-clear, so firmware must mask the source or scrub the pool rather than acknowledge it.

|Dead-space refusal flavour (specification)
|`memory_map.adoc` says the fabric refuses an address past the extent a unit allocates, and names no response flavour for it. `spi_controller` refuses a dead-space write with SLVERR. The access is refused either way, which is the contract `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` asserts, so the flavour is reported as `DEADSPACE-FLAVOUR` for the design owner and is not failed. Which error responses are permitted is the open question.

|OSS hygiene exclusions
|No licensed/proprietary IP in the build. Bender targets stay `sep` / `sep_el2` / `sep_wrapper` only.

|Verilator covergroups
|SystemVerilog covergroups are not available under Verilator. Feature coverage is the iconic scorecard; VCS Phase 1 bins are in link:SEP_FCOV.adoc[SEP_FCOV]. RTL code coverage is a separate, unthresholded number (`README.md`, Code coverage).

|ROM-FW ownership
|Boot-ROM firmware contracts are out of this VPLAN (separate owner / `rom_fw` class).

|Observation probes
|The TB observation/inject ports are the closed set enumerated in link:SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc[SEP_TB_ARCH].
|Behavioural OTP array
|The array behind the fuse-command interface is a simulation-only behavioural bank, not an OTP macro. Two properties the eFuse programming entries appear to establish are properties of that model rather than of hardware: write-one-to-set additivity is OR-only by construction, so a bank that overwrote instead of OR-ing cannot be represented and no-clobber is not a failable contract; and persistence across cold reset comes from the model having no reset on its storage. What is proven against real logic is that each program reached its addressed bit through the real programming path, and that the sense FSM re-reads the array after reset with the shadow tracking it.

|JTAG MMR token-block access
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/periphs.adoc` allows the MMR token block and denies the shadow, the program/read interface, and the SHIM. No checker claims DECERR on the MMR token block. Inbound ownership under correct programming (CFG, outbound CFG, remap banks, and region CSRs outside every allow window) is owned by `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test` `CHK-OWNERSHIP`.
|RTL assertions are backend-dependent
|What participates depends on the backend. Under Verilator, `prim_assert.sv`, `abr_prim_assert.sv` and `ocah_assert.svh` all gate their macros on `VERILATOR`, so design and vendored-IP SVA expand to nothing, and the SEP Verilator targets pass no `--assert`, so the two-state `ocah_axi_sva` rules are dropped as well. Under VCS those macros are live. The shared native profile also defines `COMMON_CELLS_ASSERTS_OFF`, so the PULP `common_cells` inline assertions are off on every backend. Where a checker proves a property, that checker is the whole of the proof on Verilator; no assertion layer sits underneath it there.

|Entropy-pool interrupt numbering (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` numbers the two entropy-pool interrupts one lower than the canonical table in `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`. PIC source IDs are 1-based, so aggregation index 36/37 is PIC source 37/38; firmware following `crypto.adoc` would enable and mask the wrong two sources. No checker asserts this; do not XFAIL it.

|CPU reset-vector TDR (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` documents a CPU reset-vector TDR selected by a JTAG opcode. The vendored tap implements no such register — the vector reaches the CPU through a direct wrapper input. The testbench shifts that opcode on every CPU-boot run and the sequence is inert; it is not evidence of the TDR.

|Scratch bank register count (spec vs RTL)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` requires "two sets of 16 x 32-bit scratch register banks". `hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_scratch/sep_scratch.rdl` declares `SCRATCH[8]`, so each bank implements eight registers, not sixteen. The reset-domain behaviour of the banks that exist is proven; the count is not, and no test asserts it. This row is the record of the difference, not a claimed contract. Do not XFAIL.

|AP/STEE output-remap attributes and pass-through (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` gives the AP/STEE remappers a cacheable attribute and says unprogrammed regions are transparent pass-throughs. `hw/ip/output_remap/rtl/output_remap.sv` has neither a valid bit nor a cacheable bit and rewrites the address unconditionally, so an unprogrammed region zeroes the upper address bits instead of passing through. The vehicle on this bank is `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`, which proves a programmed window translates and that a beat outside it is refused; the pass-through behaviour of an unprogrammed region has no checker, so this row is the record of the gap and not a claimed contract. Do not XFAIL.

This row is about the AP/STEE bank only. The local-master aliaser is a different block: `hw/ip/axi_alias_remap/rtl/axi_alias_remap.sv` carries `region_valid` per region, drives the outbound cache bits from the region on a hit, and passes an address through unchanged when no region hits. `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test` covers the enable with `CHK-VALID-GATE`.

|Class 2 scan-root count (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/attack_countermeasures.adoc` states "exactly 10" Class 2 scan roots. The RTL instantiates an eleventh on the entropy-pool EDN leg that is absent from the list, and nothing lints the manifest against the RTL. That manifest is the sole input deciding what is never scan-dumpable.

|`sec_dis` is not forced
|No entry forces `sec_dis`. The LC→`feat_ctrl` stitch stays an end-to-end path; a forced override is not an acceptable substitute for it.

|Checkers sharing one pass line
|Three firmware tests evaluate several conditions and print a single pass line, so their rows do not fail independently of each other: `sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test` (3 of 3 checkers), `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test` (5 of 5), `sep_mailbox_plic_test` (4 of 4). Firmware counters still gate the verdict.

|Lifecycle codes with no owner
|Closed for the encodings, narrowed for the mechanism. `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` walks four of the seven named codes on the OTP-program path (`LC_TEST_DEV`, `LC_PROD`, `LC_RMA_SIP_1`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_1`); the `LC_STATE` write-1-to-set leaves add `LC_RMA_SIP_0`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_0` and `LC_PROD_END` as sensed starting states with a `FEAT_CTRL` claim on each, plus two codes outside the named set. What remains unowned is the *pairing*: `LC_RMA_SIP_0`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_0` and `LC_PROD_END` are sensed but never reached by programming a fuse bit, because the OTP image is a t=0 deposit and a fuse survives reset — from a given image only a W1S superset is reachable in one leaf.

|Contracts not claimed (no vehicle in `all`)
|Phase 3: entropy-pool drain-under-fill; isolate drain-before-reset on the AES and OTBN domains (HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge and the shared TRNG drain are proven); locked-field CPU PIC/ISR; ESRC health-test quality and `prim_count` redundancy fault inject; inbound-filter negative product; non-identity inbound global remap; eleven-output `dbg_disable` gate test; named negative matrices (`sep_cpu_lsu_*_matrix_test`, `sep_fabric_gap_matrix_test`, `sep_irq_extended_connectivity_test`, `sep_efuse_uvm_permission_matrix_test`); KMAC sideload fail-closed + recovery (`ErrKeyNotValid` / `CMD.err_processed`).

|===
